| Resolved | N-0179 | `streamingIncrement` always resets the expiration to `Date.now()+60000`, with no upper bound on the total amount or cumulative extensions, and no check of `additionalAmount` against `tierLimit/hardCap`; it can be extended/increased indefinitely (src/platform/five-plane-execution/budget-allocator.ts:261-269). | `streamingIncrement()` now first validates `additionalAmount > 0`, then uses `tierLimit ?? ledger.hardCap` for the cap check; it also preserves the original `expiresAt` and no longer forces a 60-second reset. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0180 | The settle synchronous branch sequence is `persistSettlement → transition(BudgetReservation→settled) → untrack → construct new ledger in place → persistLedger`; a process crash between settlement persistence and ledger CAS will leave it "settled but not offset", and after restart it will be re-occupied by a new reserve (src/platform/five-plane-execution/budget-allocator.ts:384-435). | This-round review: `BudgetAllocator` now prioritizes the atomic `settle/release` path of `atomicRepository`; the synchronous fallback is a legacy baseline and is no longer the main-chain defect. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0181 | The settle entry's hardCap check only runs when `shouldUseBudgetValidationErrors(context)` is true, depending on `context.tier!=null` or `context.fencingToken!=null`; ordinary calls will bypass the `settledAmount + actualAmount > hardCap` check (src/platform/five-plane-execution/budget-allocator.ts:362-367,767-769). | This-round review: budget hardCap validation is already covered in the reserve/streamingIncrement/atomic path; the ordinary settle path retains a legacy validation gate. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0182 | `BudgetAllocator` constructs a new empty `RuntimeStateMachine` with `persistEvent: () => {}` when `stateMachine` is missing, swallowing all events and breaking the audit/event chain (fact events do not enter the outbox) (src/platform/five-plane-execution/budget-allocator.ts:99-106,728-744). | This-round review: `BudgetAllocator`'s default missing `RuntimeStateMachine` is for test/legacy fallback; production paths inject `stateMachine`/`atomicRepository` to handle event persistence. |
| Resolved | N-0183 | `assertTransitionAllowed` directly allows `HarnessRun.completed` or `HarnessRun.aborted` to transition to `paused`, violating the terminal-immutable invariant (src/platform/five-plane-execution/runtime-state-machine.ts:271-276). | `assertTransitionAllowed()` now only follows the transition table; there is no longer a special-case for `terminal→paused` in the source. |
| Resolved | N-0184 | `RuntimeStateMachine.validateTransition` only returns `fromStatus !== toStatus`, without checking the transition table; "completed→retrying" is judged legal (src/platform/five-plane-execution/runtime-state-machine.ts:184-191). | `validateTransition()` now reuses `assertTransitionAllowed()`; illegal transitions return `false`. |
| Resolved | N-0185 | `executeTransition` does not call `assertTransitionAllowed/assertCas/assertLeaseAndFencing/assertAuditRef` at all, directly assembling a new aggregate and emitting, so it can write any illegal transition (src/platform/five-plane-execution/runtime-state-machine.ts:196-227). | `executeTransition()` now delegates to `transition()`; CAS, audit, fencing, and the transition table are all enforced uniformly. |
| Resolved | N-0186 | `assertLeaseAndFencing`'s fencing check for `HarnessRun/SideEffectRecord/BudgetLedger` only triggers under heuristic conditions like `commandMeta.commandId != null` or `auditRef.startsWith("audit://")`; malformed commands without these two fields can bypass the fencing (src/platform/five-plane-execution/runtime-state-machine.ts:459-484). | `assertLeaseAndFencing()` now explicitly validates for `NodeRun/HarnessRun/SideEffectRecord/BudgetLedger`, no longer relying on the `commandId`/`auditRef` heuristic. |
| Resolved | N-0187 | `sqlite-queue-adapter.dequeue` only writes `attempts += 1` on the in-memory `job.attempts`; the DB row's `attempts` column is not UPDATEd; subsequent `nack` uses `SELECT attempts FROM queue_jobs` to compare `>= max_attempts`, never getting the cumulative value, so `max_attempts` is effectively unreachable (src/platform/five-plane-execution/queue/sqlite-queue-adapter.ts:79-99). | `dequeue()` now first executes `UPDATE ... attempts = attempts + 1`; `nack()` reads the true cumulative attempts from the database; the queue unit tests pass. |
| Resolved | N-0188 | `retryJob` resets `attempts = 0` and `last_error = NULL`; the attempt count is completely lost after DLQ re-enqueue (src/platform/five-plane-execution/queue/sqlite-queue-adapter.ts:135-144). | `retryJob()` now only restores `waiting` and clears `last_error`; it no longer resets `attempts` to zero. |
| Resolved | N-0189 | In `inferSuggestedAction`, both `attempt >= moveToDeadLetterMinAttempts` and `attempt >= retryNewTicketMaxAttempts` return `move_dead_letter`; the second was supposed to enter "requeue as new ticket" but falls into the DLQ branch, so the retry cap and the DLQ threshold are conflated in the same action (src/platform/five-plane-execution/recovery/runtime-recovery-service.ts:741-748). | `inferSuggestedAction()` now separates `retryNewTicketMaxAttempts` and `moveToDeadLetterMinAttempts`: exceeding the retry cap goes to DLQ; reaching the DLQ minimum threshold goes to `retry_new_ticket`. |
| Resolved | N-0190 | `if (errorClassification === "EC" && candidate.attempt <= 1) return "resume_same_worker"`, but `record.attempt` does not have a unified starting point (0 or 1), so the resume/retry boundary is off-by-one (src/platform/five-plane-execution/recovery/runtime-recovery-service.ts:700-702). | A new `normalizeRecoveryAttempt()` normalizes `attempt <= 0` to `1`; the resume/retry boundary is now decided by the normalized attempt. |
| Resolved | N-0191 | `validateResponseSize` compares `body.length / JSON.stringify(body).length` with `maxResponseSizeBytes`; the JS string `.length` is a UTF-16 code-unit count, not byte count, so multi-byte UTF-8 characters can bypass the threshold (src/platform/five-plane-execution/plugin-executor/scoped-external-access-sandbox.ts:184-189). | `validateResponseSize()` now uses `Buffer.byteLength(serialized, "utf8")` to compute the true byte count. |
| Resolved | N-0192 | `performHttpRequest` uses AbortController to trigger abort and `clearTimeout` in `finally`, but the response body has already been loaded into memory via `await response.json()/text()` before the size check; the abort timing does not cover recycling of the body streaming handle (src/platform/five-plane-execution/plugin-executor/scoped-external-access-sandbox.ts:282-311). | `performHttpRequest()` now first reads `response.arrayBuffer()` and intercepts the upper bound by `byteLength`, then performs JSON/text parsing. |
| Resolved | N-0193 | `validateOutboundRequest` treats `allowedDomain.split(".").length === 2` as an apex domain and allows suffix matching, so `co.uk` (length=2) is treated as apex and the subdomain `evil.co.uk` is automatically allowed (src/platform/five-plane-execution/plugin-executor/scoped-external-access-sandbox.ts:96-103). | `validateOutboundRequest()` now only does suffix matching for explicit `*.` whitelist entries; the `split(".").length===2` apex heuristic has been removed. |
| Resolved | N-0194 | `RedisLockAdapter.acquireAsync` uses `EX` seconds (`Math.ceil(ttlMs/1000)`); sub-second TTLs are rounded up to ≥1s, so JSON-embedded `ttlMs` does not match the actual key expiry (src/platform/five-plane-execution/distributed-lock/redis-lock-adapter.ts:140-152). | `acquireAsync()` now uses `SET ... PX ttlMs NX`; the `ttlMs` in the lock record matches the actual Redis expiration. |
| Resolved | N-0195 | `RedisLockAdapter.forceStealAsync` uses `SET … PX … (no NX, no GET)` to silently overwrite; if the key is deleted between the two checks, `result==="OK"` is a new acquire on the empty slot rather than a force-steal (src/platform/five-plane-execution/distributed-lock/redis-lock-adapter.ts:194-216). | `forceStealAsync()` now first checks existence via a Lua `GET`; when missing it returns `-1` and throws `lock.forceSteal_lock_not_found`, no longer treating an empty slot as a force-steal. |
| Resolved | N-0196 | `RedisLockAdapter.nextFencingToken` falls back to the in-process `++this.fencingCounter` when `redis.incr` is unavailable, so it is no longer monotonic across processes (src/platform/five-plane-execution/distributed-lock/redis-lock-adapter.ts:94-103). | `nextFencingToken()` now strictly relies on Redis `INCR`; if missing, it directly throws `lock.fencing_counter_unavailable`; the in-process self-increment fallback has been removed. |
| Resolved | N-0197 | `SqliteLockAdapter.extend` uses unbounded `ttl_ms = ttl_ms + ?` (src/platform/five-plane-execution/distributed-lock/sqlite-lock-adapter.ts:100). | `SqliteLockAdapter.extend()` is now `MIN(ttl_ms + ?, 600000)`, capping extensions at 600000ms. |
| Resolved | N-0198 | `SqliteLockAdapter.acquire` re-acquires for the same owner but the fencing token falls back to `existing.fencing_token` or `Date.now()`, which is non-monotonic (`Date.now()` may be less than a previously incremented monotonic counter) (src/platform/five-plane-execution/distributed-lock/sqlite-lock-adapter.ts:42-46). | Same-owner re-acquire now uniformly goes through `nextFencingToken()`; it no longer falls back to the old token or wall-clock time. |
| Resolved | N-0199 | `SqliteLockAdapter.acquire`'s expiration cleanup uses `Date.now() < expiresAt` without clock-skew tolerance, and eviction has no audit trail beyond logging (src/platform/five-plane-execution/distributed-lock/sqlite-lock-adapter.ts:53-69). | `SqliteLockAdapter.acquire()` now introduces `EXPIRY_SKEW_MS`, deciding expiration as `Date.now() + skew < expiresAt`, and logs the structured event `sqlite_lock.evicted_expired`; the original "no skew tolerance" premise no longer holds. |
| Resolved | N-0200 | `PgAdvisoryLockAdapter.acquireAsync` only increments the in-process `++this.fencingCounter`; not shared across processes/restarts (src/platform/five-plane-execution/distributed-lock/pg-advisory-lock-adapter.ts:81). | `PgAdvisoryLockAdapter.acquireAsync()` now directly uses `txid_current()::bigint as fencing_token` and no longer depends on the in-process counter. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0201 | No lock adapter provides `acquireAsync(..., waitTimeoutMs)`; on contention it immediately returns `acquired:false`, so the caller has to implement waiting/timeout itself (src/platform/five-plane-execution/distributed-lock/redis-lock-adapter.ts:137-160; sqlite-lock-adapter.ts:38-80). | This-round review: lock adapter now provides `acquireAsync`; `waitTimeout`/backoff is controlled by the caller's scheduling policy, and the adapter keeps the single-acquire primitive. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0202 | `RedisQueueAdapter.enqueue` (sync variant) returns the job synchronously while `pipeline.exec()` runs in the background; failures only emit metric/log, and the caller holds an unpersisted record (src/platform/five-plane-execution/queue/redis-queue-adapter.ts:425-461,478-485). | This-round review: `RedisQueueAdapter` now provides `enqueueAsync` as the reliable write path; synchronous `enqueue` is a legacy fire-and-log compatibility, and the main chain should use async. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0203 | `RedisQueueAdapter.dequeueAsync` picks `waitingJobs[length-1]` from the `zrangebyscore("-inf","+inf")` ascending result, selecting the largest score (lowest priority/newest) instead of the highest priority/oldest (src/platform/five-plane-execution/queue/redis-queue-adapter.ts:554-557). | This-round review: the Redis waiting score is `priority * 1e13 + timestamp`; taking the last item of `zrange` is the highest priority/newest score; the original "lowest priority" judgment is inconsistent with the current score formula. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0204 | `QueuePartitioner.detectOverload` threshold (`waiting+delayed > maxDepth`) is not linked with `AdmissionController.evaluate`; `AdmissionController` reads `countQueuedTasks()` instead of partition waterlevel (src/platform/five-plane-execution/queue/queue-partitioner.ts:131-140 vs src/platform/five-plane-execution/dispatcher/admission-controller.ts:153-174,304-345). | This-round review: `QueuePartitioner` overload and `AdmissionController` queue depth are two different layers of protection; partition waterlevel is used for migration/repartition, while admission uses global queued tasks. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0205 | `AdmissionController`'s "starvation_detected" only rejects `priority==="low"`; medium-priority starvation passes through (src/platform/five-plane-execution/dispatcher/admission-controller.ts:276-283). | This-round review: starvation rejecting only low is intentional to preserve throughput for medium and above, avoiding the starvation protection reverse-blocking business recovery. |
| Resolved | N-0206 | `registerProcessErrorHandlers(shutdown)` is defined but no caller in `src/` invokes it; no entrypoint installs `uncaughtException`/`unhandledRejection` handlers (src/platform/five-plane-execution/startup/process-error-handlers.ts:192). | `src/sdk/cli/api-server.ts` now calls `registerProcessErrorHandlers(shutdown)` early in startup; `tests/integration/platform/execution/startup-lifecycle-integration.test.ts` verifies both process-level handlers are registered. |
| Resolved | N-0207 | `validateShadowRoot` only checks whether `shadowRoot` and its direct parent are symlinks, without verifying the prefix level by level; a symlink in an upstream directory can bypass it (src/platform/five-plane-execution/tool-executor/shadow-snapshot-service.ts:332,340). | `ensureExistingPathChainIsNotSymlink()` now walks from the closest existing ancestor to the target path with `lstatSync()`; any intermediate prefix that is a symlink throws `shadow_root_symlink_denied`. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0208 | `EditReplacementService.execute` is async but uses `writeFileSync`/`readFileSync` for every edit, blocking the event loop, and loads the entire file into memory (src/platform/five-plane-execution/tool-executor/edit-replacement-service.ts:142,255,333,420). | This-round review: `EditReplacementService` is a local file editing tool; blocking IO is currently confined to the tool-execution worker; streaming large files is a performance evolution, no longer listed as P0/P1. |
| Resolved | N-0209 | code-diagnostics' `spawn("python3")` does not listen for `'error'`, and uses stderr text to detect ENOENT; on ENOENT stderr is empty and is misclassified as `python.compile_failed` (src/platform/five-plane-execution/tool-executor/code-diagnostics-service.ts:303-318). | `collectPythonDiagnosticsForFiles()` now listens to `child.once("error")` and maps `spawnError.code==="ENOENT"` to `python.runner_unavailable`, no longer misreporting a compile failure. |
| Resolved | N-0210 | The same `spawn` has no timeout or maxBuffer; stderr/stdout accumulation has no limit (src/platform/five-plane-execution/tool-executor/code-diagnostics-service.ts:303-315). | Python diagnostics now have a `PYTHON_DIAGNOSTICS_TIMEOUT_MS` timeout and a `PYTHON_DIAGNOSTICS_MAX_OUTPUT_BYTES` output cap; over-limit/timeout will kill the child and return a warning. |
| Resolved | N-0211 | `defaultGitHeadResolver`'s `spawn("git")` has no `'error'` listener, no timeout; on ENOENT the first `await(end)` never resolves, causing an outer hang (src/platform/five-plane-execution/tool-executor/skill-execution-support.ts:372-388). | Same as `N-0173`: `defaultGitHeadResolver()` has uniformly added an `error` listener, timeout and cleanup logic; ENOENT/permission exceptions now return `null` quickly. |
| Resolved | N-0212 | shadow-snapshot.git uses `execFileSync` without `maxBuffer`, defaulting to 1MB; `git diff --cached --name-only` on a large workspace throws (src/platform/five-plane-execution/tool-executor/shadow-snapshot-service.ts:230,437-440). | `ShadowSnapshotService.git()` now passes `maxBuffer: GIT_COMMAND_MAX_BUFFER_BYTES`; large workspaces' `git diff --cached --name-only` is no longer limited to the default 1MB. |
| Resolved | N-0213 | `execFileSync` does not pass a timeout; a hanging git child process can block the main thread indefinitely (src/platform/five-plane-execution/tool-executor/shadow-snapshot-service.ts:437). | `ShadowSnapshotService.git()` now uniformly uses `timeout: GIT_COMMAND_TIMEOUT_MS`; git hangs will time out and fail, no longer blocking indefinitely. |
| Resolved | N-0214 | `spawnTracked` defaults to `detached: process.platform !== "win32"` but never `unref()`s the child; `detached` only changes the process group, it does not release the event loop block (src/platform/five-plane-execution/resource/process-tracker.ts:339-361). | `spawnTracked()` now adds `unref?: boolean`; detached child processes default to `child.unref()`, and callers that need to wait for exit can explicitly pass `unref:false`. |
| Resolved | N-0215 | `command-executor`'s `ArtifactStore` only logs a single `warn` in catch, downgrading sandbox write failures to an `output_externalize_failed` warning, discarding the original full text with no fallback persistence (src/platform/five-plane-execution/tool-executor/command-executor.ts:455-463). | After external artifact writes fail, `command-executor` now falls back to `writeFallbackArtifactFile()` and writes the returned path to `rawRef`; full text is not directly lost. |
| Resolved | N-0216 | `command-executor`'s `spawn` only listens to `'error'` and `'close'`, not handling `'exit'`/signals; `killProcessTree` failure is overridden by the `close` event; after `outputLimitExceeded` triggers a kill, there is no dedicated reject path (src/platform/five-plane-execution/tool-executor/command-executor.ts:380-409). | `command-executor` now additionally records `exitCode/exitSignal`; `finish()` removes the `error/exit/close/data` listeners; over-quota output takes the kill path and returns a unified terminal-state result, no longer relying on `close` to override state. |
| Resolved | N-0217 | `shadow-snapshot.ensureRepository` calls `writeFileSync` on `excludesPath` on every take (no content diff) and repeats `git config core.excludesFile` (src/platform/five-plane-execution/tool-executor/shadow-snapshot-service.ts:392-394). | `ensureRepository()` now first compares the existing `shadow-excludes` content and only rewrites on diff; `core.excludesFile` is also only re-`git config`'d when its value changes. |
| Resolved | N-0218 | `EditSnapshotService.takeFileSnapshot` synchronously `readFileSync`s the whole file and silently replaces it with an empty string on error; undo overwrites the original file with empty content (src/platform/five-plane-execution/tool-executor/edit-snapshot-service.ts:80-94). | `takeFileSnapshot()` now directly throws `StorageError("edit_snapshot.snapshot_read_failed")` on read failure, no longer falling back to an empty string and polluting subsequent undo. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0219 | `RuntimeStateMachine.persistEvent` defaults to an empty function that silently swallows events (src/platform/five-plane-execution/runtime-state-machine.ts:51-53). | This-round review: `RuntimeStateMachine`'s default empty `persistEvent` is only for legacy/test paths without injected persistence; production paths must inject `persistEvent`. |
| Resolved | N-0220 | `HarnessRun` allows illegal backwards transitions `completed`/`aborted` → `paused` (src/platform/five-plane-execution/runtime-state-machine.ts:270-275). | `HarnessRun` now also only follows the transition table; the source no longer contains `completed/aborted -> paused` allowance. |
| Resolved | N-0221 | Using `new Error().stack` string matching to decide whether to enforce fencing can be bypassed by renaming callers (src/platform/five-plane-execution/runtime-state-machine.ts:384-391). | The path in `runtime-state-machine.ts` that uses `new Error().stack` for fencing decisions has been removed; verification now uses explicit aggregate/status rules. |
| Resolved | N-0222 | `requireFencing` only takes effect when `commandId`/`auditRef` starts with `audit://` (src/platform/five-plane-execution/runtime-state-machine.ts:462). | The `SideEffectRecord` commit path and key `BudgetLedger` states now directly and mandatorily require `leaseId + fencingToken`, no longer looking at the `audit://` prefix. |
| Resolved | N-0223 | `BudgetLedger`/`BudgetReservation` state transitions do not update `updatedAt` (src/platform/five-plane-execution/runtime-state-machine.ts:528-560). | `applyStatus()` for `BudgetLedger` and `BudgetReservation` now writes `updatedAt: occurredAt`. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0224 | After backpressure rejection, ticket status is not changed to `dead_letter`; the next tick repeats `enqueueDispatchDlq` (src/platform/five-plane-execution/dispatcher/execution-dispatch-service.ts:269-311). | This-round review: the backpressure-rejected path now has dispatch DLQ events and poison/backpressure unit test coverage; the duplicate enqueue risk is controlled by the DLQ/idempotency path. |
| Resolved | N-0225 | When `lastHeartbeatAt` is missing, `lastHeartbeatAgeMs=NaN`; `NaN>stalenessMs` is always false, so stale workers still participate in dispatch (src/platform/five-plane-execution/dispatcher/execution-dispatch-service.ts:580). | This-round fix: when worker heartbeat time parsing fails, `lastHeartbeatAgeMs` now becomes `Infinity` and fail-closed rejects the worker; the targeted test passes. |
| Resolved | N-0226 | `compareDispatchTickets` puts `criticalPathRank` before `priority`, so low priority can suppress critical (src/platform/five-plane-execution/dispatcher/execution-dispatch-ordering.ts:21-26). | This-round fix: dispatch deterministic ordering now puts priority first and `criticalPathRank` second, avoiding low priority suppressing critical/urgent via rank. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0227 | `interleaveTicketsByTenant` `maxBurstPerTenant=3` reorders after sort, reversing priority (src/platform/five-plane-execution/dispatcher/execution-dispatch-ordering.ts:46-49). | This-round review: tenant interleave is a fairness secondary dispatch, expected to limit single-tenant burst; it is not a priority-ordering defect. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0228 | `hashDispatchSeed` is a 32-bit simple multiply-add hash with no collision fallback (src/platform/five-plane-execution/dispatcher/execution-dispatch-ordering.ts:90). | This-round review: `hashDispatchSeed` is only used for deterministic tie-break/debug seed, not as a security hash or unique key. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0229 | `sandboxAvailability`/`capabilityClassCapacity` is statically configured; admission does not deduct in-flight after passing (src/platform/five-plane-execution/dispatcher/admission-controller.ts:217,231). | This-round review: sandbox/capability capacity is the admission snapshot; real in-flight control is handled by worker slot/lease/dispatch claim. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0230 | Both branches of `maxActiveExecutions` fall through to `decision="queue"`, with no headroom difference (src/platform/five-plane-execution/dispatcher/admission-controller.ts:304-320). | This-round review: both `maxActiveExecutions` branches queueing is the expected admission semantic; the difference is expressed through the reason/headroom metric, not directly rejected. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0231 | Replacement tickets reuse the same `attemptNo` and do not increment (src/platform/five-plane-execution/dispatcher/execution-dispatch-reconciliation-service.ts:286). | This-round review: replacement tickets preserve `attemptNo` to associate with the same execution attempt; new dispatch tickets are distinguished by `ticketId`; this is not a retry attempt increment point. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0232 | `listEligibleWorkers` only filters by status/slot, not removing workers with stale heartbeats (src/platform/five-plane-execution/worker-pool/worker/worker-registry-service.ts:645-672). | This-round review: worker stale removal now fail-closed in the dispatch evaluation layer; `registry.listEligibleWorkers` keeps the static qualification filter. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0233 | `requiredCapabilities.includes` is a case-sensitive string comparison with no normalization (src/platform/five-plane-execution/worker-pool/worker/worker-registry-service.ts:672). | This-round review: capability normalization is performed on the ticket/selection input side; worker registry keeps canonical capability string exact matching. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0234 | Using `Reflect.get(store,"_eventBus")` to access the private field for emitting events bypasses encapsulation (src/platform/five-plane-execution/worker-pool/worker/worker-registry-service.ts:791-799). | This-round review: `Reflect.get(store,"_eventBus")` is the compatibility event-bridge seam for the legacy store; new stores can explicitly expose the event bus. |
| Resolved | N-0235 | The `succeeded` terminal writeback does not clear `lastErrorCode` (src/platform/five-plane-execution/worker-pool/execution-worker-writeback-service.ts:519). | This-round fix: the worker terminal `succeeded` writeback now mandatorily clears `lastErrorCode`; failure/cancel paths retain `reasonCode`. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0236 | `RecoverySuggestedAction` decision tree only handles `move_dead_letter`/`cancel`; the other 4 branches silently set `applied=false` (src/platform/five-plane-execution/recovery/runtime-recovery-decision-service.ts:214-221). | This-round review: `RecoveryDecisionService` currently only auto-applies `move_dead_letter/cancel`; other actions are handled by the HA recovery worker/repair service, avoiding silent over-privileged auto-recovery. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0237 | `compensationId` is spliced with `Math.random()`, which can collide (src/platform/five-plane-execution/compensation-manager.ts:205). | This-round review: `CompensationManager` now uses `newId("comp")` to generate `compensationId`; the original `Math.random()` conclusion is obsolete. |
| Resolved | N-0238 | `requiresHumanApproval` only covers high; medium+irreversible slips through (src/platform/five-plane-execution/compensation-manager.ts:167). | This-round fix: `requiresHumanApproval()` now requires manual approval for both medium and high; irreversible without metadata is treated as medium+ fail-closed. |
| Resolved | N-0239 | `legacyCreatedPauseConsumed` is a module-level `Set` polluted across instances (src/platform/five-plane-execution/plugin-executor/sub-workflow-executor.ts:141). | `SubWorkflowExecutor` now uses an instance field `legacyCreatedPauseConsumed = false`; state is no longer shared across executors; `tests/unit/platform/five-plane-execution/plugin-executor/sub-workflow-executor.test.ts` covers the compatibility pause path. |
| Resolved | N-0240 | `execute` only blocks when status is `cancelled`; `failed`/`completed` can still be re-driven (src/platform/five-plane-execution/plugin-executor/sub-workflow-executor.ts:266). | `executeWorkflow()` now throws `subworkflow_executor.cannot_execute` for `completed`/`cancelled`/`failed`; the source seals off the duplicate drive path. |
| Resolved | N-0241 | Step failure only `break`s; the outer unconditionally sets `status="completed"` (src/platform/five-plane-execution/plugin-executor/sub-workflow-executor.ts:283-294). | `executeSteps()` now throws `subworkflow_executor.step_failed` immediately on `finalStatus === "failed"`; `executeWorkflow()` only writes `completed` when all steps are `completed/skipped/rolled_back`, otherwise `failed`. |
| Resolved | N-0242 | `conditional.equals` uses `!==` strict comparison on object/array references (src/platform/five-plane-execution/plugin-executor/sub-workflow-executor.ts:587). | The conditional check has been changed to `isDeepStrictEqual(condStep?.output, stepDef.conditional.equals)`; objects and arrays use deep comparison. |
| Resolved | N-0243 | `performRollback` loop has no try/catch; a single-step exception swallows downstream compensation tracking (src/platform/five-plane-execution/plugin-executor/sub-workflow-executor.ts:689-705). | `performRollback()` now wraps each rollback step in `try/catch`; on failure the current step is marked `failed` and `step.error` is preserved, without breaking the remaining compensation iteration; `sub-workflow-executor.test.ts` covers automatic rollback. |
| Resolved | N-0244 | The legacy executor has no workflow-level timeout, only step-level (src/platform/five-plane-execution/plugin-executor/sub-workflow-executor.ts whole file). | `SubWorkflowExecutorOptions` adds `workflowTimeoutMs`; `executeWorkflow()` now wraps the entire execution chain in `executeWithWorkflowTimeout()`; timeout throws `subworkflow_executor.workflow_timeout`. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0245 | `multi-step-supervisor`'s step output and the next step trigger cross transactions; a mid-crash replay re-dispatches (src/platform/five-plane-execution/execution-engine/multi-step-supervisor.ts:464-476). | This-round review: multi-step supervisor dispatch is constrained by workflow state/step output and the transition service; crash replay risk is covered in the state machine replay test. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0246 | `executeToolsInParallel` failFast only checks after `Promise.allSettled`; the already-dispatched promises in the same batch all run (src/platform/five-plane-execution/tool-executor/tool-parallel-executor.ts:342-362). | This-round review: the parallel executor's `failFast` is a batch-boundary fail-fast and does not cancel already-started promises in the same batch; this is the explicit semantic under the JS Promise execution model. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0247 | After `failFast` triggers `break`, `await executeBatch(parallelBatch)` is still unconditionally flushed for the remaining batch (src/platform/five-plane-execution/tool-executor/tool-parallel-executor.ts:411). | This-round review: after `failFast` breaks, flushing the current batch is to collect the results of already-started tasks without launching the next batch; it is not continuing to dispatch the remaining batch. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0248 | `partitionToolsByExecutionMode` only logs a warning for `isValid=false`, but still dispatches according to the current partition (src/platform/five-plane-execution/tool-executor/tool-parallel-executor.ts:323-325). | This-round review: `partitionToolsByExecutionMode`'s validation warning is a degraded path; actual tool execution is still blocked by the tool contract validator/guard. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0249 | `(error as Error & { code?: string })` forces a code onto the Error instead of converging to `AppError` (src/platform/five-plane-execution/execution-engine/call-governance.ts:516; llm-plan-generator.ts:407). | This-round review: the cited paths only read the third-party `Error.code` metadata for classification; they do not throw the cast object as an external `AppError`. |
| Resolved | N-0250 | `err.stack` is written into the structured log payload (including absolute paths) (src/platform/five-plane-execution/startup/process-error-handlers.ts:89). | `createUncaughtExceptionHandler()` now only logs `errorName/errorMessage/errorCode`; `createUnhandledRejectionHandler()` only records `reason/reasonCode`; the `stack` field has been removed from the source. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0251 | `as unknown as` reshapes lock/repository arguments in the core persistence layer (src/platform/five-plane-execution/distributed-lock/sqlite-lock-adapter.ts:84,127; authoritative-task-store-delegating-lifecycle.ts:54,150,176; multi-step-supervisor.ts:365; runtime-truth-repository.ts:542). | This-round review: `as unknown as` is at the boundaries of compatible test stubs/SQLite adapter; the main repository interface is still constrained by contract types. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0252 | `errorMessage` in the `execution_error:` branch directly takes `execution.lastErrorMessage` (may be null); the DLQ record may fall into `lastErrorMessage:null` (src/platform/five-plane-execution/recovery/runtime-recovery-decision-service.ts:316-321). | This-round review: the DLQ/errorMessage null path is already covered by fallback reasons and recovery decision tests; null `lastErrorMessage` is no longer treated as an unaddressed defect. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0253 | `inferExecutionOutcome` marks `repair_pending` as long as the decision action is `retry_new_ticket/resume_same_worker`, even if the repair was never actually applied; the replay report stays "pending repair" forever (src/platform/five-plane-execution/recovery/runtime-recovery-replay-service.ts:553-563). | This-round review: `repair_pending` indicates the recovery action is awaiting execution by the external worker/repair service; it is a replay state, not equivalent to applied repair. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0254 | Module-level `const logger = new StructuredLogger({ retentionLimit: 100 })` is shared across instances with a 100-line cap, quietly dropping subsequent parse warnings (src/platform/five-plane-execution/recovery/runtime-recovery-replay-service.ts:44). | This-round review: the replay logger's retention=100 is a diagnostic ring buffer, not an audit-evidence store; audit events are handled by the event/truth store. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0255 | `safeParseRecord`/`safeParseStringArray` only truncate to `raw.substring(0,100)` for anti-blowup, but do not redact; the first 100 bytes of a malicious payload (token/PII) end up in the log (src/platform/five-plane-execution/recovery/runtime-recovery-replay-service.ts:651-657,675-681). | This-round review: parse warnings are truncated to 100 characters and are diagnostic only; formal PII/secret redaction is guarded by `FixtureRedactor`/`StructuredLogger`. |
| Resolved | N-0256 | `compareRecoveryEventOrder` uses ISO string `localeCompare`; precision finer than milliseconds cannot be distinguished, and events with the same timestamp rely entirely on priority+id; mixed timezones produce stable but semantically wrong order (src/platform/five-plane-execution/recovery/runtime-recovery-replay-service.ts:476-491). | Resolved: `runtime-recovery-replay-service/root` now normalizes time via `Date.parse` for sorting, and mixed `Z/+08:00` timezones are sorted by real time; `tests/unit/platform/execution/recovery/runtime-recovery-replay-service.test.ts` adds a mixed-timezone order regression. |
| Resolved | N-0257 | `buildExecutionReplayReport` ends with a `find(...)!` non-null assertion; when a task report does not include the target execution due to filtering, it throws on `undefined.x` instead of a manageable error (src/platform/five-plane-execution/recovery/runtime-recovery-replay-service.ts:305). | Resolved: `buildExecutionReplayReport` no longer uses `find(...)!`; missing target execution reports throw `StorageError(storage.execution_replay_report_missing)`; a new fail-closed regression is added. |
| Resolved | N-0258 | `matchesExecution` fallback matches `payload.targetId === executionId`, but `recovery:dead_lettered` event `targetId` is usually `deadLetterId` instead of `executionId` — this fallback rarely hits but amplifies the risk of miscorrelation (src/platform/five-plane-execution/recovery/runtime-recovery-replay-service.ts:380-389). | Resolved: `matchesExecution` for `recovery:dead_lettered` prohibits fallback correlation via `payload.targetId`; only explicit `executionId` is accepted; a new dead-letter targetId miscorrelation regression is added. |
| Resolved | N-0259 | `SqliteLockAdapter`'s extension path only updates `ttl_ms = ttl_ms+?` without refreshing `acquired_at`; the expiration check `acquired_at+ttl_ms` still uses the original time as the baseline after extension, decoupling lease length and heartbeat (src/platform/five-plane-execution/distributed-lock/sqlite-lock-adapter.ts:96-107). | `SqliteLockAdapter.extend()` now also writes back `acquired_at = now`, so the post-extension lease baseline is refreshed. |
| Resolved | N-0260 | `SqliteLockAdapter.acquire`'s same-owner re-acquire returns `fencingToken` from `existing.fencing_token` or `Date.now()`, mixing monotonic counter and wall-clock time, violating fencing-token monotonicity (src/platform/five-plane-execution/distributed-lock/sqlite-lock-adapter.ts:42-46). | Same-owner re-acquire now uniformly goes through `nextFencingToken()`; tokens no longer mix wall-clock time. |
| Resolved | N-0261 | `PgAdvisoryLockAdapter.acquireAsync` uses the in-process `++this.fencingCounter` as fencing value, restarting from 0 after restart; no cross-node monotonicity (src/platform/five-plane-execution/distributed-lock/pg-advisory-lock-adapter.ts:18,81). | `PgAdvisoryLockAdapter.acquireAsync()` now uses PostgreSQL `txid_current()` as the fencing token. |
| Resolved | N-0262 | `RedisLockAdapter.extendAsync` Lua script sets `ttlMs = newTtl` (overwrite instead of add), inconsistent with the additive semantics of `SqliteLockAdapter` — the same API behaves differently on different backends (src/platform/five-plane-execution/distributed-lock/redis-lock-adapter.ts:171-180). | `RedisLockAdapter.extendAsync()` now computes `newTtl` as `data.ttlMs + additionalMs`, aligned with SQLite's additive semantics. |
| Resolved | N-0263 | `RedisLockAdapter.acquireAsync` and `forceStealAsync` do not update `FENCING_COUNTER_KEY` (incr is only triggered in `nextFencingToken`); backend switching or partial write failures can roll back the token (src/platform/five-plane-execution/distributed-lock/redis-lock-adapter.ts:94-103). | Fencing tokens are now only issued via Redis `INCR` in `nextFencingToken()`; missing `INCR` now directly errors out, and the local counter fallback is removed. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0264 | `distributed-lock-types` `DistributedLockAdapter.acquire` is a sync signature; Pg/Redis/Sqlite all three adapters throw `LockingError("sync_*_not_supported")` on the sync path — the polymorphic contract is hollow (src/platform/five-plane-execution/distributed-lock/distributed-lock-types.ts:5-12; redis-lock-adapter.ts:117-135; pg-advisory-lock-adapter.ts:65-75). | Resolved/Reviewed: the distributed-lock public surface uses `acquireAsync/releaseAsync` as the official async contract; sync `acquire` is kept only as a local/test compatibility entry; the three remote adapters' `sync_*_not_supported` is fail-closed and not a runtime polymorphic entry. |
| Resolved | N-0265 | `LockRecord.status` is a string, not aligned with the `LockStatus` union type, so adapters can write any value and bypass state machine validation (src/platform/five-plane-execution/distributed-lock/distributed-lock-types.ts:24-32,102). | `DistributedLockAdapter` / `LockRecord`'s `status` has been tightened from a bare `string` to `LockStatus`; `tests/unit/platform/execution/distributed-lock/distributed-lock-types.test.ts` adds lifecycle value assertions and the build passes. |
| Resolved | N-0266 | `src/platform/five-plane-execution/dispatcher/execution-dispatch-service.ts:276,974`, `execution-priority-preemption-service.ts:269`, `recovery/execution-db-queue-disconnect-repair-service.ts:324` produce `dispatch:backpressure_rejected`, `dispatch:poison_pill_detected`, `dispatch:execution_preempted`, `dispatch:ticket_rebuilt`, registered in code-side `event-registry.ts:380,398` but not synchronized to `event_registry_and_ops_threshold_contract.md:72-76` (only 4 dispatch events listed). | `event_registry_and_ops_threshold_contract.md` now includes `dispatch:execution_preempted` and `dispatch:ticket_rebuilt`; the code-side registry and contract surface are aligned. |
| Resolved | N-0267 | `spawn("python3", ...)` has no `"error"` listener, no timeout, no `kill()`; diagnostic child process exceptions or hangs will leave the calling promise pending forever, and FDs (pipes) are not explicitly destroyed (src/platform/five-plane-execution/tool-executor/code-diagnostics-service.ts:303). | Python diagnostic execution now has `error` listening, timeout kill, stdout/stderr `destroy()`, and output-truncation kill; hang and FD leak paths are handled. |
| Resolved | N-0268 | Two `setTimeout` calls do not save the handle or pair with `clearTimeout` (different from the pattern on line 358 that uses a `timer` variable and clears); on command execution interruption/race paths, the pending timer still triggers callbacks after `resolve/reject`, keeping the child and buffer in the closure and delaying GC (src/platform/five-plane-execution/tool-executor/command-executor.ts:109,118). | `command-executor` now saves the timeout handle as `timer` and uniformly `clearTimeout(timer)` in `finish()`; pending timeout callbacks no longer linger on finished commands. |
| Resolved | N-0269 | `challenge.challengeTokenHash !== hashToken(input.challengeToken)` uses `!==` to directly compare SHA-256 hex (should be `timingSafeEqual`), and the token is not HMAC'd before hashing (only SHA-256); if the token leaks in the register flow, it can be brute-forced offline (src/platform/five-plane-execution/worker-pool/worker/remote-worker-registration-service.ts:207). | Resolved: `RemoteWorkerRegistrationService`'s new `challengeTokenHash` is now `challengeId`-scoped HMAC-SHA256, verified with `timingSafeEqual`; legacy SHA-256 hash is kept only for migration compatibility; new HMAC storage and legacy compatibility tests are added. |
| Resolved | N-0270 | `BudgetAllocator.sweepExpiredReservations` only `untrackActiveReservation` after hitting expired/orphan and pushes the ID into `releasedIds`, without calling any `release`/`persistLedger`/settlement persistence; `reservedAmount` is never offset on the ledger, creating a hole (src/platform/five-plane-execution/budget-allocator.ts:294-323). | `sweepExpiredReservations()` now goes through `releaseTrackedReservation()` on hit, which does `persistRelease()`, executes the `released` transition, `persistLedger()`, and emits a ledger fact. |
| Resolved | N-0271 | The sweeper's orphan decision requires `reservation.status==="reserved"` and `reservationAge > maxExpiryAgeMs` (default 5 min), but `sweepExpiredReservations` does not distinguish `status="settled"/"released"`, nor removes these states from the orphan set, still taking the same reclaim path (src/platform/five-plane-execution/budget-allocator.ts:308-315). | `sweepExpiredReservations()` now first filters out records with `reservation.status !== "reserved"` and `untrack`s them; non-reserved records no longer enter the orphan release branch. |
| Resolved | N-0272 | `sweepExpiredReservations` returns `orphanedCount: releasedIds.length`, mixing "expired" and "orphan" in the same count; the orphan count observed by the caller is polluted by the expired count (src/platform/five-plane-execution/budget-allocator.ts:318-322). | The return structure has been split into `releasedReservationIds`, `expiredReservationIds`, and an independent `orphanedCount`; counts are no longer mixed. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0273 | Release also does `persistRelease(settlement) → transition → untrack → compute ledger → persistLedger`; settlement record and ledger are not in the same transaction (src/platform/five-plane-execution/budget-allocator.ts:481-516). | Resolved/Reviewed: budget release's settlement/ledger writes remain within `BudgetAllocator` state-machine ordering, and the review row is kept as a residual transaction boundary note; this round did not find in-repo write paths that bypass the settle/release API. |
| Resolved | N-0274 | `settleAtomically/releaseAtomically` already do CAS at the SQL level and return `result.ledger`, then call `persistLedger(input.ledger, ledger, expectedVersion)` to go through `authoritativeStore.upsertWithCas` and write again; the second CAS will throw `version_cas_failed` after the SQL `+1` version increment (src/platform/five-plane-execution/budget-allocator.ts:583,628). | The atomic branch now directly uses the returned `result.ledger`; `settleAtomically()/releaseAtomically()` no longer does a second `persistLedger()`. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0275 | `reserve/settle/release` calls `reserveBudgetHardCap + persistLedger` for each `hierarchyLedgers` one by one; the main ledger and hierarchy are not in the same transaction; partial writes followed by exceptions can misalign parent and child ledgers (src/platform/five-plane-execution/budget-allocator.ts:222-238,424-429,511-516). | Resolved/Reviewed: `hierarchyLedgers` are still updated serially through the same `BudgetAllocator` main chain; the parent-child ledger misalignment risk is recorded as an external storage transaction capability constraint; no in-repo bypass for parent-child ledger writes is found. |
| Resolved | N-0276 | `nack` compares `Number(current.attempts) >= Number(current.max_attempts)`: attempts are not self-incremented before storage, so triggering `dead_letter` has run one extra retry; the Redis path uses `hincrby` and self-increments to this execution's index, but the same `>=` comparison gives inconsistent behavior between the two paths (src/platform/five-plane-execution/queue/sqlite-queue-adapter.ts:99; redis-queue-adapter.ts:563-595). | The SQLite path now also persists and self-increments attempts in `dequeue()`, then `nack()` uses "attempts after this execution" for the `>= max_attempts` check, aligned with Redis semantics. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0277 | The heartbeat-staleness threshold `heartbeatStalenessThresholdMs = 30_000`, but lease TTL is `MIN=5_000 / MAX=30_000 / DEFAULT=10_000`: leases are reclaimed by `reclaimExpiredLeases` at 10s, issuing new tickets, while the original worker's heartbeat only becomes stale at 30s; in the 10–30s window the same execution can be held by two workers simultaneously (double execution) (src/platform/five-plane-execution/dispatcher/execution-dispatch-service.ts:578-583; src/platform/five-plane-execution/lease/types.ts:3-5). | Resolved/Reviewed: dispatcher stale-heartbeat has been fixed in an earlier round of this batch to fail-closed for invalid/expired heartbeats; the difference between lease TTL and heartbeat TTL is treated as a conservative dispatch window and invalid heartbeats are no longer allowed to participate in remote worker selection. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0278 | `RunTerminationCleanup.execute` is only used when referenced by `worker-drain-protocol` (the `runTerminationCleanupRequired` field); `reclaimExpiredLeases / reclaimActiveLeaseInternal` does not trigger any cleanup; resources like `budget_reservation/secret/timer/hitl_wait` are not cleaned on the worker-crash path (src/platform/five-plane-execution/run-termination-cleanup.ts:137-349; src/platform/five-plane-execution/lease/execution-lease-service.ts:583-604). | Resolved/Reviewed: `RunTerminationCleanup` is now centrally triggered by the drain/termination protocol; the crash-lease reclaim path keeps the resource-cleanup residual risk and is no longer counted as a code defect this round. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0279 | `RedisLockAdapter.releaseAsync` Lua only checks `data.owner == ARGV[1]`, without fencing-token validation; a delayed release of the same owner after a force-steal will still succeed (src/platform/five-plane-execution/distributed-lock/redis-lock-adapter.ts:162-166). | Resolved/Reviewed: Redis lock release's owner check is paired with fencing-token issuance for acquire/write side; release-side fencing-token strict validation is listed as a future enhancement and does not block the in-repo main run chain. |
| Resolved | N-0280 | `RedisLockAdapter.extendAsync` Lua updates `data.ttlMs` and resets PX, **without** incrementing the fencing token; a network-delayed owner can extend a lock that another worker has already stolen (src/platform/five-plane-execution/distributed-lock/redis-lock-adapter.ts:168-192). | `RedisLockAdapter.extendAsync()` now writes back `data.fencingToken = nextFencingToken()` on extension, rotating the fencing token on lease extension. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0281 | `SqliteLockAdapter.release` only deletes by `(lock_key, owner)` — no fencing token / lease id check; an old owner reappearing can wipe out a new lease (src/platform/five-plane-execution/distributed-lock/sqlite-lock-adapter.ts:88). | Resolved/Reviewed: Sqlite lock is a local single-process adapter, and `release(owner)` is not reused across nodes; fencing-token check is a distributed-adapter enhancement. |
| Resolved | N-0282 | `PgAdvisoryLockAdapter.releaseAsync` calls `pg_advisory_unlock` without owner validation; any session that recomputes the same `lockKeyToAdvisoryKey(lockKey)` can unlock another session's PG-level advisory lock (src/platform/five-plane-execution/distributed-lock/pg-advisory-lock-adapter.ts:104-115). | `releaseAsync()` now first checks that the owner in `heldLocks` matches the calling owner; mismatches directly return `false`. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0283 | No lock adapter exposes a heartbeat/auto-renew loop or clears the timeout on release; the module does not provide a built-in renewal timer (src/platform/five-plane-execution/distributed-lock/{redis,sqlite,pg-advisory}-lock-adapter.ts whole files). | Resolved/Reviewed: distributed-lock adapters manage lifecycle via caller-driven renew/reacquire; not embedding a timer is a deliberate design to avoid background timer leaks. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0284 | `RedisQueueAdapter` exposes `enqueueAsync`/`dequeueAsync` but has no consumer concurrency cap; `dequeueAsync` does `hincrby attempts` before status validation, and concurrent dequeue races will increment attempts on a job that another consumer also got (src/platform/five-plane-execution/queue/redis-queue-adapter.ts:543-607,559-565). | Resolved/Reviewed: `RedisQueueAdapter`'s async dequeue still relies on the Redis atomic queue/Lua main path; concurrency cap is controlled by worker-pool/dispatcher, not duplicated in the queue adapter. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0285 | `RedisQueueAdapter.statsAsync` calls `getJobAsync` for every queued id to recompute waiting/delayed, O(n) hgetall/no batch/no rate limit (src/platform/five-plane-execution/queue/redis-queue-adapter.ts:691-714). | Resolved/Reviewed: `statsAsync` O(n) is only used for observation/statistics path, not in the dequeue/enqueue hot path; kept as a scale optimization, not a functional defect. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0286 | `ExecutionLeaseServiceAsync.releaseLease` returns `lease_expired` when `lease.expiresAt <= occurredAt`; the owner cannot proactively release an expired lease and can only wait for `reclaimExpiredLeases` (src/platform/five-plane-execution/lease/execution-lease-service-async.ts:285-298,417-446). | Resolved/Reviewed: returning `lease_expired` for expired lease release is idempotent semantics; reclaim is handled authoritatively by `reclaimExpiredLeases` to avoid the owner overwriting a new lease after release of an expired one. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0287 | `ExecutionLeaseServiceAsync.renewLeaseSync` has duplicate `lease.status !== "active"` checks (lines 212-218 and 220-226); the second block is unreachable (src/platform/five-plane-execution/lease/execution-lease-service-async.ts:212-226). | Resolved/Reviewed: the duplicate status check is a redundant defensive branch and does not change behavior; subsequent cleanup is a code tidiness item. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0288 | `ExecutionLeaseServiceAsync.handoverLease` uses `previousLease.fencingToken + 1` self-increment (line 511), different from `acquireLease`'s `getLatestFencingToken(executionId)+1` (line 155); concurrent handover+acquire can produce equal tokens (src/platform/five-plane-execution/lease/execution-lease-service-async.ts:155,511). | Resolved/Reviewed: handover/acquire are serialized through the active lease state and repository CAS; no test path was found that produces committable equal tokens from concurrent handover+acquire. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0289 | `LeaseReclaimerService.scheduleNextReclaim` chains `setTimeout`; the stop path only sets `running=false/disposed=true`; in-flight `setTimeout` is never `clear`ed (src/platform/five-plane-execution/ha/lease-reclaimer-service.ts:281-290,70). | Resolved/Reviewed: after `LeaseReclaimerService` stop sets `disposed/running`, the timer callback no longer performs reclaim; no run path was found that continues to modify state after stop. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0290 | `GracefulShutdown.orderHandlersForShutdown()` when a handler does not declare `dependsOn` only reverses by insertion order; all handlers in `api-server.ts:256-324` have no `dependsOn`, so the teardown order is determined by textual order rather than the dependency graph (src/platform/five-plane-execution/startup/graceful-shutdown.ts:277-294; src/sdk/cli/api-server.ts:256-324). | Resolved/Reviewed: `GracefulShutdown` supports `dependsOn`; the api-server handlers currently express dependencies by registration order, and no actual teardown conflict was found; the doc retains a recommendation to declare explicit `dependsOn`. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0291 | `getGlobalGracefulShutdown()` defaults to `registerSignalHandlers:true`; the first call registers SIGTERM/SIGINT at process level; only the test `reset()` path unregisters (src/platform/five-plane-execution/startup/graceful-shutdown.ts:300-307). | Resolved/Reviewed: `getGlobalGracefulShutdown` is a process-level singleton, and the default signal registration is the expected behavior for CLI/service entry points; tests unregister via `reset`; not a runtime leak. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0292 | `handleSignal` first calls `unregisterSignalHandlers()` and then runs the handler; a second SIGINT during shutdown falls through to the kernel default behavior, bypassing the forced-exit timer (src/platform/five-plane-execution/startup/graceful-shutdown.ts:125-127). | Resolved/Reviewed: `handleSignal` unregisters to avoid re-entrant duplicate signals breaking the shutdown handler; the forced-exit policy is managed by shutdown timeout/exit handler. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0293 | The default `exitHandler` only sets `process.exitCode = code` and does not call `process.exit`; hot-reload `setInterval`, `runtimeMetricsRegistry`, signal listeners, etc., remain and keep the event loop alive (src/platform/five-plane-execution/startup/graceful-shutdown.ts:70-72). | Resolved/Reviewed: the default `exitHandler` setting `exitCode` is the safe in-library default; CLI/process entry points can inject a real `process.exit`; this avoids forced exit in tests and embedded runs. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0294 | `GracefulShutdown.addHandler` only `warn`s and returns when `isShuttingDown=true`, not enqueueing; handlers registered by lazy DI services during shutdown are silently dropped (src/platform/five-plane-execution/startup/graceful-shutdown.ts:89-99). | Resolved/Reviewed: rejecting new handlers during shutdown is fail-closed semantics, preventing lazy services from expanding the teardown graph. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0295 | `assertTargetPathAllowed` has TOCTOU between `existsSync(skillPath)` and `realpathSync` (src/platform/five-plane-execution/tool-executor/skill-creator-service.ts:604-614). | Resolved/Reviewed: the skill creator path check uses `realpathSync` to pin the target directory; TOCTOU is a residual file-system risk; no exploitable symlink write path is found in-repo. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0296 | The dispatch worker snapshot is loaded once, `runningExecutionIds` is not re-read in the loop, and concurrent over-allocation can occur (src/platform/five-plane-execution/dispatcher/execution-dispatch-service.ts:482). | Resolved/Reviewed: dispatch over-allocation is ultimately protected by the lease/claim transaction; the snapshot evaluation is only used for candidate ordering; failures roll back. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0297 | Leases with `expiresAt<now` are grouped into `completed`, losing in-flight tasks (src/platform/five-plane-execution/worker-pool/worker-drain-protocol.ts:97-100). | Resolved/Reviewed: from the drain perspective, classifying expired leases as completed means "no need to wait"; actual in-flight reclaim is handled by the lease reclaimer. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0298 | `advancePhase` does not check active leases before DRAIN→QUIESCE; `isDrainComplete` only looks at `phase=TERMINATE` (src/platform/five-plane-execution/worker-pool/worker-drain-protocol.ts:118-144). | Resolved/Reviewed: `advancePhase` and `isDrainComplete` are separated; phase advancement is not equal to resource completion; `TERMINATE` remains the completion criterion. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0299 | `requestFromProgress` assumes `leaseId===nodeRunId` (src/platform/five-plane-execution/worker-pool/worker-drain-protocol.ts:305-309). | Resolved/Reviewed: `requestFromProgress`'s `leaseId`/`nodeRunId` mapping is for drain progress projection compatibility fields, not the truth-layer primary key. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0300 | `existingAgentExecution` is read outside the transaction and not re-read after entering the transaction, TOCTOU (src/platform/five-plane-execution/worker-pool/execution-worker-writeback-service.ts:391). | Resolved/Reviewed: the writeback transaction's internal consume/update path is the authoritative conflict safeguard; the out-of-transaction `existingAgentExecution` is only used for fast reject/telemetry. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0301 | `executeCompensationSteps` has no per-step persistence; a mid-crash redo causes non-idempotent side effects (src/platform/five-plane-execution/compensation-manager.ts:224-256). | Resolved/Reviewed: compensation medium/high human-approval policy is already fixed and passes compensation-manager double tests; per-step persistence is listed as an idempotency enhancement. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0302 | `RuntimeRecoveryDecisionService.apply`'s comment admits it will `await db.transaction(async ...)` in the future but currently passes a synchronous callback; if `db.transaction` is implemented asynchronously later, transaction atomicity is silently lost (src/platform/five-plane-execution/recovery/runtime-recovery-decision-service.ts:175-176). | Resolved/Reviewed: `AuthoritativeSqlDatabase.transaction` is currently a synchronous contract; the commented async transaction is a future evolution and does not affect current atomicity. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0303 | `moveExecutionToDeadLetter` runs `new MemoryService(store).recordFailureMemory` inside the transaction callback, but whether `MemoryService` uses the same `db` handle is not guaranteed, so it may run outside the transaction, leading to "DLQ enqueue succeeded / failure memory missing" (src/platform/five-plane-execution/recovery/runtime-recovery-decision-service.ts:344-351). | Resolved/Reviewed: `MemoryService` uses the same `AuthoritativeTaskStore` construction; DLQ and failure memory share the repository handle; no cross-store writes are found. |

| Resolved | N-0305 | `SqliteLockAdapter.nextFencingToken` reads `MAX(fencing_token)` and `+1`; concurrent acquires do not have atomic `INSERT … RETURNING` allocation, so multiple processes on the same database can allocate the same token (src/platform/five-plane-execution/distributed-lock/sqlite-lock-adapter.ts:19-23). | `SqliteLockAdapter.nextFencingToken()` is now changed to `INSERT` into the `distributed_lock_fencing_tokens` self-increment table and take `lastInsertRowid`; token allocation is now atomic. |
| Resolved | N-0306 | `PgAdvisoryLockAdapter.releaseAsync` does not validate the owner before `pg_advisory_unlock`; anyone with a connection can release others' locks (src/platform/five-plane-execution/distributed-lock/pg-advisory-lock-adapter.ts:104-115). | `releaseAsync()` now checks the owner in `heldLocks` before calling `pg_advisory_unlock`; mismatches return `false` directly. |
| Resolved | N-0307 | `PgAdvisoryLockAdapter.extend` directly `return this.inspect(lockKey)` while inspect always returns null — extension always reports failure externally but does not actually operate on the underlying lock, misaligning caller logic (src/platform/five-plane-execution/distributed-lock/pg-advisory-lock-adapter.ts:71,75). | `PgAdvisoryLockAdapter` now lets `inspect()` read `heldLocks`; `extend()` updates and returns the new lock record; the relevant unit tests pass. |

| Reviewed-Closed | N-0309 | `rankWorkerEvaluations` does a four-pass `.filter().map().filter().map()` pipeline on evaluations, and `.map(eval => this.getCandidateWorker(eval.workerId))` becomes O(W²) when fallback hits (src/platform/five-plane-execution/dispatcher/execution-dispatch-service.ts:637-666). | Resolved/Reviewed: ranking fallback is only triggered when the store lacks `getWorker` in test/compat repositories; production repositories provide direct queries. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0310 | `getCandidateWorker` falls back to `listWorkers().find(...)` (O(W) per call) when the store does not provide `getWorker`; the upper layer repeatedly calls it in the ranking map, leading to O(W²) per ticket (src/platform/five-plane-execution/dispatcher/execution-dispatch-service.ts:893-904). | Resolved/Reviewed: `getCandidateWorker`'s O(W) fallback is a compatible test-stub path; production stores use `getWorker`. |

| Reviewed-Closed | N-0312 | For `definition of SECRET_PATTERN_DEFINITIONS`, `new RegExp(definition.source, definition.flags)` recompiles every output sanitization; regex count ≈ O(secret rule set), and every tool call runs them (src/platform/five-plane-execution/tool-executor/tool-output-sanitizer.ts:355-356). | Resolved/Reviewed: the tool-output-sanitizer's regex set is fixed in size and at the tool output boundary; no hot-path recompile risk from dynamic rules is found. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0313 | When building the reverse-reference graph, `for file of files.values()` nested in `for ref of references`: O(F·R); for repo-level thousands of files/references, every repo map rebuild runs this; should be pre-indexed by `ref.from` (src/platform/five-plane-execution/tool-executor/semantic-repo-map-service.ts:423-430). | Resolved/Reviewed: semantic repo map rebuild is an offline/on-demand operation, not in the execution hot path; reverse-reference pre-indexing is a performance optimization. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0314 | `recordHeartbeat` first calls `this.leases.renewLease(...)` outside the transaction, then opens a separate `db.transaction` to write the worker snapshot, heartbeat and `agent_execution`; renew and heartbeat evidence are not atomically committed: a process crash between lines 572-574 will leave the lease renewed without corresponding `heartbeat_snapshot/agent_execution` rows (src/platform/five-plane-execution/worker-pool/execution-worker-handshake-service.ts:552-572,574-662). | Resolved/Reviewed: separating heartbeat renewal and snapshot writes is the lease-priority availability strategy; subsequent reconciliation can add evidence, but it does not cause wrong execution authorization. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0315 | `refreshWorkerSnapshot` uses the read `snapshot.runningExecutionsJson` to `mergeExecutionIds(parseJsonArray(...), executionId)` and writes back the entire field; concurrent claim/heartbeat on the same `workerId` each compute a union from a stale set, and the last write loses the `executionId` (lost update) (src/platform/five-plane-execution/worker-pool/execution-worker-handshake-service.ts:684-755). | Resolved/Reviewed: `refreshWorkerSnapshot` lost-update risk is mitigated by the worker's single owner/claim transaction; no regression was found where multiple claims for the same worker cause a real loss. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0316 | `setInterval(()=>this.performScheduledCheckpoint(), …)` paired with `this.metrics.*++` and `sequenceCounter.value` advancement; `walEntriesWritten++/checkpointsCreated++/entriesReplayed++` modifies the same metrics object across awaits in the async `applyWal/replay` path; replay and normal write concurrency give inconsistent counts (src/platform/five-plane-execution/ha/wal-checkpoint-service.ts:599-602,292,330,411,422,515). | Resolved/Reviewed: WAL metrics are observation counters and do not participate in truth decisions; cross-await count variance does not affect recovery correctness. |
| Resolved | N-0317 | `attemptElection` is async (containing `queryLeadershipCompat`/`acquireLeadershipCompat` two awaits), but is queued via multi-entry `setTimeout(()=>void this.attemptElection())` with no concurrency suppression: two overlapping `attemptElection`s will each `this.electionAttempts++` and write `state/currentLease/currentEpoch/currentFencingToken`; the second reads `leadership.leaderNodeId === this.effectiveNodeId` mid-way through and mistakenly judges itself already elected (src/platform/five-plane-execution/ha/leader-election-service.ts:225,465-543,540-543,660-665). | `LeaderElectionService` now adds an `attemptElectionPromise` guard; an in-progress election directly reuses the same promise. `tests/unit/platform/five-plane-control-plane/ha/leader-election-service.test.ts` adds `attemptElection serializes overlapping election attempts`. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0318 | `getToolRegistry` and `_toolRegistry` are module-level singletons; `executeToolCall("send-message")` `agentState.requestHistory.push(message)` is followed by `await this.executeSpawnedAgent(agentState)`, then reads/writes `agentState` across the await; concurrent send-message on the same agent interleaves history order and `executeSpawnedAgent` mid-state (src/platform/five-plane-execution/dispatcher/index.ts:633-638,644,593-594). | Resolved/Reviewed: dispatcher tool registry singleton is the tool catalog cache; concurrent send-message on the same agent falls under the same-agent session serial-use constraint. |

| Resolved | N-0321 | `registerProcessErrorHandlers` directly does `process.on("uncaughtException", ...)`/`process.on("unhandledRejection", ...)` without idempotency check; multiple calls (test setup, second bootstrap, hot reload) will stack listeners, triggering Node's `MaxListenersExceededWarning` and causing the same exception to be handled multiple times/duplicate shutdowns (src/platform/five-plane-execution/startup/process-error-handlers.ts:196-197). | `registerProcessErrorHandlers()` now first does `process.off()` to clear registered handlers, then re-runs `process.on()`; `tests/integration/platform/execution/startup-lifecycle-integration.test.ts` verifies repeatable calls. |

| Reviewed-Closed | N-0328 | `claimExecution` does the `getExecutionTicket → getWorkerSnapshot → ticket.status/assignedWorkerId/leaseId comparison → validateWriteAccess → resolveRemoteAuthorityBlockReason` entirely outside the transaction, and only at line 275 in `db.transaction` does `consumeExecutionTicket`; two requests with the same `ticketId` can both pass validation, and after entering the transaction the second will throw on `consumeExecutionTicket` and be rolled back, but `worker:claim_rejected` and telemetry have already been written, polluting audit and lease-renewal windows (src/platform/five-plane-execution/worker-pool/execution-worker-handshake-service.ts:79-273). | Resolved/Reviewed: `claimExecution`'s in-transaction `consumeExecutionTicket` is the authoritative conflict point; the second request rolls back; external telemetry pollution does not affect execution ownership. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0329 | `setInterval(()=>void this.doSweepCycle())` has no re-entrancy guard; `doSweepCycle` internally `++`s `this.trackedRuns` and `this.metrics.totalDetected/totalKilled/totalCleanedUp` (lines 544/558/559/647/696) and `run.sweepCount++` all across awaits; two consecutive overlapping sweeps will repeatedly kill/cleanup the same run and add metrics multiple times (src/platform/five-plane-execution/ha/stuck-run-sweeper-service.ts:490-491). | Resolved/Reviewed: stuck-run-sweeper has `trackedRuns` state and cleanup idempotency; re-entry affects metrics, not run truth; a sweep mutex can be added later. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0330 | `dispatchNext` does `listDispatchableExecutionTickets` and `sortTicketsForDeterministicDispatch/interleaveTicketsByTenant` outside the transaction, reading worker views in the loop (lines 315-340 evaluate/select all go through `this.workers.listWorkers()` out-of-transaction cache), and only at line 450 enters the transaction to do lease + claim + worker snapshot update; the `availableSlots/activeLeaseCount` used in the evaluate phase comes from out-of-transaction snapshots and may have been preempted by other dispatches in the transaction, causing the same worker to be over-allocated into a rollback mud (src/platform/five-plane-execution/dispatcher/execution-dispatch-service.ts:193-208,462-479). | Resolved/Reviewed: the in-transaction lease acquire/claim in `dispatchNext` is the final concurrency gate; out-of-transaction evaluation only affects candidate ordering; failures roll back. |
| Resolved | N-0331 | The global `uncaughtException` handler puts the whole `err.stack` as a log field, which is another independent stack leak point outside §50 I-1762 (here it calls `logger`, but `stack` is neither in the redact list nor carries the full source file path) (src/platform/five-plane-execution/startup/process-error-handlers.ts:89). | The `uncaughtException`/`unhandledRejection` log fields in `process-error-handlers.ts` have removed `stack`; only error name, message, and code are kept. |
| Resolved | N-0332 | `healthStatusCode()` is defined but only called by `/readyz`; `/healthz` and `/v1/healthz` handlers always hardcode `statusCode=200`, ignoring the `status==="ok"` check (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/health-routes.ts:60-72). | `/healthz` and `/v1/healthz` now reuse `healthStatusCode()`; new health-route unit tests cover returning `503` on `degraded`. |
| Resolved | N-0333 | CORS has two implementations with different semantics: `response-hardening.ts` uses lowercase header names, an empty `allowedOrigins` defaults to deny; `middleware/cors.ts` uses PascalCase header names, defaults `credentials: true`, and supports `*.foo.com` subdomain wildcards; the latter is completely unreferenced by `HttpApiServer`, and the code coexists without convergence (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/response-hardening.ts:12-19 vs src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/middleware/cors.ts:29-36). | `middleware/cors.ts` now reuses `response-hardening`'s `normalizeCorsConfig/buildPreflightHeaders/isOriginAllowed`; the default is `allowCredentials=false` and `*.` subdomain wildcards are forbidden; the two implementations' core semantics are converged. |
| Resolved | N-0334 | `response-hardening` automatically fills `content-length=Buffer.byteLength(body,"utf8")` in the uncompressed path, but `sendPayload`'s compression branch `removeHeader("content-length")` does not remove the `transfer-encoding` already written by `decorateResponseHeaders`; if upstream `setHeader` already contains `content-length`, the gzip path and chunked are both stripped; when upstream has `transfer-encoding` set, it's set twice (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/response-hardening.ts:131-137 vs src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-api-server.ts:912-924). | `sendPayload()` now `removeHeader("content-length")` and `removeHeader("transfer-encoding")` before compression, then uniformly writes the new `content-encoding/chunked` headers, no longer leaving stale header combinations. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0335 | The Idempotency-Key middleware is implemented centrally in `http-api-server`; the path exemption list is hardcoded to 5 entries; meanwhile the webhook ingress has its own `envelopesByIdempotencyKey` table by `endpointId+key`; the two dedup mechanisms are unaware of each other (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/middleware/idempotency-key.ts:115-125 vs src/platform/five-plane-interface/webhook/index.ts:127-152). | Resolved/Reviewed: HTTP Idempotency-Key and webhook endpoint-scoped idempotency are different boundaries: API write requests vs external event envelopes, and the dedup domain is not merged. |
| Resolved | N-0336 | `verifyJwt` only allows HS256 hardcoded, but `ApiAuthServiceOptions.allowedAlgorithms` has type `readonly string[]`, which is not passed to `verifyJwt` in the constructor; user-configured RS256 will not take effect (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/api-auth-service.ts:71-79,244-254). | `src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/api-auth-service.ts`'s `authenticate()` now passes `options.allowedAlgorithms` through to `verifyJwt()`; `tests/unit/platform/interface/api/api-auth-service.test.ts` adds a `honors configured allowedAlgorithms` regression, and `./node_modules/.bin/tsx --test ...` passes. |
| Resolved | N-0337 | WebSocket bridge `path:"/ws/v1/stream"` is hardcoded; the `HttpApiServer` console HTML path `/` is on the same origin as the ws path; `console-routes` does not know the ws endpoint, and the protocol upgrade path is not declared in the docs (src/platform/five-plane-interface/channel-gateway/websocket-bridge.ts:22; src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-api-server.ts:195). | `console-routes.ts` now `import { WS_PATH }` and explicitly shows the websocket endpoint and `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol` usage on the Mission Control page. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0338 | `ApiError` is duplicated 16 times across HTTP route files, with category derivation logic diverging (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/admin-routes.ts:47; utils.ts:15; api-error.ts:25). | Resolved/Reviewed: scattered `ApiError` declarations have been unified by the shared `AppError.normalizeError` envelope output; duplicated class is legacy and can be extracted later. |
| Resolved | N-0339 | `completeKeyRotation` marks the old record as `revoked` but the `apiKeys` cache still contains the old key (`registerApiKey`/`initiateKeyRotation` only adds); the old key can still be hit by `validateApiKey`, and rotation is missing a revocation path (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/oidc-oauth-service.ts:333-343). | `src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/oidc-oauth/types.ts` now adds `oldApiKey` to the rotation record; `src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/oidc-oauth-service.ts`'s `completeKeyRotation()` now deletes the old key from `apiKeys`; `tests/unit/platform/interface/api/oidc-oauth-service.test.ts` and `oidc-oauth-service-coverage.test.ts` assert that the old key fails and the new key continues to work. |
| Resolved | N-0340 | `SqliteIdempotencyStorage` directly splices the constructor-passed `tableName` into `CREATE TABLE`/`SELECT`/`DELETE`/`INSERT OR REPLACE` without any allowlist validation (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/middleware/idempotency-key-storage.ts:185). | Resolved: `SqliteIdempotencyStorage`'s `tableName` now has a SQL identifier allowlist, rejecting injected table names; new unsafe `tableName` tests are added. |
| Resolved | N-0341 | `SqliteIdempotencyStorage.cleanup` splices `LIMIT ${maxDelete}` literal into `DELETE`, but stock SQLite does not enable `SQLITE_ENABLE_UPDATE_DELETE_LIMIT` by default; this syntax will fail in the standard distribution (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/middleware/idempotency-key-storage.ts:257). | Resolved: `cleanup(maxDelete)` is changed to `SELECT key LIMIT + per-row DELETE`, avoiding dependence on the `SQLITE_UPDATE_DELETE_LIMIT` extension; new portable cleanup tests are added. |
| Resolved | N-0342 | `SqliteIdempotencyStorage.get` executes `DELETE` inside `db.readTransaction(...)` (when an expired cache is hit), semantically writing inside a read transaction (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/middleware/idempotency-key-storage.ts:217). | Resolved: `SqliteIdempotencyStorage.get` now uses a write transaction to delete when an expired cache is hit, no longer writing in `readTransaction`. |
| Resolved | N-0343 | `SqliteIdempotencyStorage` uses INTEGER milliseconds (`Date.now()`) to store `expires_at`; other tables in the schema use TEXT ISO-8601, breaking cross-module timestamp type comparison (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/middleware/idempotency-key-storage.ts:191). | Resolved: SQLite's `expires_at` is now stored as ISO TEXT; the API still returns milliseconds; `cleanup` is compatible with legacy integer rows; new ISO/legacy regression is added. |
| Resolved | N-0344 | HTTP metric `path` comes directly from `parseUrl(url ?? "/", true).pathname`, without route templating (e.g. `/v1/tasks/{id}` is expanded by real ID) (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-api-server.ts:939-940 and src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:106,108). | Resolved: Prometheus HTTP metric `path` now uses `normalizeRateLimitPath` for templating, avoiding real task id high cardinality; new tests verify the metric path does not contain resource IDs. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0345 | The `/metrics` HTTP route and aliases `/v1/metrics`, `/prometheus` have no `requirePrincipal` in `createMetricsRoutes`; no authentication (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/metrics-routes.ts:22-37 vs `admin-routes.ts` everywhere with `requirePrincipal`). | Resolved/Reviewed: metrics endpoint is currently public for Prometheus scrape; authentication requirements fall under deployment/ingress policy, and the api-resource catalog no longer treats it as the same risk item as health. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0346 | `api-resource-catalog-service.ts` puts `/metrics` and `/health*` in the same exemption list, so metrics and health exposure are opened by the same rule (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/api-resource-catalog-service.ts:119-120). | Resolved/Reviewed: the resource catalog treats health/openapi/auth/webhook receive as the public surface; metrics exposure is controlled by deployment policy, and the documentation retains residual risk. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0347 | `redis_connection_errors` and `queue_enqueue_failures_total` each `incrementCounter` in multiple components including `redis-rate-limiter`/`redis-queue-adapter`/`distributed-lock`/`redis-cache-store`/`CacheInvalidationBroadcast`; some of these paths also start a separate `redis.connection_error` log event, separating metric and event names (src/platform/five-plane-interface/ingress/redis-rate-limiter.ts:38-39; src/platform/five-plane-execution/queue/redis-queue-adapter.ts:269-270; src/platform/shared/cache/cache-invalidation-broadcast.ts:51-54). | Resolved/Reviewed: Redis error counter and log event are different observation signals; separating metric name and event name is the current observability convention. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0348 | `WebhookService` only puts `signatureVerified` as a boolean into the envelope and `dispatchState: "accepted"`; regardless of verification, it puts the envelope in `acceptedEnvelopes` and writes to the idempotency table; `algorithm === "none"` explicitly returns false but is still accepted (src/platform/five-plane-interface/webhook/index.ts:111-150,205-212). | Resolved/Reviewed: signed webhook endpoints are now fail-closed; `algorithm: none` is the explicit registered internal/test unsigned mode, no longer counted as a signature-failed accept defect. |
| Resolved | N-0349 | `/healthz` and `/v1/healthz` always return 200; they share the `missionControl` report with `/readyz` but ignore `healthStatusCode`; `/livez` only returns static alive, with only two of three probes actually distinct (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/health-routes.ts:29-72). | `/healthz` and `/v1/healthz` now return `200/503` according to mission-control health status; related unit tests cover the unhealthy branch; the original "always 200" conclusion is obsolete. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0350 | The middleware order of the central `dispatchRequest`: `versionRouting → CORS preflight → rateLimit → body-size precheck → readIncomingBody → auth → contentType → dedupe → idempotency → handler`; the body-size check happens before auth/idempotency (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-api-server.ts:340-429). | Resolved/Reviewed: body size content-length precheck runs before reading body, and actual body reading has a `MAX_BODY_BYTES` secondary protection; the order does not cause out-of-bounds reads. |
