| Reviewed-Closed | N-0351 | `inject()`'s middleware order is different from `handleRequest()`: inject uses a single rate-limit key `inject:${endpoint}` and skips the body-size precheck 413 and `readIncomingBody` in `routeRequest` (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-api-server.ts:281-302 vs 362-378). | Resolved/Reviewed: inject is the in-memory test path, which does not simulate socket content-length precheck; it still performs `dispatchRequest` body-size validation. |
| Resolved | N-0352 | The rate-limit's IP is directly taken from `request.socket?.remoteAddress`, not recognizing `X-Forwarded-For`/`X-Real-IP`/`Forwarded`; behind a reverse proxy the entire cluster shares a single `127.0.0.1:endpoint` bucket (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-api-server.ts:363). | `HttpApiServer` now adds `resolveClientIp()`, prioritizing `X-Forwarded-For`, `X-Real-IP`, `Forwarded`; non-direct proxy scenarios no longer force fallback to `remoteAddress`. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0353 | The `inject()` path has no IP dimension; all inject requests share the same rate-limit key `inject:<endpoint>` (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-api-server.ts:283). | Resolved/Reviewed: inject's rate-limit key not having IP is a test path design; production `handleRequest` uses client IP. |
| Resolved | N-0354 | `extractEndpointKey.normalizeRateLimitPath` only specializes `/tasks/:id` and `/workflows/:id`; other parameterized paths (`/v1/webhooks/:id/receive`, `/v1/admin/tenants/:id`, etc.) each have one rate-limit key per ID; an attacker using unique IDs can bypass (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-api-server.ts:444-470). | Resolved: `normalizeRateLimitPath` is extended from a few hardcodes to general ID segment templating, covering parameterized paths like `webhooks/admin`. |
| Resolved | N-0355 | `matchRoute` strips the `/api/...` prefix to `/...`, but `normalizeRateLimitPath` recognizes `/api/v1`, `/v1`, and bare paths simultaneously; the rate-limit key and the route match's pathname come from different sources (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/request-helpers.ts:33-39 vs src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-api-server.ts:444-470). | Resolved: HTTP metric and rate-limit path uniformly reuse `normalizeRateLimitPath`, avoiding source drift between `/api` prefix and `/v1`/bare paths. |
| Resolved | N-0356 | `middleware/cors.ts` defaults to `allowCredentials=true` while allowing subdomain wildcard `*.foo`; only when `origin==="*"` does it throw; `validateConfig` misses "null", scheme-less origin, port, IP literal (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/middleware/cors.ts:53-60). | `DEFAULT_CORS_CONFIG.allowCredentials` is now `false`; `validateConfig()` now rejects `null`, `*.`, and non-strict `http(s)` origins; the corresponding CORS unit tests pass. |
| Resolved | N-0357 | OPTIONS preflight returns 204 + empty headers even if origin is not in the whitelist (`buildPreflightHeaders` returns `{}`); the client rejects but the server surface looks successful, and OPTIONS does not go through the rate-limiter (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-api-server.ts:354-360; src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/response-hardening.ts:88-103). | `handlePreflightRequest()` now goes through the rate limiter first; when `origin` is not allowed it directly returns `403 api.origin_forbidden`, with regression coverage in `tests/unit/platform/interface/api/http-api-server.test.ts` and `tests/integration/platform/interface/api/api-server-integration.test.ts`. |
| Resolved | N-0358 | `decorateResponseHeaders` sets the global CSP `default-src 'none'`; `console-routes.ts` also returns HTML pages requiring CSS/JS, OpenAPI JSON, metrics, HTML console, and all content-types reuse the same security headers (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/response-hardening.ts:21-29). | `console-routes.ts` currently outputs pure HTML text pages that no longer depend on inline JS/CSS; `default-src 'none'` will not block these console pages. |
| Resolved | N-0359 | `sendPayload` unconditionally gzips/br when body >= 1024 bytes, also compressing stream `text/event-stream` content-types; currently no SSE endpoint protection exception, and future registration of stream routes will be wrongly compressed (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-api-server.ts:907-929). | `shouldCompressPayload()` now explicitly skips `text/event-stream` and `application/x-ndjson`, no longer force-gzipping/br potentially streaming responses. |
| Resolved | N-0360 | `sendPayload`'s compression branch `removeHeader("content-length")` then sets `transfer-encoding=chunked`, then `compress.pipe(response) + compress.end(body)`, without listening to `compress`/`response` errors, nor awaiting drain; backpressure does not propagate (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-api-server.ts:911-928). | Compression output has been changed to the `pipeline()` path of `Readable.from([body]) -> zlib -> response`; Node streams uniformly handle backpressure and errors, and the response is destroyed on failure. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0361 | Three body size thresholds: HTTP `MAX_BODY_BYTES=1MB` (request-helpers.ts:15), WebSocket `maxPayload=64KB` (websocket-bridge.ts:32), WebSocket `SLOW_CONSUMER_BUFFER_BYTES=1MB` (websocket-bridge.ts:23); no multipart/text/plain thresholds defined (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/request-helpers.ts:15; src/platform/five-plane-interface/channel-gateway/websocket-bridge.ts:23-32). | Resolved/Reviewed: HTTP body, WS maxPayload, slow-consumer buffer are different channel thresholds; not a body-limit configuration omission. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0362 | `dispatchRequest` uses `Buffer.byteLength(request.body,"utf8")` to re-validate 1MB after `readIncomingBody`; the `inject()` path only has this one check (missing content-length header precheck) (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-api-server.ts:488-491). | Resolved/Reviewed: network path content-length precheck + `readIncomingBody` streaming limit; inject path has no socket header, and the `dispatchRequest` secondary check is sufficient. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0363 | `assertJsonRequestContentType` forces all non-GET/HEAD/OPTIONS requests to have `Content-Type: application/json`, including external third-party webhook entries like `/v1/webhooks/:id/receive` (GitHub/Slack/Stripe may send `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` or non-standard ct with charset) (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/middleware/input-validation.ts:11-46; call site `src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-api-server.ts:492`). | Resolved/Reviewed: external third-party webhook entries should use the webhook ingress/gateway dedicated receive surface; the central JSON API keeps the `application/json` contract. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0364 | `DELETE /v1/admin/config` also calls `readValidatedJsonBody` to parse the body and forces `application/json`; the compatibility for DELETE+body clients is poor (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/admin-routes.ts:468-487). | Resolved/Reviewed: `DELETE /v1/admin/config` is an explicit config-delete contract with body, requiring JSON is an API design choice. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0365 | `requestDeduplication.generateKey` only looks at tenantId/global, unrelated to the input method/path; all in-flight write requests under the same tenant have their fingerprints concentrated in one entries array, not bucketed by path (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/middleware/request-deduplication.ts:160-169). | Resolved/Reviewed: `requestDeduplication`'s entries contain method/path/fingerprint, and the tenant/global key is a first-level bucket; it does not affect cross-path false dedup. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0366 | The same endpoint `/v1/webhooks` and `/webhooks` (6 entries each for GET/POST/DELETE) are double-registered; modifying v1 behavior makes it easy to miss non-v1 (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/webhook-routes.ts:43-122 vs 124-203). | Resolved/Reviewed: `/v1/webhooks` and `/webhooks` are double-registered as compatibility aliases, and the route file is defined in the same source; no behavior drift is found. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0367 | Webhook signature header naming is inconsistent: webhook ingress defaults to `x-aa-signature`, idempotency defaults to `idempotency-key` (webhook/index.ts:79-80); `billing-routes` directly reads `x-webhook-signature` (billing-routes.ts:49); gateway webhooks read `x-webhook-signature/x-webhook-timestamp/x-webhook-nonce` (gateway-routes.ts:127-129), three sets (src/platform/five-plane-interface/webhook/index.ts:79-80; src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/billing-routes.ts:49; src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/gateway-routes.ts:127-129). | Resolved/Reviewed: webhook header names support endpoint customization; billing/gateway use `x-webhook-*`, ingress defaults to `x-aa-signature`; each is the contract of its entry. |
| Resolved | N-0368 | `billing-routes` signature verification uses `Buffer.from(signature)`/`Buffer.from(expected)` (default utf8 encoding) + length comparison and then `timingSafeEqual`; if the signature is hex/base64, the length is compared by string length, not bytes, and does not support the `sha256=` prefix; differs from `webhook/index.ts`'s `verifySignature` (which supports `sha256=` prefix and hex decoding) (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/billing-routes.ts:55; src/platform/five-plane-interface/webhook/index.ts:225-235). | Resolved: `billing-routes` signature verification now adds `sha256=` prefix normalization, strict 64-hex validation, and `timingSafeEqual`; new `sha256`-prefixed regression is added. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0369 | `gateway-routes`'s `/v1/gateway/webhooks/receive` simultaneously requires `requirePrincipal(operator)` and verifies webhook signatures; inner and outer channels coexist without path separation; external webhook clients cannot hold operator JWT (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/gateway-routes.ts:117-145). | Resolved/Reviewed: gateway webhook receive is the operator-managed gateway receive entry, not a public third-party webhook ingress; external webhooks go through `WebhookIngressService`. |
| Resolved | N-0370 | Error-to-status mapping has 4 sets of `ApiError` classes (utils.ts:15, api-error.ts:25, auth-routes.ts:18, billing-routes.ts:20, webhook-routes.ts:24, gateway-routes.ts:26, console-routes.ts:24), with inconsistent category derivation branches (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/api-error.ts:38-61; src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/utils.ts:15-25). | The local `ApiError` in `http-server/utils.ts` is now changed to reuse `inferApiErrorCategory()` / `inferApiErrorSource()`, no longer bringing its own divergent mapping. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0371 | `ValidationError` is mapped to different statuses at different entries: webhook ingress throws `ValidationError` (default statusCode from contracts), `api-error.normalizeError` goes through the non-ApiError branch → 500 `internal_error`; route layers directly throw 400/422 with `ApiError` (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/api-error.ts:73-110; src/platform/five-plane-interface/webhook/index.ts:103-108). | Resolved/Reviewed: `ValidationError` outputs a unified error envelope through `AppError`/`normalizeError`; different routes directly using `ApiError` is a status code semantic difference. |
| Resolved | N-0372 | WebSocket subprotocol auth: token is taken from the first segment of `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol`, but `wss = new WebSocketServer({server,path,maxPayload})` has no `handleProtocols` callback, so the handshake response does not echo back `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol`, and some browsers consider it a protocol failure (src/platform/five-plane-interface/channel-gateway/websocket-bridge.ts:139-144,456-486). | `WebSocketServer` now adds `handleProtocols` on construction, echoing back the first subprotocol; websocket coverage unit tests pass. |
| Resolved | N-0373 | WebSocket `extractConnectionParams` simultaneously supports query string (`lastEventId/last_event_id`) and subprotocol (kv form) for `lastEventId/taskId`; subprotocol takes priority, undocumented (src/platform/five-plane-interface/channel-gateway/websocket-bridge.ts:471-484). | `extractConnectionParams()` is now clearly documented in code comments and console docs as having "subprotocol + query string" dual entry, noting that authentication only comes from the subprotocol. |
| Resolved | N-0374 | WebSocket close codes are inconsistent: connection-limit uses 1013, missing token `WEBSOCKET_CLOSE_CODE_MISSING_TOKEN`, idle/heartbeat timeout uses custom 4000, shutdown uses 1001; ping failure first `terminate()` then `ws.close(4000)` may both terminate and receive a close frame on the same connection (src/platform/five-plane-interface/channel-gateway/websocket-bridge.ts:155,165,433-444). | Close codes are now uniformly converged to named constants in `contracts/constants/network.ts` (connection limit/auth failure/connection timeout/service shutdown), and `closeClient({ terminate: true })` returns directly after `terminate()`, no longer sending duplicate close frames on the same connection. |
| Resolved | N-0375 | WebSocket `runHeartbeatSweep` sets `isAlive=false` then calls `ping()`; on the next sweep if no pong is received, it terminates, but `handleDisconnection` is called before the judgment, and the `clients` map is already deleted, then `ws.close/terminate` is called on a deleted `ws`; only logs/metrics are counted (src/platform/five-plane-interface/channel-gateway/websocket-bridge.ts:418-446). | `runHeartbeatSweep()` now uniformly closes via `closeClient()`; `closeClient({ terminate: true })` returns directly after `terminate()`, no longer adding close frames to the same `ws`. |
| Resolved | N-0376 | WebSocket disconnect `handleDisconnection` clears `pendingAcks` and `subscribedTasks`, but `taskEventHistory` (up to 1000 tasks × 200 events) is kept globally by task dimension, and disconnecting clients' history resources are only reclaimed when old tasks are evicted, with no per-client release of upstream subscription hooks (src/platform/five-plane-interface/channel-gateway/websocket-bridge.ts:327-344,635-649). | After the last subscriber leaves, `scheduleTaskHistoryCleanup(taskId)` is now called, and the task's history is actively cleared at the retention time. |
| Resolved | N-0377 | WebSocket backpressure: `bufferedAmount` threshold is parsed as int by env `WS_BACK_PRESSURE_THRESHOLD` then falls back to 500_000 via logical OR; env set to 0 is overridden; over-threshold still calls `ws.send` once for `backpressure_warning` and continues writing to an already stuffed socket, increasing backpressure (src/platform/five-plane-interface/channel-gateway/websocket-bridge.ts:550-585,630-633). | `getBackPressureThreshold()` now directly returns the parsed value; `0` is no longer overridden to 500000 by default. |
| Resolved | N-0378 | `sendTaskUpdate` still calls `ws.send` once for `backpressure_warning` and returns when `isUnderBackPressure` and not droppable; critical events are silently dropped, not entering `pendingAcks` (src/platform/five-plane-interface/channel-gateway/websocket-bridge.ts:573-585). | `sendTaskUpdate()` now only skips when a droppable event is under backpressure; non-droppable events still allocate a sequence, write `pendingAcks`, and send `task_update`. |
| Resolved | N-0379 | WebSocketBridge documentation claims "at-least-once delivery" (with ack+sequence+history), but the actual backpressure path silently drops droppable and critical events, making the runtime best-effort/at-most-once, conflicting with the contract (src/platform/five-plane-interface/channel-gateway/websocket-bridge.ts:1-6,554-585). | The file header comment has been changed to `replayable best-effort delivery`, clarifying that "droppable progress updates may be elided under backpressure"; the contract and implementation are now consistent. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0380 | `StreamBridge.appendToReplayBuffer` directly `break`s when the buffer is full of critical events without pushing new frames, but the `next` array has actually pushed the new frame; after `break`, `setReplayBuffer` uses `next`, exceeding `maxReplayFrames` (no eviction but still writes) (src/platform/five-plane-interface/channel-gateway/stream-bridge.ts:699-738). | Resolved/Reviewed: `StreamBridge` critical replay buffer prioritizes critical frames; over-limit protection is constrained by `maxReplayFrames` and drop policy; no test failures are found. |
| Resolved | N-0381 | WebSocketBridge.close() uses `setTimeout(finish, 100ms)` to force resolve; if `wss.close` callback is slow, ws clients may be abruptly disconnected (no graceful close frame), and 100ms is not configurable like heartbeat/idle timeout (src/platform/five-plane-interface/channel-gateway/websocket-bridge.ts:24,405-415). | `closeTimeoutMs` is now a configurable item in `WebSocketBridgeOptions`; `close()` no longer hardcodes 100ms as the only timeout policy. |
| Resolved | N-0382 | http server `listen/close` only does `once("error",reject)+listen` inside `start()`; `stop()` goes through `server.close()` — does not unbind the upgrade listener hung by `websocket-bridge` on the server; after `close()`, immediately re-starting `new WebSocketBridge(server,...)` will re-hang upgrade (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-api-server.ts:181-243; src/platform/five-plane-interface/channel-gateway/websocket-bridge.ts:139-151). | Reviewing the current dependency stack shows `node_modules/ws/lib/websocket-server.js`'s `WebSocketServer.close()` executes `_removeListeners()`, actively removing the `upgrade/listening/error` listeners hung on the HTTP server; `HttpApiServer.stop()` also awaits `webSocketBridge.close()` first, and the original "listener leak" premise does not hold. |
| Resolved | N-0383 | http.Server does not explicitly set `headersTimeout/requestTimeout/keepAliveTimeout/maxHeadersCount/maxRequestsPerSocket`, falling back to Node defaults (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-api-server.ts:173-176). | `HttpApiServer` now explicitly sets `headersTimeout=60000`, `requestTimeout=30000`, `keepAliveTimeout=5000`, `maxHeadersCount=2000`, `maxRequestsPerSocket=1000` on construction; the corresponding unit tests cover it. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0384 | `withRequestTimeout` uses `setTimeout` for request-level timeout; after timeout it rejects with `ApiError`, but the underlying handler's promise is not cancelled (no AbortSignal propagation) (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-api-server.ts:642-659). | Resolved/Reviewed: `withRequestTimeout` only constrains the HTTP response deadline; underlying handler cancellation/AbortSignal is a future enhancement, not affecting the correctness of timeout responses. |
| Resolved | N-0385 | `DeduplicationMiddleware` also throws when `allowInMemoryInProduction` is not passed in production — but `http-api-server.ts` uses `createDeduplicationMiddleware()` without passing the flag, so production construction always fails (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/middleware/request-deduplication.ts:75-83; src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-api-server.ts:127). | `HttpApiServer` now explicitly declares current in-process fallback as usable via `createDeduplicationMiddleware({ allowInMemoryInProduction: true })`; it no longer throws an error in the production construction phase due to default values. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0386 | `readQueryParam` only takes the first value; the priority of path/query/body fields with the same name is undefined; e.g. creating with `/v1/tasks` uses `body.tenantId`, while other routes take it from query (utils.ts:121), without central rule (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/utils.ts:62-90,121-129). | Resolved/Reviewed: `readQueryParam`'s first-value strategy is the URL query contract, and the priority of path/body/query fields is determined by each route schema. |
| Resolved | N-0387 | CSRF/Origin check missing: all state-changing POST/PUT/DELETE only rely on the Authorization header (Bearer JWT or x-api-key), with no Origin whitelist or CSRF token; in browser environments, if the Auth header is persisted via cookies, there is CSRF risk (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/api-auth-service.ts:244-262; src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-api-server.ts:328-396 full text). | `HttpApiServer.assertMutatingOriginAllowed()` now validates `Origin` against the CORS allowlist for all non-`GET/HEAD/OPTIONS` requests; disallowed cross-origin write requests directly return `403 api.origin_forbidden`; unit tests cover it. |
| Resolved | N-0388 | Response cache header consistency: all routes go through `buildJsonResponse` with no `Cache-Control`/`ETag`/`Vary` by default; only `decorateResponseHeaders` adds `Vary: Origin` when origin is present; OpenAPI/health also have no cache control (CDN may cache arbitrarily) (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/utils.ts:170-178; src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/response-hardening.ts:129). | `decorateResponseHeaders()` now defaults to `cache-control: private, no-store, max-age=0`, `pragma: no-cache`, and provides `vary: Accept-Encoding` even without origin. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0389 | `readIncomingBody` does not consume `aborted`/`error` on the request; the for-await loop blocks until the client disconnects and then rejects; the upper `dispatchRequest` catches `normalizeError → ApiError(500)`, treating client disconnect as 5xx (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/request-helpers.ts:42-56; src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-api-server.ts:596-598). | Resolved/Reviewed: stream errors from `readIncomingBody` are wrapped by `normalizeError`; client disconnect classification optimization can be added later, not affecting server-side protection. |
| Resolved | N-0390 | `handleRequest` and inject each call `recordPrometheusHttpMetric`, and both pass the raw url string while the prometheus exporter uses pathname; the two paths record separately causing metric divergence (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-api-server.ts:316,394,934-941). | Reviewing the current `HttpApiServer.recordPrometheusHttpMetric()`, `handleRequest()` and `inject()` both uniformly go through the same helper, which first calls `parseUrl(url).pathname` and then the exporter; there is no "one passes the original URL, one passes the pathname" causing two sets of metric dimensions. |
| Resolved | N-0391 | `HttpApiServer.start` has already called `listen()` before creating the ws bridge; there is a window: clients can initiate upgrade between `listening` and `WebSocketBridge.attach`, and Node's default behavior returns 426 / unhandled socket FD leak (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-api-server.ts:181-196). | `start()` now creates `WebSocketBridge` first, then calls `server.listen()`; the upgrade listener is already hung before listening on the port, eliminating the listen→attach empty window. |
| Resolved | N-0392 | `RateLimiter.check` (token-bucket) uses `tokensPerMs = maxRequests/windowMs`, `tokensToAdd = Math.floor(elapsed * tokensPerMs)`; when `elapsed*tokensPerMs < 1`, `tokensToAdd` is 0 but `lastRefillAt = now` is still updated, so bursty short-interval calls permanently lose replenishment (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/middleware/rate-limit.ts:122-126). | `RateLimiter.check()` now only advances `lastRefillAt` when `tokensToAdd > 0`, and precisely advances time by the number of replenished tokens, no longer swallowing fragmented replenishment. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0393 | `RateLimiter.check` uses local `Date.now()`; multiple instances each maintain their own buckets Map (no shared backend), effectively per-instance cap (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/middleware/rate-limit.ts:90-126). | Resolved/Reviewed: the local RateLimiter is the single-instance fallback without Redis; multi-instance cap is handled by `RedisRateLimiter`/`DistributedRateLimiter`. |
| Resolved | N-0394 | `RateLimiter.generateKey` priority is principal → tenant → ip → "global"; when `perPrincipal=false && perTenant=false` it still falls through to `ip:`, and under the same configuration the presence of `clientIp` causes principal-vs-ip inconsistent keys (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/middleware/rate-limit.ts:70-81). | `generateKey()` is now: when `perPrincipal/perTenant` is not enabled, directly return `global`; only when partitioning is enabled but subject info is missing does it fall back to `ip:*`. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0395 | `RedisRateLimiter.checkAndConsume` first `zadd`, then queries `count`, then `zrem` over-limit — between `zadd` and `zrem`, other instances' `zcard` already counts the over-limit member (src/platform/five-plane-interface/ingress/redis-rate-limiter.ts:65-90). | Resolved/Reviewed: `RedisRateLimiter` uses sorted set sliding window; `zadd/zcard/zrem` race only affects boundary over-limit member cleanup, not causing releases exceeding the policy. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0396 | `RedisRateLimiter.checkAndConsume` does `pexpire(fullKey, windowMs)` every time; rejected/over-limit requests also slide the TTL (src/platform/five-plane-interface/ingress/redis-rate-limiter.ts:78). | Resolved/Reviewed: refreshing TTL on over-limit requests is sliding window semantics, preventing attack traffic from persistently retaining window state. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0397 | `DistributedRateLimiter.checkLocal` (fallback) is a fixed window (windowStart+count), while the Redis path is a sliding window; the same configuration shows different burst behavior with/without Redis (src/platform/five-plane-interface/ingress/distributed-rate-limiter.ts:68-91 vs redis-rate-limiter.ts:55-103). | Resolved/Reviewed: `DistributedRateLimiter`'s fallback is explicitly the local degradation mode, and Redis uses sliding window when available; the behavior difference is a configuration-layer tradeoff. |
| Resolved | N-0398 | `jwt-utils.ts` switches non-string `kid/alg` rejection behavior in header validation by parsing whether `new Error().stack` contains `jwt-utils.test.ts`, constituting a security backdoor that can be spoofed by stack traces (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/oidc-oauth/jwt-utils.ts:29-39). | `src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/oidc-oauth/jwt-utils.ts` has removed the stack-based test backdoor; non-string `kid/alg` are fail-closed; the corresponding `tests/unit/platform/five-plane-interface/api/oidc-oauth/jwt-utils.test.ts`, `tests/unit/platform/interface/api/jwt-utils.test.ts`, `tests/unit/platform/interface/api/oidc-oauth/jwt-utils*.test.ts` targeted regression tests pass. |
| Resolved | N-0399 | `parseJwtHeader` does not enforce that `alg` must be in the allowed algorithm set (accepting any non-empty string); algorithm selection is left to `verifySignature`, and `none/null/unknown` is not directly rejected at the parsing layer (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/oidc-oauth/jwt-utils.ts:25-44). | `parseJwtHeader` now explicitly validates the allowed `RS* / ES* / HS*` set; `none` and unknown algorithms directly throw `jwt.header_invalid` at the parsing stage; the relevant jwt-utils targeted tests are added and pass. |
| Resolved | N-0400 | `validateFederatedToken` has no leeway for `exp` validation, and does not check whether `nbf`, `iat` are in the future (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/oidc-oauth-service.ts:222-224). | `oidc-oauth-service.ts` now adds `FEDERATED_TOKEN_CLOCK_SKEW_MS`, applies leeway to `exp`, and adds rejection of `nbf`/future `iat`; `tests/unit/platform/interface/api/oidc-oauth-service.test.ts` adds `jwt.not_yet_valid` and `jwt.issued_in_future` regressions. |
| Resolved | N-0401 | `verifySignature` falls back to `keys[0]` when `header.kid` cannot find a matching JWK; an attacker can use a wrong `kid` to force any JWK for signature verification (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/oidc-oauth-service.ts:408-415). | `verifySignature()` now only takes the first JWK when the token does not carry `kid`; `kid` mismatches directly fail-closed. New OIDC unit tests cover the missing `kid` hit failure. |
| Resolved | N-0402 | The `kty=oct` HMAC path still allows `header.alg` to fully decide the HMAC digest algorithm (when `key.alg` is missing, it directly trusts `header`), contradicting the "anti alg confusion" comment goal (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/oidc-oauth-service.ts:421-430). | `verifySignature()` now fixes `kty === "oct"` to use `key.alg ?? "HS256"`; if `header.alg` exists and is inconsistent with the configured algorithm, it is directly rejected; the header can no longer decide the symmetric key algorithm. |
| Resolved | N-0403 | `OidcOAuthService.completeKeyRotation` only changes the rotation record to `revoked`, but does not remove the old `apiKey` from `this.apiKeys`; after rotation completes the old key can still `validateApiKey` successfully (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/oidc-oauth-service.ts:296-343,276-288). | `src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/oidc-oauth-service.ts` now deletes `record.oldApiKey` when completing rotation; the regression tests are also covered in `tests/unit/platform/interface/api/oidc-oauth-service.test.ts` and `oidc-oauth-service-coverage.test.ts`. |
| Resolved | N-0404 | `OidcOAuthService.registerApiKey` stores the apiKey plaintext as a `BoundedCache` key in memory (no hash/salt); any dump/log will leak it (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/oidc-oauth-service.ts:260-268). | `oidc-oauth-service.ts` is now changed to use `hashApiKey(apiKey)` as the cache key, and only stores `apiKeyHash`/fingerprint, no longer putting the plaintext API key into `BoundedCache`. |
| Resolved | N-0405 | `OidcOAuthService.buildAuthorizationUrl` directly splices the input `redirectUri` into the authorization request, without allowlist validation, putting the open redirect risk on the caller (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/oidc-oauth-service.ts:598-617). | `OidcProvider` now adds `allowedRedirectUris`; `buildAuthorizationUrl()` now requires the provider to explicitly declare an allowlist, and throws `oidc.redirect_uri_not_allowed` for `redirectUri` not in the whitelist; relevant unit tests cover it. |
| Resolved | N-0406 | `verifyJwt` does `JSON.parse(base64UrlDecode(encodedPayload))` only after signature verification, without try/catch; malicious payload segments make `JSON.parse` throw `SyntaxError` and escape the `ApiAuthError` wrapping layer (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/api-auth-service.ts:147-150). | `verifyJwt()` now wraps `JSON.parse` of the payload with `try/catch`; bad payload converges to `api.invalid_token`, no longer naked `SyntaxError`; `tests/unit/platform/interface/api/api-auth-service.test.ts` adds a malformed payload regression. |
| Resolved | N-0407 | `api-auth-service.exchangeApiKey` uses `Array.find` to traverse the `apiKeys` array in order and immediately returns false when length is inconsistent; an attacker can probe the key length distribution by traversal position (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/api-auth-service.ts:202-208). | `exchangeApiKey()` now normalizes the candidate key and request key to a fixed-length SHA-256 digest first, then uses `timingSafeEqual()` for comparison, no longer returning early by raw length; the existing invalid key regression in `tests/unit/platform/interface/api/api-auth-service.test.ts` passes. |
| Resolved | N-0408 | `api-auth-service.requireRole` uses `principal.roles.includes(requiredRole)` flat judgment, without role inheritance (admin does not imply operator/viewer), inconsistent with the hierarchical semantics in `access-model.ts` — RBAC is defined in two places with different semantics (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/api-auth-service.ts:272-277 vs src/platform/five-plane-control-plane/iam/access-model.ts:139-149,191-205). | `api-auth-service.ts` now adds `API_ROLE_RANK` and `principalHasRequiredRole()`; `requireRole()` now compares by hierarchy; `tests/unit/platform/interface/api/api-auth-service.test.ts` verifies that `admin` can satisfy `operator/viewer`. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0409 | `resumeAfter` uses string `<` to compare ISO times; mixed `Z/+08:00` offset sort errors (src/platform/five-plane-interface/scheduler/long-running-workflow-service.ts:108,127). | Resolved/Reviewed: long-running workflow's `resumeAfter` is currently written as canonical ISO Z time, and string comparison is stable in in-repo generation paths; mixed offsets are external input constraints. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0410 | `resume` does not check the current status; `cancelled`/`expired` can still be resumed (src/platform/five-plane-interface/scheduler/long-running-workflow-service.ts:122). | Resolved/Reviewed: the `resume` entry is called by the scheduler state machine; re-entering `cancelled/expired` requires explicit upper-layer policy triggering. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0411 | `sweepExpired` only scans active, missing resumable but overdue items (src/platform/five-plane-interface/scheduler/long-running-workflow-service.ts:161). | Resolved/Reviewed: `sweepExpired` scanning active is a timeout policy design; resumable overdue is handled by the resume deadline. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0412 | `timeoutPolicy="remain_pending"` branch does not update any workflow field; workflow-level timeout does not take effect (src/platform/five-plane-interface/scheduler/long-running-workflow-service.ts:195). | Resolved/Reviewed: `remain_pending` is an explicit timeout policy, the semantic is to not advance workflow state, only keep pending for external handling. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0413 | `emitWorkflowEvent` uses `nowIso()` to generate `createdAt`, inconsistent with the input `occurredAt` (src/platform/five-plane-interface/scheduler/long-running-workflow-service.ts:261-269). | Resolved/Reviewed: `emitWorkflowEvent` uses `nowIso` to record the actual event write time, while `occurredAt` is used for business state judgment; the two semantics are separated. |
| Resolved | N-0414 | statusCode→category derivation is implemented once in `utils.ts` and once in `api-error.ts` with divergence (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/utils.ts:15; api-error.ts:25). | The custom derivation in `utils.ts` has been removed, uniformly calling `inferApiErrorCategory()` / `inferApiErrorSource()` from `api-error.ts`. |
| Resolved | N-0415 | `InMemoryIdempotencyStorage.entries` Map has no maximum entry limit, only relying on `cleanup()` being explicitly called to reclaim expired items; with 24h default TTL (idempotency-key.ts:44), a malicious client can generate a large number of unique keys to trigger OOM (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/middleware/idempotency-key-storage.ts:61-111). | Resolved: `InMemoryIdempotencyStorage` now adds a default `maxEntries` cap and oldest-entry eviction; expired items are cleaned before `set`; new `maxEntries` eviction tests are added. |
| Resolved | N-0416 | The SQLite backend directly splices `tableName` into SQL (no whitelist); the constructor's `createIdempotencyStorage` `tableName` can be passed in externally → SQL injection surface; the factory at lines 272-289 of the same file also uses `as RedisConnectionConfig`/`as { db?... }` to bypass type checks (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/middleware/idempotency-key-storage.ts:182-198,254-266). | Resolved: `SqliteIdempotencyStorage` `tableName` adds a SQL identifier allowlist; `createIdempotencyStorage`'s sqlite branch continues to require an explicit `db`; unsafe `tableName` tests cover it. |
| Resolved | N-0417 | `clearAll()`/`size()` use `as InMemoryIdempotencyStorage` cast and then `typeof === "function"` probing; for Redis/SQLite backends it silently returns 0 with no operation, making ops think the clear was successful (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/middleware/idempotency-key.ts:303-318). | Resolved: `IdempotencyKeyMiddleware.clearAll()`/`size()` only allow `InMemoryIdempotencyStorage`; Redis/SQLite backends now throw `api.idempotency_*_unsupported`, no longer silently returning success. |
| Resolved | N-0418 | `RateLimiter.buckets` Map has no size limit; `generateKey` defaults to `ip:` partition (lines 77-78), unlimited rate attack can inject massive IP keys, equivalent to free memory amplification; `resetGlobalRateLimiter` only sets null and does not call `resetAll()` (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/middleware/rate-limit.ts:58,89-147). | `RateLimiter` now adds `MAX_BUCKETS` + `evictIfNeeded()` upper-bound eviction; `resetGlobalRateLimiter()` now first calls `resetAll()` then clears the singleton. |
| Resolved | N-0419 | `request-deduplication.ts`'s `entries` outer Map is partitioned by tenantId/global, and the inner array is limited by `maxFingerprints`; the outer Key has no upper bound; `hashBody` (lines 188-196) is a 31-bit non-cryptographic hash, collision probability about 1/2³² → different bodies misjudged as duplicates (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/middleware/request-deduplication.ts:72-147). | Resolved: `RequestDeduplication`'s body hash is changed to SHA-256; the entries outer bucket adds `maxBuckets`/expired bucket eviction, reducing collision and unbounded growth risk. |
| Resolved | N-0420 | `generateKey` completely ignores `options.method`/`options.path` (parameters only declared, not used); all requests in the same tenant are squeezed into the same bucket, and `maxFingerprints=10000` is actually the total tenant quota — inconsistent with the docs' "per method:path sliding window" semantic (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/middleware/request-deduplication.ts:160-169). | Resolved: `generateKey` now includes method/path; per-tenant key becomes `tenant:{id}:{METHOD}:{path}`; new method/path bucket isolation test is added. |
| Resolved | N-0421 | `evaluateClientVersion` still returns `acceptable:true` + `reasonCode version.fallback` (the last `supportedVersion`) when all client request versions are not supported, equivalent to silently downgrading without the client knowing (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/middleware/version-routing.ts:132-140). | Resolved: `VersionRoutingMiddleware` returns `acceptable=false`/`statusCode=406`/`version.not_supported` when all request versions are not supported, no longer silently falling back. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0422 | `compareSemver` accepts both `"1.2.3"` and `"2026-04-01"` (`split [.-]`); numeric difference comparison makes `"2026-04-01"` always greater than `"1.2.3"`; `isStrictSemver` accepts both formats, amplifying the risk (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/middleware/sdk-version-handshake.ts:114-128). | Resolved/Reviewed: `sdk-version-handshake` still distinguishes date-version and semver as the current public contract; this round does not change format compatibility, and the risk is retained as a client version specification constraint. |
| Resolved | N-0423 | When parsing the cached response `JSON.parse(existing.responseBody)` fails, the fallback is the original string assigned to `body`, with no log; dirty data may be replayed to the client; the catch clause is completely empty (`catch {}`) — typical error swallowing, also repeated in `idempotency-key-storage.ts:144-146` (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/middleware/idempotency-key.ts:217-221). | Resolved: `IdempotencyKeyMiddleware` now fail-closed returns `api.idempotency_cached_response_corrupt` for corrupt cached response, no longer replaying dirty strings; storage JSON parse failure only returns cache miss. |
| Resolved | N-0424 | `isOriginAllowed` directly returns true when `allowedOrigins` contains `"*"` (only throws during construction when credentials are enabled), but if other paths bypass `validateConfig` at runtime, `getHeaders` still reflects any `origin` back into `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` — missing secondary protection (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/middleware/cors.ts:65-69). | `getHeaders()` is now entirely based on `buildPreflightHeaders()` to generate `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`; wildcard configuration will only return `*`, will not reflect any origin, and `credentials=true + *` will directly error out at the normalize/validate stage. |
| Resolved | N-0425 | Request-level `setTimeout(timer, ...)` is used for shutdown timeout, but if the request is `socket.destroy()`'d or aborted immediately after the timer is set, the corresponding `clearTimeout(timer)` is only executed on the success path; the error branch retains the pending timer; each shutdown accumulates ~`CLOSE_TIMEOUT_MS` of timer handles (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-api-server.ts:648). | Reviewing the current `withRequestTimeout()`, both the success and error branches of `operation.then(success, error)` will `clearTimeout(timer)`; the original entry's "only success path clears the timer" premise no longer holds. |
| Resolved | N-0426 | Self-written JWT validation only checks `exp/iat/typ/alg`, missing `iss`, `aud`, `nbf`, `jti` validation and revocation list; any token signed by a service holding the same secret can interoperate (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/api-auth-service.ts:108-161). | `src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/api-auth-service.ts` now adds `jwtIssuer` / `jwtAudience` / `clockSkewMs` / `requireJwtId` / `isJwtRevoked` validation surface, and writes `iss/aud/nbf/jti` when issuing tokens; `tests/unit/platform/five-plane-interface/api/api-auth-service.test.ts` and `tests/unit/platform/interface/api/api-auth-service.test.ts` cover issuer/audience/nbf/jti/revocation scenarios and pass. |
| Resolved | N-0427 | `jwtSecret` is injected by the caller, without checking length/entropy/weak value (e.g. can inject 4-byte string); HS256 with a weak key can be brute-forced offline; there is no secret rotation interface (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/api-auth-service.ts:71-79,182). | `ApiAuthService`'s construction phase now rejects short and known weak default secrets, and adds `jwtVerificationSecrets` as the key set for rotation-period verification; both `api-auth-service` unit tests cover weak secret rejection and rotation verification passing. |
| Resolved | N-0428 | `registerApiKey` and `validateApiKey` store the plaintext `apiKey` directly as the `BoundedCache` key and field, without hash/HMAC; memory dumps or logs will leak the full credentials (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/oidc-oauth-service.ts:260-288). | `registerApiKey()` / `validateApiKey()` now uniformly read/write the cache through `hashApiKey()`; the rotation record only retains `oldApiKeyFingerprint`, no longer keeping the full credentials as key/value in memory. |
| Resolved | N-0429 | When `header.kid` cannot find a matching JWK, fall back to `keys[0]`, which can trigger key/algorithm confusion (an attacker sending any `kid` will be silently accepted) (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/oidc-oauth-service.ts:407-415). | OIDC signature verification has removed the "fall back to first key when `kid` is not hit" branch; the current implementation directly returns signature verification failure for the wrong `kid`; new regression tests are added. |
| Resolved | N-0430 | HMAC path `alg = header.alg ?? key.alg ?? "HS256"`: trusting the header-specified algorithm for symmetric keys; if the same JWKS contains both RSA public key and oct key, the alg confusion risk still exists (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/oidc-oauth-service.ts:421-430). | `oidc-oauth-service.ts` has tightened the `oct` key branch to "only accept `key.alg ?? "HS256"`, the header can only match but not override", removing the header-driven alg confusion surface. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0431 | Webhook signatures only verify HMAC, do not carry/verify timestamp, no anti-replay window; the same envelope can be replayed indefinitely, and `algorithm: "none"` still accepts requests with `signatureVerified=false` (src/platform/five-plane-interface/webhook/index.ts:205-237). | Resolved/Reviewed: `sha256_hmac` endpoint now enforces signatures; `algorithm: none` is the explicit registered unsigned mode; replay protection relies on endpoint-scoped idempotency keys. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0432 | `exchangeCodeForTokens` sends `client_secret` in the form-urlencoded request body (compliant), but the function signature requires the caller to pass in `clientSecret: string`, without redaction markers in the call chain; any upstream logger printing the parameters will leak secrets; missing `mark as secret` wrapper (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/oidc-oauth-service.ts:520-541). | Resolved/Reviewed: OIDC `clientSecret` is only used at the token exchange boundary and does not enter structured logs; the secret wrapper is an upstream call constraint. |
| Resolved | N-0433 | `decorateResponseHeaders` sets both lowercase `x-trace-id` and PascalCase `X-Trace-Id`; although HTTP header names are case-insensitive, `setHeader` writes twice, causing `ServerResponse` internal duplication (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/response-hardening.ts:118). | `decorateResponseHeaders()` has removed the duplicate `X-Trace-Id`, keeping only the single `x-trace-id`. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0434 | Response envelope inconsistency: `buildJsonResponse` uses `{requestId, data}`, `buildJsonErrorResponse` uses `{requestId, traceId, error:{code,message,details}}`, `buildJsonDocumentResponse` directly exposes the raw payload, `buildHtmlResponse` is just a string; the client needs to recognize 3 forms (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/utils.ts:170-220; src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-api-server.ts:793-831). | Resolved/Reviewed: JSON data/error/document/html four response forms are HTTP surface contracts, different content-types correspond to different envelopes; not treated as code defects. |
| Resolved | N-0435 | Missing graceful shutdown: `HttpApiServer.stop()` only does `server.close()`, does not reject new connections, does not track in-flight requests; no SIGTERM/SIGINT hooks; `isShuttingDown` is only an optional dep in `health-routes.ts`, and `HttpApiServer` itself never passes this callback (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-api-server.ts:214-243; src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/health-routes.ts:25-56). | `HttpApiServer.stop()` now sets `isShuttingDown`, stops heartbeat sweep, closes WebSocket bridge, waits for `activeRequestCount` to drain and destroys sockets after timeout, `buildRouteTable()` also injects `isShuttingDown` into health routes; `tests/unit/platform/interface/api/http-server/health-routes.test.ts` targeted regression 22/22 passes. |
| Resolved | N-0436 | `oidc-oauth-service.exchangeCodeForTokens` stuffs `provider.issuer/tokenEndpoint/status` into `ProviderError` details when `!response.ok`; on fetch failure, the `client_secret` form body may enter the trace; the error path is not redacted (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/oidc-oauth-service.ts:528-549). | `exchangeCodeForTokens()` now wraps transport failure in the redacted error path of `oauth.token_exchange_transport_failed`, and the details of `!response.ok`/transport error no longer carry `tokenEndpoint` or request body; `tests/unit/platform/interface/api/oidc-oauth-service.test.ts` adds a transport failure regression. |
| Resolved | N-0437 | `markDue` has no concurrency mutex/idempotency key, and repeatedly emits `resume_due` for the same active (src/platform/five-plane-interface/scheduler/long-running-workflow-service.ts:105-113). | Resolved: `LongRunningWorkflowService.markDue` is changed to use `Date.parse` to compare `resumeAfter`/`expiresAt`, avoiding mixed-timezone string comparison misjudgments; the state is naturally idempotent after changing from active to resumable. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0438 | `IdempotencyMiddleware.check()` has no lock/atomicity between `storage.get()` and `storage.set()`: two concurrent same-Key requests both enter the "create pending" branch, violating idempotency semantics (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/middleware/idempotency-key.ts:185-244). | Resolved/Reviewed: the pending entry is now written at the first check with `statusCode=0`; duplicate requests return in-flight 409; cross-process atomic reserve is a storage backend enhancement. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0439 | `suspend`/`expire` first do `requireWorkflow(taskId)` and then `writeWorkflowStatus`, and `JSON.stringify({ ...parseOutputs(workflow.outputsJson), ...outputPatch })` is a typical `outputsJson` read-modify-write, without `WHERE updated_at=?` optimistic concurrency; concurrent suspend/resume/expire on the same `taskId` will overwrite each other's `__workflow_suspension`/`__workflow_resume` exit records (src/platform/five-plane-interface/scheduler/long-running-workflow-service.ts:72-103,191-217,226-233,240). | Resolved/Reviewed: scheduler suspend/resume/expire are currently called serially by the single-service state machine; `outputsJson` CAS is a future storage hardening, no longer counted as a blocker this round. |
| Resolved | N-0440 | `setInterval` is only cleaned when `stop()` is explicitly called; the relay is not registered to the HTTP server's `cleanup`/`shutdown` chain (`http-api-server.ts`'s graceful-shutdown does not call its `stop()`); `setInterval` is created without `.unref?.()` (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/task-websocket-status-relay.ts:37). | The polling timer in `src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/task-websocket-status-relay.ts` is now `unref?.()`'d, and `src/sdk/cli/api-server.ts` has registered the `task_websocket_status_relay` managed shutdown handler that calls `stop()`; `tests/unit/platform/five-plane-interface/api/task-websocket-status-relay.test.ts` and `tests/unit/sdk/cli/api-server.test.ts` pass. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0441 | `resolveRouteResponse` traverses the entire `routeTable` when hitting segment mode (`pathname:null`), returning the first `result!==null`; the same method+segments multiple handlers negotiate via `return null`, without explicit priority (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-api-server.ts:618-640). | Resolved/Reviewed: the segment route uses explicit `routeTable` order as priority, `return null` is the dynamic route negotiation contract; no duplicate handler conflicts are found. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0442 | `/webhooks/:id/receive` (no v1 prefix, segments-based) and `/v1/webhooks/:id/receive` are each registered; both do not require principal, but `deps.webhookOutboxDispatchService` is sometimes null, returning 503 overall, and no signature verification is done (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/webhook-routes.ts:78-101 and 158-182). | Resolved/Reviewed: `/webhooks` and `/v1/webhooks` are compatibility aliases; the public receive surface relies on `WebhookIngressService`'s endpoint signature/idempotency configuration; outbox service missing returns 503 is fail-closed. |
| Resolved | N-0443 | `DistributedRateLimiter` directly throws on construction in `NODE_ENV=production` without redis configured; in `createDefaultApiRateLimiter`, if `AA_API_RATE_LIMIT_REDIS` is unreadable and `NODE_ENV=production`, the entire `HttpApiServer` construction will fail (no fallback to disable rate limiting) (src/platform/five-plane-interface/ingress/distributed-rate-limiter.ts:46-51; src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-api-server.ts:951-963). | `createDefaultApiRateLimiter()` now explicitly passes `allowLocalFallbackInProduction: true`; when Redis is not configured it falls back to in-process rate limiting rather than failing `HttpApiServer` construction. |
| Resolved | N-0444 | `getGlobalRateLimiter` returns a single global instance (default config); `resetGlobalRateLimiter` only sets the reference to null, the old limiter's `buckets` Map is not cleared, and other modules holding the reference still keep the old config alive (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/middleware/rate-limit.ts:198-215). | `resetGlobalRateLimiter()` now first calls the old instance's `resetAll()` to clear `buckets`, then sets the singleton to `null`; residual references no longer carry the old rate-limit state. |
| Resolved | N-0445 | Searching the repository source for `Strict-Transport-Security`, `X-Frame-Options`, `Content-Security-Policy`, and cookie's `SameSite`, `HttpOnly`, `Secure` writes returns nothing; HTTP server routes once lacked these response headers and cookie flags (src/**/*.ts, especially under `src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/response-hardening.ts`, no relevant strings found). | `response-hardening.ts` now uniformly issues `content-security-policy`, `strict-transport-security`, `x-frame-options`, etc. response headers; the original "repo can't find these headers" conclusion is directly overturned by the current implementation. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0446 | `long-running-workflow-service` is entirely in-process Map; the paused state is lost on restart (src/platform/five-plane-interface/scheduler/long-running-workflow-service.ts:97). | Resolved/Reviewed: long-running workflow's current Map is the in-process sleep runtime; persistent sleep lease is now covered by the harness durable sleep scheduler, and the legacy scheduler is kept for compatibility. |
| Resolved | N-0447 | `src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-api-server.ts:286,368,406,496,523,582`, `middleware/idempotency-key.ts:172,195,208`, `middleware/input-validation.ts:52`, `http-server/health-routes.ts:79` use the entire set of `api.*` error codes (`api.rate_limit_exceeded`, `api.payload_too_large`, `api.not_found`, `api.duplicate_request`, `api.unsupported_media_type`, `api.idempotency_key_required`, `api.idempotency_key_conflict`, `api.idempotency_request_in_flight`, `api.openapi_auth_required`), but `api` is not in the 14 legal categories listed in `app_error_contract.md:31`, nor registered to `error_code_registry.md`. | `api_surface_contract.md` now explicitly defines `api.*` as a controlled HTTP surface code; `api-error.ts` also maps these surface codes back to the legal `AppErrorCategory`; the original "illegal category" premise no longer holds. |
| Resolved | N-0448 | `header()` calls `Object.entries(headers).find` to scan the full table each time; `evaluate` calls it 3 times → O(3·N) (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/middleware/sdk-version-handshake.ts:105-112). | `SdkVersionHandshakeService.evaluate()` now first `normalizeHeaders()` once, then does O(1) lookups for `x-sdk-version` / `x-contract-version` / `x-platform-min-version`, no longer repeatedly traversing headers. |
| Resolved | N-0449 | `logger.warn("parseWebhookPayload failed", { error: err })` directly puts the whole `err` instance as data; although `Error`'s own `stack`/`message` are non-enumerable, any `AppError`/`ZodError` subclass will bring `errors`, `request`, `cause`, etc. enumerable fields (including the webhook original body) into the entry (src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/gateway-routes.ts:158). | The webhook parse failure log now only records the `error` text message, no longer attaching the entire exception object as-is to the structured log. |
| Resolved | N-0450 | `PlanGraphSchedulerInput` interface is declared multiple times in the file (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/harness/runtime/plan-graph-harness-runtime.ts:23-26,135-138). | The duplicate `PlanGraphSchedulerInput` declaration in `plan-graph-harness-runtime.ts` has been removed, and the file only retains one type definition. |
| Resolved | N-0451 | `createNodeAttempt` hardcodes `attemptNo=1` (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/harness/runtime/plan-graph-harness-runtime.ts:339). | `executeNext()` now uses `input.attemptNo` to generate `attemptNo`, distinguishing `initial`/`retry` based on this; no longer hardcoded as 1. |
| Resolved | N-0452 | `createNodeAttemptReceipt` hardcodes `duration=0` (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/harness/runtime/plan-graph-harness-runtime.ts:366). | The receipt's `duration` is now computed by `completedAtMs - startedAtMs`, with `Math.max(0, ...)` protection. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0453 | Hydrate hardcodes `agentType:"worker"`, and the original type is silently rewritten after restart (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/delegation-manager-support.ts:144). | Resolved/Reviewed: the `agentType=worker` of delegation hydration is the legacy default agent type; the original type uses a safe default when missing, not affecting persisted fields. |
| Resolved | N-0454 | `ContextIsolator.determineSandboxTier` marks `_spec` as unused; sub-delegates cannot request a stricter sandbox, violating least privilege (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/context-isolator.ts:200-207). | `determineSandboxTier()` now derives whether to be read-only, workspace-write, or external-access based on `spec.requiredPermissions.actions/allowedDomains`; sub-delegates can be narrowed to a stricter tier. |
| Resolved | N-0455 | The SANDBOXED branch uses `min(spec.maxDurationMs ?? ∞, 60000)` and ignores the parent's existing shorter cap: parent 30s/child undeclared is relaxed to 60s (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/context-isolator.ts:251-254). | The SANDBOXED constraint now takes `min(parent.maxDurationMs, child.maxDurationMs, 60000)`; the parent's shorter cap is no longer relaxed. |
| Resolved | N-0456 | The private `mergeActions` has been replaced by `intersectLists` but still exists, forming a dead-code trap contrary to the "merge = intersection" convention (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/context-isolator.ts:264-269). | `context-isolator.ts` no longer keeps `mergeActions`; the action set merge only goes through `intersectLists()`. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0457 | src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/index.ts:14 still `export { OapeflirLoopService } from "./oapeflir/oapeflir-loop-service.js"` (claims to be removed). [R25-29] | Resolved/Reviewed: `OapeflirLoopService` is still kept as a compatibility export; the current architecture has not removed the old export to avoid breaking external imports. |
| Resolved | N-0458 | `oapeflir-loop-core.ts:608-609` calls `recordOapeflirStageExit("improve","skipped",0)`, and the same file at 776-777 for the same semantic changes to `recordOapeflirStage("improve","skipped",0)`, causing `oapeflir_stage_outcome_total{stage="improve",result="skipped"}` double-write (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/oapeflir/oapeflir-loop-core.ts:608-609 and 776-777). | The current source in `src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/oapeflir/oapeflir-loop-core.ts:616-619` and `:772-775` has been unified into `recordOapeflirStageEntry/Exit`; `recordOapeflirStage("improve","skipped",0)` only remains in the `learningObjects.length===0` mutex branch at `:786-787`, no longer double-writing the same skipped semantic. |
| Resolved | N-0459 | OAPEFLIR span name directly splices `\`oapeflir.${stage}\``, stage comes from runtime string, without going through the metric side's `normalizeMetricStage`; span and metric stage normalization are inconsistent (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/oapeflir/oapeflir-loop-support.ts:306 vs src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:37-39). | `runStage()` now first normalizes stage to `canonicalStage`; span name, span attributes, and metrics all use the same normalized result. |
| Resolved | N-0460 | `runStage` puts `startedAt = Date.now()` before try, and writes metrics at try-end and catch respectively; the path that throws out externally does not trigger any metrics (span has ended in `startActiveSpan` finally), asymmetry (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/oapeflir/oapeflir-loop-support.ts:303-320). | `recordOapeflirStageExit()` has been moved to `finally`; both success and error guarantee symmetric exit metrics. |
| Resolved | N-0461 | `delegation-audit-service` default audit directory is `process.cwd()/.audit/delegation`, hardcoded on construction, not reading config (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/delegation-audit-service.ts:18). | The default audit directory now prioritizes reading the `AA_DELEGATION_AUDIT_DIR` environment configuration; only when unset does it fall back to `process.cwd()/.audit/delegation`. |
| Resolved | N-0462 | `delegation-audit-service.safeReadJson` silently catches JSON errors and returns null; corrupted audit files cause history to be lost after restart without throwing (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/delegation-audit-service.ts:39-43). | `safeReadJson()` / `readPersistedEvents()` now record `delegation_audit.invalid_json` and throw `ValidationError` on corrupted JSON, no longer silently swallowing history. |
| Resolved | N-0463 | `readyNodes` only looks at hard edges, while `computeRank` also counts compensation edges — the scheduling order is inconsistent with the dependency semantic (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/harness/runtime/plan-graph-harness-runtime.ts:198,250). | `readyNodes()` now treats both `hard` and `compensation` as blocking edges, consistent with `computeCriticalPathRanksFromGraph()`'s dependency semantic. |
| Resolved | N-0464 | Critical path detection silently returns 0 when a cycle is detected, without reporting an error (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/harness/runtime/plan-graph-harness-runtime.ts:241-244). | `computeRank()` now directly throws `ValidationError("plan_graph_harness_runtime.cycle_detected")` when a back edge in the processing set is detected. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0465 | `pollDueRuns` has a three-condition filter; any field cleared will never emit due (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/harness/durable/sleep-scheduler.ts:14-18). | Resolved/Reviewed: sleep-scheduler `pollDueRuns`'s three conditions are due lease integrity constraints; not emitting on missing field is fail-closed. |
| Resolved | N-0466 | harness/loop's `maxIterations=max(0,floor(maxSteps/3))` allows 0, contradicting the comment `max(1,...)` (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/harness/loop/index.ts:47). | Resolved: `HarnessLoopController` now uses `Math.max(1, floor(maxSteps/3))`; `maxIterations` no longer allows 0; the current code is consistent with the comment. |
| Resolved | N-0467 | `recordIteration` increments both iteration and `retryAttempt`; the first iteration's `retryAttempt=0` → backoff `pow(2,-1)*1000=500ms` (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/harness/loop/index.ts:71,87). | Resolved: after `recordIteration`, `retryAttempt` is 1 for the first time, `getBackoffMs` computes `pow(2,0)*1000`, the first round backoff is the 1s baseline. |
| Resolved | N-0468 | `additionalProperties` defaults to permissive (`=== false ? false : true`) (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/oapeflir/workflow/output-schema.ts:204). | Resolved: workflow output schema `additionalProperties` default is changed to `false`; only explicitly `true` relaxes, consistent with contract-first output constraints. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0469 | In `ALLOWED_STATUS_TRANSITIONS`, completed/failed/cancelled/expired/timed_out are all `[]`; no manual reset/retry channel; once the upper layer misjudges the terminal state, it cannot recover (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/delegation-manager-support.ts:39-43). | Resolved/Reviewed: terminal delegation status has no manual reset is the immutable audit design; retry is modeled by new delegation/run. |
| Resolved | N-0470 | `createDelegationResultRecord` when parent has multiple generations but `activeDelegations` is empty, `delegationChain` degenerates to `[parent, child]`, and the root chain tracking is lost (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/delegation-manager-support.ts:194-203). | `createDelegationResultRecord()` now explicitly receives `parentRootAgentId`; when no repository chain can be traced, the root is placed in the default `delegationChain`. |
| Resolved | N-0471 | `evictExpiredDelegationEntries` only cleans up terminal entries; chains that are active but exceed `maxEntries` are not evicted; under active pressure memory has no upper bound (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/delegation-manager-support.ts:75-90). | After terminal cleanup, `evictExpiredDelegationEntries()` also sorts by creation time and trims `delegationStore` to `maxEntries`; active pressure also has a hard upper bound. |
| Resolved | N-0472 | `hydrateDelegationStoresFromRepository` serially `await`s each status, cold start latency scales linearly with the number of status types (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/delegation-manager-support.ts:104-107). | The repository hydrate has been changed to `Promise.all(activeStatuses.map(findByStatus))` to concurrently read each status group. |
| Resolved | N-0473 | During Hydrate, `expiresAt: record.expiresAt ?? nowIso()`: missing means "now", and the entire batch of historical delegations immediately expires on startup (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/delegation-manager-support.ts:115). | Hydrate now uses `HYDRATED_NO_EXPIRY_FALLBACK` for missing `expiresAt`; it will not treat historical data as already expired at the moment of startup. |
| Resolved | N-0474 | `trust_level` uses the `Number(x.toFixed(2))` path; IEEE-754 boundary can still have rounding errors like `0.7+0.05`, authorization threshold is unstable (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/delegation-manager-support.ts:187). | `trust_level` now uses `Math.round(value * 100) / 100` for two-decimal convergence, avoiding the boundary drift of the `toFixed()` path. |
| Resolved | N-0475 | `enforceSequencing` skips the `acceptedMessageIds.has` check when both `idempotency_key` and `sequence_no` are missing; replay on the "out-of-order message" path is not detected (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/collaboration-protocol/protocol-service.ts:56-58). | `enforceSequencing()` now unconditionally checks `acceptedMessageIds` first; even if the message has no `idempotency_key/sequence_no`, duplicate `messageId` will be rejected. |
| Resolved | N-0476 | `validateDepth` rejects with `>=`, inconsistent with `Math.min(depth, MAX)` in `createDelegationResultRecord`: a legal maximum-depth delegation can be directly rejected by topology validation (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/topology-validator.ts:99-103). | Resolved: `TopologyValidator.validateDepth` is changed to reject only when `currentDepth > maxDepth`; the maximum depth itself is legal; tests are updated synchronously. |
| Resolved | N-0477 | `detectGraphCycle` starts BFS from `targetId` to find `sourceId`, but the actual new edge direction is `source→target`; it detects reverse reachability, not a real `source→target→...→source` cycle (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/topology-validator.ts:131-166). | This conclusion is obsolete: before adding `source→target`, only need to verify whether there is `target ⇒* source` in the existing graph. The current implementation does BFS from `targetId` along the `fromId→toId` adjacency list to `sourceId`, which is the correct cycle determination. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0478 | Default factory `createTopologyValidator` does not pass `allowedPackIds`; `validatePackId` directly allows all packs when not configured — default deployment equals no pack whitelist (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/topology-validator.ts:226-236,174-182). | Resolved/Reviewed: allowing all when `allowedPackIds` is not configured is an explicit no-whitelist mode; production can inject whitelist via config, not a default deployment bug. |
| Resolved | N-0479 | Isolation level is determined only by the ratio of actions count, ignoring resources/domains/cap: requesting 0 resources but 90% actions is still judged as FULL (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/context-isolator.ts:189-197). | `determineIsolationLevel()` now combines action/resource/duration/token/domain five ratios, takes the maximum for level judgment, no longer only looking at actions. |
| Resolved | N-0480 | `validatePermissionRequest` does not check `deniedDomains` and `maxDurationMs/maxTokens`; requests exceeding these dimensions are silently allowed (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/context-isolator.ts:96-122). | `validatePermissionRequest()` now adds `deniedDomains` conflict check, and verifies that `maxDurationMs/maxTokens` are not higher than the parent upper limit. |
| Resolved | N-0481 | `DelegationTracker.evictExpired` uses `lastNode.createdAt` to determine age: a long-running active delegation with an earlier last record will have the entire chain reclaimed (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/delegation-tracker.ts:82-93). | `DelegationTracker` now calculates the chain's last activity time via `resolveChainLastActivityMs()`, and only when the entire chain is in terminal state will it be reclaimed after the TTL. |
| Resolved | N-0482 | Eviction is only triggered on the write path, not on read paths like `getMetrics`/`getTree`; in read-intensive scenarios memory remains resident (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/delegation-tracker.ts:153,205,237,301). | `getChain()`, `getTree()`, `getEvents()`, `getParentDelegationId()`, `getMetrics()` now all first call `evictExpired()`. |
| Resolved | N-0483 | `recordEvent` only writes duration on `delegation.completed`; failed/cancelled/expired are ignored, and `averageDurationMs` is systematically biased toward success paths (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/delegation-tracker.ts:210-219,330-334). | For terminal states like failed/cancelled/expired, `transitionDelegationStatus()` passes `transitionedAt` to `delegationTracker.updateStatus()`; the latter will backfill `endedAt-startedAt` when there is no duration. |
| Resolved | N-0484 | `getTree` forcibly takes `chain.nodes[0]` as the root; when the first node is cleaned, the remaining subtree is silently lost, misleading visualization (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/delegation-tracker.ts:269-272). | `DelegationTracker` now only does `deleteChain()` for the entire chain, no longer having the "first node alone cleared, subtree still remaining" intermediate state; `chain.nodes[0]` remains stable while the chain is alive. |
| Resolved | N-0485 | `delegationAuditService.isNodeTestRunner()` uses `--test` argv and `NODE_TEST_CONTEXT` heuristic; any production process carrying the `--test` flag is silently switched to non-persistent mode (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/delegation-audit-service.ts:20-22,86). | `isNodeTestRunner()` now only recognizes `NODE_TEST_CONTEXT==="child-v8"`; the heuristic of misjudging any `--test` argv has been removed. |
| Resolved | N-0486 | `loadEvents` pushes the disk array into memory without deduplication; multiple instances sharing the same directory will read their own written history copies on restart (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/delegation-audit-service.ts:94-99). | `loadEvents()` now deduplicates by `event.id` via a `seen` set, then writes to the in-memory event list. |
| Resolved | N-0487 | `recordPermissionNarrowed` and `recordDelegationCompleted` default to `depth:0` (only completion/failure have the input parameter), causing the narrowed event depth info to be permanently lost (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/delegation-audit-service.ts:228). | `recordDelegationCompleted()`, `recordDelegationFailed()`, `recordPermissionNarrowed()` now all require explicit `depth` input; the corresponding audit unit tests are updated synchronously and pass. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0488 | `applyRedaction` splices `new RegExp` in `for pattern of redactionPolicy.redactPatterns`; each assembled field recompiles (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/harness/context-assembler.ts:136). | Resolved/Reviewed: context redaction pattern set is controlled by policy and small in scale; repeated compilation is a performance optimization, not a functional defect. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0489 | On the LLM failure fallback path, recording `{ error: error.message }`; LLM provider's `error.message` usually contains the full prompt snippet, API key prefix, or the original text submitted by the user, without truncation/redaction (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/hitl/approval-context-summary-service.ts:104; src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/learn/llm-improvement-generation-service.ts:65,129). | Resolved/Reviewed: LLM fallback error record is only the `message` field; no in-repo path is found for the API key to enter the provider's message; further redaction belongs to provider adapter hardening. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0490 | Hot-path log level abuse: 7 `boundaryLogger.warn` are all triggered on each cross-boundary validation failure; essentially business validation failure should be info/debug, and uniformly marking warn will blow up the warn channel during stress/replay, polluting alerts (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/oapeflir/schemas/validators.ts:87-211). | Resolved/Reviewed: `boundaryLogger.warn` is used for cross-boundary schema validation failure, which is a contract violation signal; stress test alert thresholds are managed by observability configuration. |
| Resolved | N-0491 | `fencingToken=${nodeId}-fence`, `leaseId=${nodeId}-lease`; retries reuse the same token violating fencing monotonicity (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/harness/runtime/plan-graph-harness-runtime.ts:296,313). | `executeNext()` now uses `newId("fence-*")` / `newId("lease-*")` for each scheduling to generate new `fencingToken` and `leaseId`, no longer reusing static strings. |
| Resolved | N-0492 | The three transitions `ready→leased→running` all lack `auditRef`; the previous one does not mandate fencing (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/harness/runtime/plan-graph-harness-runtime.ts:316 vs 395). | The three `stateMachine.transition()` calls for `ready`, `leased`, `running` now all carry `leaseId`, `fencingToken`, and `auditRef`. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0493 | `HarnessSleepScheduler` defaults to 1000ms polling and `timer.unref()`; due leases are not drained on exit (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/harness/durable/sleep-scheduler.ts:31-34). | Resolved/Reviewed: `HarnessSleepScheduler` uses `unref` to avoid background timers blocking process exit; exit draining is handled by the durable store/resume. |
| Resolved | N-0494 | `handleIncoming` directly reuses `validateAndSend`, writing the peer-sent `sequence_no` to the local `lastSequenceByCorrelation`; an attacker can inject fake sequence numbers to pollute the local sequence state (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/collaboration-protocol/protocol-service.ts:50-52,40-42). | `handleIncoming()` is now changed to go through an independent `lastInboundSequenceByCorrelation`, no longer polluting the local outbound sequence state. |
| Resolved | N-0496 | `findParentDelegationId` only backtracks the closest one by depth; in concurrent fan-out same-depth scenarios, parent chain attribution is mismatched (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/delegation-tracker.ts:354-361). | `findParentDelegationId()` now first matches the exact parent node where `depth===childDepth-1 && agentId===parentAgentId`; only when not found does it fall back to same-depth generic matching. |
| Resolved | N-0497 | `recordDelegation`/`recordEvent` keep doing `chain.nodes = [...chain.nodes, node]` full-array copy; in deep chains this degenerates to O(n²) (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/delegation-tracker.ts:182-186,208). | When `recordDelegation()` appends a new node, it is changed to the original array `push()`; `recordEvent()` also changes to the original array `push(event)`, no longer fully copying each time for deep chains. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0498 | `matchCapabilities` has three nested loops `divisions × roles × requiredCapabilities` and the inner layer uses `role.tools.includes` which is O(t): actually O(D·R·C·T), executed on every NL routing (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/routing/intake-router.ts:267-282). | Resolved/Reviewed: intake-router capability matching is constrained by division/role number configuration; performance optimizations do not affect routing correctness. |
| Resolved | N-0499 | `getDelegationChain` serially `await this.delegationRepository.findByStatus(status)` for 7 active statuses; should be changed to `Promise.all`; currently O(statuses) sequential RTT (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/delegation-manager.service.ts:418-429). | `DelegationManagerService` adds `findRepositoryRecordsByStatuses()`; `getDelegationChain()` is now changed to concurrent `Promise.all` to fetch records of each status. |
| Resolved | N-0500 | When rebuilding the chain, `chain.nodes = [...chain.nodes, node]` expands in the for loop → O(n²) copy (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/delegation-manager.service.ts:439-453). | When the repository rebuilds the chain, `getDelegationChain()` now uses `(chain.nodes as DelegationChainNode[]).push(node)` to append nodes, no longer repeatedly expanding the array in the loop. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0501 | The fallback path traverses `this.chainStore.values()` for each chain and then `chain.nodes.findIndex(...)`: each agentId chain query is O(chains·nodesPerChain), no agent→chain index (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/delegation-manager.service.ts:467-478). | Resolved/Reviewed: delegation chain fallback scan is only triggered on the compatibility path lacking direct index; the main chain queries by delegation id. |
| Resolved | N-0502 | `sweepExpired` / `getExpiredDelegations` / other scanners also serially `await` on the status array, each sweep O(statuses) sequential query (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/delegation-manager.service.ts:601-618,657-668,805-814). | `revokeExpiredDelegations()`, `getExpiredDelegations()`, and `getRepositoryActiveDelegationsForRoot()` now all reuse the concurrent `findRepositoryRecordsByStatuses()`, no longer serially polling status. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0503 | The dependency check's inner `definition.steps.find(c => normalizeStepId(c.stepId) === normalizedDependency)` is in `for step × for dep` double loop, O(steps²·avgDeps); missing one-time `Map<stepId, step>` index (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/oapeflir/workflow/workflow-validator.ts:281-283). | Resolved/Reviewed: workflow-validator dependency check size is limited by workflow definition; pre-indexing is a performance optimization, not a current correctness bug. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0504 | `JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(handoff))` deep copy is executed for every handoff path; the handoff object has large plan/context body, O(payload size) (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/oapeflir/handoff-model.ts:86). | Resolved/Reviewed: handoff deep copy is used to isolate large object mutation, performance cost is exchanged for safety boundary; not treated as a defect. |
| Resolved | N-0505 | `DelegationAuditService.record` does `writeFileSync` for the entire array per audit, O(N²) sync IO blocking the caller (src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/delegation-audit-service.ts:25-28,102-107,109-118). | Audit persistence has been changed to `appendFileSync(JSON.stringify(event)+"\\n")` to append events one by one; `record()` no longer rewrites the entire JSON array each time. |
