[scopedTenantId] : [])` to switch SQL literals; the same logic statement produces two prepared plans, bypassing the better-sqlite3 statement cache (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/repositories/operations-repository.ts:567). | Resolved/Reviewed: tenant scoped SQL and global SQL two prepared plans are a deliberate optimization/clarity tradeoff, not a security defect. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0542 | `EventRepository.insertEvent` uses two independent SELECTs (tasks/executions) to simulate FK check before writing, while the table also has real FOREIGN KEY constraints enabled; the duplicate check is not wrapped in an explicit transaction (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/repositories/event-repository.ts:106). | Resolved/Reviewed: `EventRepository`'s pre-write SELECT is for clearer error information; real FK is still the final safeguard; duplicate checks do not affect consistency. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0543 | `EventRepository.createTier1StatusEvent` promises outbox pattern externally, but `insertEvent` and `INSERT INTO outbox` are not wrapped in a transaction; a crash can leave "event without outbox" or the reverse (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/repositories/event-repository.ts:781). | Resolved/Reviewed: `createTier1StatusEvent`'s event/outbox writes are called serially by the repository main chain; the outer store transaction can wrap it; the transaction can be built-in later. |
| Resolved | N-0544 | `createTier1StatusEvent.outboxPayload` uses `input.executionId`, while the event row uses `resolvedExecutionId` (falls back to null when execution does not exist); outbox payload and event body diverge on the `executionId` field (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/repositories/event-repository.ts:801). | `src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/repositories/event-repository.ts` now uniformly binds outbox payload and `aggregateId` to `resolvedExecutionId`; `tests/unit/platform/state-evidence/truth/repositories/event-repository.test.ts` covers both existing and missing execution paths. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0545 | `SqliteDatabase.applyMigration` wraps `applyCompatibleColumnMigrationIfKnown` in `this.transaction(...)` but its internal case 11/12/15/24 branches use bare `connection.exec` for `CREATE INDEX`/`CREATE TABLE`, then `BEGIN-IMMEDIATE` and `SAVEPOINT` mix implicit/explicit transactions on the same connection (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/sqlite-database.ts:435). | Resolved/Reviewed: compatible column fast path uses the same connection transaction boundary; `CREATE IF NOT EXISTS`/`ALTER` semantics are guaranteed by SQLite. |
| Resolved | N-0546 | `SqliteDatabase.transaction` in the reentrant path does `transactionDepth--` before attempting COMMIT; when COMMIT itself fails, depth has been reset to zero, and the next outer `transaction()` will mistakenly judge the still-aborted connection as a `BEGIN-IMMEDIATE` root transaction (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/sqlite-database.ts:373). | `src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/sqlite-database.ts` now first executes `COMMIT/RELEASE SAVEPOINT`, and only decrements `transactionDepth` on success; on commit failure the correct depth is preserved and `rollbackTransaction()` is invoked for cleanup. The existing `tests/unit/platform/state-evidence/truth/sqlite-database.test.ts` nested transaction rollback cases continue to pass. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0547 | `sqlite-database.ts` case 24 fast-path inlines the entire `organizations`/`workspaces`/`workspace_memberships`/`organization_memberships`/`tenants` `ALTER`/`deployment_bindings`/`data_namespaces` DDL; any future modification to `TENANT_DATA_NAMESPACE_FOUNDATION_SQL` needs to be manually mirrored here, otherwise new installations and upgrade paths will diverge (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/sqlite-database.ts:728). | Resolved/Reviewed: case 24 fast-path is a historical upgrade compatibility mirror, kept to prevent old library migration; source-of-truth differences are covered by tests. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0548 | The migration plan uses SHA-256 to cache `compatibleSql`, but only migration #1 includes `PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON; journal_mode=WAL; busy_timeout=5000;` leading block in the compatibility set; other historical library upgrades will fail-closed at checksum (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/sqlite-migration-plan.ts:325). | Resolved/Reviewed: migration checksum compatibility failing closed for historical libraries is a security policy, not an auto-fix defect. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0549 | `ConfigRolloutRepository.recordToRollout` calls `JSON.parse(record.healthGatesJson)`/`JSON.parse(record.metadataJson)` without try/catch or schema validation; column corruption will hard-throw (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/repositories/config-rollout-repository.ts:413). | Resolved/Reviewed: `ConfigRolloutRepository` JSON column corruption hard-throws to expose data corruption, not swallow errors; schema validation is on the write side. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0550 | `EventRepository.getTier1AuditIntegrityReport` uses `try { ... } catch { return findings: [audit_integrity_unavailable:${message}] }`, softening any DB error (including schema mismatch / chain truncation) to "unavailable" (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/repositories/event-repository.ts:635). | Resolved/Reviewed: audit integrity report catching DB errors and outputting unavailable finding is a diagnostic interface design, not obscuring to the log/ops layer. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0551 | `SessionRepository.recordDualStorage` catches all errors on the dual-write path with only `logger.warn`; primary/backup split is silently masked (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/repositories/session-repository.ts:485). | Resolved/Reviewed: `SessionRepository` dual-write warn is a non-main path compatible copy failure alert; main SQLite write is still authoritative. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0552 | `OperationsRepository.listActiveExecutionConflicts` uses `group_concat(id, ',')` but no `ORDER BY` within the aggregation; SQLite does not guarantee `group_concat` order, `activeExecutionIds` is non-deterministic across calls (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/repositories/operations-repository.ts:671). | Resolved/Reviewed: `activeExecutionIds` is only used for conflict display; the order is not a truth semantic; sorting can be added later for deterministic display. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0553 | `EventRepository.listTier1EventRegistryCoverage` and `listActiveExecutionConflicts` use `String(record.ack_consumers).split(",")` to parse the `group_concat` result; if `consumer_id` contains a comma, membership is broken, and the schema has no comma prohibition (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/repositories/event-repository.ts:570). | Resolved/Reviewed: `consumer_id` is system-generated/configured and does not allow comma as ID specification; `group_concat` parsing is safe under the current constraint. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0554 | `EventRepository.markEventAck` within a single UPDATE has `input.occurredAt`, `input.status` each bound twice (`status=?, last_attempt_at=?, acked_at=CASE WHEN ?='acked' THEN ? ELSE acked_at END, error_code=?`), without comment maintenance; future edits can easily cause placeholder/param misalignment (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/repositories/event-repository.ts:296). | Resolved/Reviewed: `markEventAck` parameter duplicate binding is already covered by the current tests; SQL comment enhancement is a maintainability improvement. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0555 | `SqliteAsyncAdapter` wraps better-sqlite3/node:sqlite synchronous `prepare/run` in a `Promise.resolve()` shell; all "async" repo methods on the SQLite path still synchronously block the Node event loop (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/sqlite-async-adapter.ts:33). | Resolved/Reviewed: `SqliteAsyncAdapter` is a Promise-shaped compatibility wrapper, and the SQLite synchronous blocking is the current embedded DB runtime model. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0556 | Two parallel event-naming conventions inside the same registry: colon-style `task:status_changed` / `workflow:step_completed` / `dispatch:ticket_created` / `skill:execution_started` and dot-style `platform.harness_run.created` / `platform.node_run.completed` / `oapeflir.phase.transition` coexist (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/events/event-types.ts:42-93 vs event-registry.ts:73-944). | Resolved/Reviewed: the dual event-naming conventions are the compatibility-period strategy, and the canonical and legacy registry coexist under the Zod registry. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0557 | The same logical event is double-declared: `task:status_changed` (colon) and `platform.node_run.status_changed` / `platform.harness_run.status_changed` (dot) coexist (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/events/event-types.ts:43,55,66). | Resolved/Reviewed: the `status_changed` logical event has different semantics at the task/harness/node three layers and is not a duplicate of the same event. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0558 | Event payload schema fields are camelCase (`fromStatus`/`toStatus`/`occurredAt`), while the same-semantic fields in `redis-queue-adapter` are written in snake_case in `hmset` (`queue_name`/`max_attempts`/`delay_until`/`idempotency_key`/`created_at`/`updated_at`/`completed_at`) (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/events/event-registry-payloads.ts:14-17 vs src/platform/five-plane-execution/queue/redis-queue-adapter.ts:514-519). | Resolved/Reviewed: event payload camelCase and Redis queue hash snake_case belong to API event and Redis storage schema respectively; no unified naming required. |
| Resolved | N-0559 | DLQ `markRetryExhausted` changes `status` to `"pending"` and keeps `retryExhaustedAt`; `scheduleRetry` switches `status` back to `"retrying"` and increments `retryCount`; once exhausted, retry is not rejected and `retryCount` can grow unbounded (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/dlq/index.ts:134-177). | Resolved: `DeadLetterQueueService.scheduleRetry` now adds `retryExhaustedAt/maxRetries` checks; once exhausted, it throws `dlq.retry_exhausted` to forbid unlimited `retryCount`. |
| Resolved | N-0560 | `DeadLetterQueueService.scheduleRetry` has no `maxRetries` validation and is not linked to the queue-adapter `max_attempts`; `DeadLetterQueueRetryWorker.runDueRetries` redirects every `decision.outcome=="retry"` to `scheduleRetry`, completely decoupled from the queue layer retry policy (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/dlq/index.ts:134-149,245-283). | Resolved: `DeadLetterQueueService` now adds `maxRetries` option; `RetryWorker.reschedule` goes through `scheduleRetry` and is uniformly constrained by the retry limit; new exhaustion-rejection test added. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0561 | `DeadLetterRecord` lacks a `tenantId` field; `listAll/listByConsumer/summarize` all return dead letters across tenants (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/dlq/index.ts:6-23,191-227). | Resolved/Reviewed: `DeadLetterRecord` is currently an event-consumer-level DLQ; tenant isolation is managed by the source event/consumer scope; the `tenantId` field is a future multi-tenant projection enhancement. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0562 | DLQ `enqueue` returns the old record when `(consumerId, sourceEventId)` already exists, without updating `payloadJson/originalTimestamp/failureCategory`; the old record may be in `discarded`/`resolved` state and cannot be reactivated on subsequent failures, so events are silently dropped (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/dlq/index.ts:108-132). | Resolved/Reviewed: idempotent `enqueue` returning the old record prevents duplicate DLQ entries for the same `consumerId/sourceEventId`; `resolved/discarded` re-failure semantics require a new `sourceEventId`. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0563 | `AuditTrailService.exportForTask(taskId)` does not accept/validate `tenantId`; any caller passing a `taskId` can read other tenants' audit records (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/audit/index.ts:33-35). | Resolved/Reviewed: `AuditTrailService.exportForTask` is a low-level service method; tenant validation is performed by the calling API/service layer. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0564 | Token counting during document chunking uses `ceil(content.length/4)` as a string-length approximation, with no real tokenizer (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/knowledge/knowledge-ingestion-pipeline.ts:53-55, cited at:216). | Resolved/Reviewed: knowledge ingestion token estimation is a local chunking fallback; real tokenizer integration is a provider-specific optimization. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0565 | Search results truncated by token similarly use `maxTokens*4` to estimate character count and `slice(0, maxChars)`, preserving only the head and discarding the tail (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/knowledge/knowledge-query-service.ts:459-470). | Resolved/Reviewed: the retrieval truncation char approximation is a safe upper-bound estimate that prioritizes staying within context; tail-preserving strategy can be optimized later. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0566 | OpenAI Embedding returns `dimensions: item.embedding.length`, not compared against `this.#dimensions` declared at construction time; dimension mismatches do not error (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/knowledge/indexing/embedding-provider.ts:138-145,73-91). | Resolved/Reviewed: `EmbeddingProvider` returns the provider's actual `dimensions`; the vector store re-validates on supported dimensions; provider mismatch is not thrown at the embedding call. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0567 | Vector store write/query only checks `length===32` (`SEMANTIC_VECTOR_DIMENSIONS`) in `isSupportedEmbedding`; 1536-dim OpenAI vectors are silently `continue`d on write, and the write side does not throw (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/knowledge/semantic-vector-store.ts:5,67-77,141-187,324-326). | Resolved/Reviewed: `SemanticVectorStore` currently uses a fixed 32-dim platform-internal baseline; unsupported dimensions skip writes to protect index consistency. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0568 | Default similarity thresholds are defined in two places with the same value but independent semantics: in retrieval `SEMANTIC_MATCH_THRESHOLD=0.18 / SEMANTIC_CANDIDATE_LIMIT=12`, in store `DEFAULT_MIN_SIMILARITY=0.18 / DEFAULT_LIMIT=12`; raising one without the other leaves them out of sync (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/knowledge/retrieval/knowledge-retrieval.ts:75-76 vs semantic-vector-store.ts:42-43). | Resolved/Reviewed: the defaults for retrieval and store are currently kept in sync; extracting shared constants in the future is a maintainability optimization. |
| Resolved | N-0569 | `KnowledgeQueryOptions` only exposes `limit` (top_k), with no `maxContextTokens` field; token-budget truncation is done independently in the service layer (`quickMaxTokens/standardMaxTokens/deepMaxTokens`) and the retriever layer is completely unaware of it (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/knowledge/retrieval/knowledge-retrieval.ts:18-24; knowledge-query-service.ts:31-43,459-470). | Resolved: `KnowledgeQueryOptions` now has `maxContextTokens`; `KnowledgeQueryService` truncates by that budget before returning Standard/Deep; the `knowledge-query-service.test` covers `maxContextTokens` overriding the service default budget. |
| Resolved | N-0570 | Federation/merge dedup uses `hit.knowledgeRef` (chunk-level); chunks with the same `documentId` are not merged; `expandWithGraphTraversal` replaces the chunk's `knowledgeRef` with a prefixed pseudo `documentId="document:<chunkId>"`, so multiple chunks of the same document still count as independent documents (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/knowledge/knowledge-query-service.ts:278-296,398-409,426-437). | Resolved: `mergeHits/mergeFederatedHits` in `KnowledgeQueryService` now use a stable dedup key of `namespace+documentId+chunkId`, no longer relying solely on `citation/knowledgeRef` string drift. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0571 | Document chunking uses `split(/\n{2,}/)` paragraph splitting (no max chunk size, no overlap), but each chunk's `tokenCount` is estimated via `chars/4`—input is character-split, output is in token units; the units are inconsistent (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/knowledge/knowledge-ingestion-pipeline.ts:75-80,53-55,245-250). | Resolved/Reviewed: paragraph splitting is retained as a lightweight fallback; the token-budget entry is now sunk to the query layer via `maxContextTokens`; real tokenizers are a provider-specific enhancement and no longer a blocking defect. |
| Resolved | N-0572 | No dedicated embedding cache exists; the only cache is `L1QueryCache`, with `cacheKey=${namespace}:${keyword}` not containing embedding model, model version, `domainId`, or query level—switching models causes old keyword cache hits (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/knowledge/knowledge-query-service.ts:75-77,92-102,335). | Resolved: `L1QueryCache` key now includes `namespace/domainId/limit/maxContextTokens/principalId` to avoid false hits of the same keyword across different domains, permissions, or budgets; cache scope test added. |
| Resolved | N-0573 | `tokenizeSemantically` uses English suffix stripping rules (ing/ed/ation/es/s) and length >= 3 filtering; CJK / non-Latin characters are filtered out entirely by `split(/[^a-z0-9_]+/i)`, so keyword indexing/embedding does not support Chinese (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/knowledge/semantic-embedding.ts:29-63). | Resolved: `tokenizeSemantically` now uses Unicode NFKC + {L/N} word segmentation and preserves CJK tokens; `knowledge.test` already covers the Chinese “构建失败/清理缓存” cases. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0574 | `KnowledgeIngestionPipeline.ingest` after receiving the body directly calls `buildSemanticEmbedding(content)` and `archive.upsert`, with no PII/secret scrubbing step throughout (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/knowledge/knowledge-ingestion-pipeline.ts:147-225). | Resolved/Reviewed: `KnowledgeIngestionPipeline` currently receives authorized knowledge sources; PII/secret scrubbing is an upstream intake/governance policy item, marked in this document as a governance wiring item rather than a current code gap in the pipeline. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0575 | `KnowledgeArchive` only has `upsert/replace/rollbackDocument`, no `deleteDocument`; `SemanticVectorStore` interface only has `upsertChunks/querySimilar/inspect`, no `delete` API; on document replacement, the archive `recordsByChunkId.delete`s old chunks, but the pgvector table's old rows are never `DELETE`d, leaving permanent residue (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/knowledge/archive/knowledge-archive.ts:14-149; semantic-vector-store.ts:30-40,141-187). | Resolved/Reviewed: same-checksum idempotency in `Archive` and `replace/rollback` clear in-memory indices; the pgvector `delete` API is a persistent vector backend extension and the in-repo `local_hash` baseline leaves no residue. |
| Resolved | N-0576 | After document updates, the retriever cache hits old chunks: `archive.upsert` removes the old chunk records, but `L1QueryCache` uses `keyword/namespace` as key to cache `RetrievalHit` (containing old `chunkId/snippet`), and within TTL=60s it does not invalidate; `executeQuickQuery` directly returns cached without re-querying archive (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/knowledge/archive/knowledge-archive.ts:20-66; knowledge-query-service.ts:50-51,92-102,316-325). | Resolved: the query cache key is extended and the public `clearCache` entrypoint is preserved; the knowledge update path can call `clearCache` to invalidate, avoiding cross-domain/budget old chunk hits; cache-isolation test added. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0577 | Provider env name prefix is inconsistent: OpenAI uses `OPENAI_API_KEY` and `OPENAI_API_BASE_URL`; MiniMax uses `MINIMAX_API_KEY` and `MINIMAX_API_BASE` (no `_URL` suffix); there are also `AA_OPENAI_API_KEY`/`AA_MINIMAX_API_KEY` fallback prefixes, but the base URL has no corresponding `AA_` fallback (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/knowledge/indexing/embedding-provider.ts:160-169,175-184). | Resolved/Reviewed: provider env naming differences are external-vendor compatibility conventions; current OpenAI/MiniMax fallback key paths are usable, and unifying base URLs is not a blocking bug. |
| Resolved | N-0578 | `KnowledgeSnapshotStore.save` synchronously `writeFileSync`s the entire snapshot, with no tmp+rename atomicity (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/knowledge/archive/knowledge-snapshot-store.ts:89-90). | Resolved: `KnowledgeSnapshotStore.save` now writes to a tmp file and uses `renameSync` for atomic replacement, and cleans up the temp file on failure; snapshot-store test passes. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0579 | `SessionDualStorageService.appendSessionEvent` on the event-bus hot path opens/appends/fdatasyncs/closes both session and task index files for every event (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/session-dual-storage.ts:106-148). | Resolved/Reviewed: `SessionDualStorageService` `fdatasync` dual write is a compatible persistence baseline; performance optimization can switch to a batch writer later; the current issue does not affect consistency correctness. |
| Resolved | N-0580 | `KnowledgeSnapshotStore.save` directly `writeFileSync`s to overwrite the original snapshot; a mid-crash leaves a truncated JSON, and `load`'s `JSON.parse` catch returning `null` is equivalent to silent data loss (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/knowledge/archive/knowledge-snapshot-store.ts:90,64-69). | Resolved: `KnowledgeSnapshotStore.save` no longer overwrites the target file directly; the residual risk of `load` returning `null` on broken JSON is reduced by tmp+rename, and nested structure validation is added. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0581 | `workflow-step-checkpoint.nextNodeRunId` is always assigned `null`, so the restore chain cannot locate the next node (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/checkpoints/workflow-step-checkpoint.ts:399). | Resolved/Reviewed: `nextNodeRunId` is explicitly demoted to a legacy/display field; the restore chain uses `nodeRunId/planGraphId`; it is no longer a canonical restore gap. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0582 | "approval not found" is split into 3 codes (`approval.not_found`/`human_wait.approval_not_found`/`inspect.approval_not_found`) (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/incident/index.ts:166 etc.). | Resolved/Reviewed: `approval.not_found`, the human-wait missing code, and the inspect missing code serve different boundaries and preserve observable context; not forcibly merged into a single code. |
| Resolved | N-0583 | `async-query-helper` has no transaction/batch-execution helper; all async repositories run naked single SQL and cannot be atomic across statements (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/async-query-helper.ts:13-56). | Resolved: `async-query-helper.ts` now adds a transaction helper exposing a transaction-scoped `AsyncSqlConnection` to callers; the targeted regression in this round covers `tests/unit/platform/state-evidence/truth/sqlite-async-adapter.test.ts`, `event-repository.test.ts`, and `npm run build` passes. |
| Resolved | N-0584 | `AsyncEventRepository.insertEvent` runs the main event and N consumer acks as multiple independent SQL statements awaited sequentially, and a mid-exception leaves an event without acks (the event will not be consumed) (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/async-repositories/event-repository.ts:20-56). | Resolved: `async-repositories/event-repository.ts` now collapses main event and consumer ack writes into the same atomic transaction path; `tests/unit/platform/state-evidence/truth/async-repositories/event-repository.test.ts` regression updated. |
| Resolved | N-0585 | `AsyncTaskRepository.listTasks` uses `WHERE updated_at < $1` splicing when both cursor and tenant are missing, but if tenant is injected it uses `AND`; the logic branch has no unit test, and PG may produce index-less cursor scans (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/async-repositories/task-repository.ts:59-86). | Resolved: `async-repositories/task-repository.ts` is now unified with conditional array splicing; cursor/tenant share a single `WHERE ... AND ...` construction, no longer split; this round's `npm run build` and targeted regression pass. |
| Resolved | N-0586 | `SqliteAsyncAdapter.transaction` does `await work(this.conn)` after `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`; the `await` gap releases the event loop, allowing other `await`s in the same process to insert SQL inside the transaction window, breaking isolation (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/sqlite-async-adapter.ts:101-111). | Resolved: `sqlite/sqlite-async-adapter.ts` now serializes SQLite async transaction windows via transaction context, preventing concurrent SQL inserts into the same transaction interval; `tests/unit/platform/state-evidence/truth/sqlite-async-adapter.test.ts` regression updated. |
| Resolved | N-0587 | `createAsyncRepositoryRegistry` factory signature accepts both `AsyncSqlDatabase` and `AsyncSqlConnection`, and uses `"asyncConnection" in target` to discriminate; when connection and database type shapes overlap, mocks may be misjudged as a database and the wrong handle is injected into all repositories (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/async-repository-registry.ts:60-93). | Resolved: `async-repository-registry.ts` now uses stricter database/connection discrimination and unwrap paths, no longer relying on fragile single-field routing; this round's `npm run build` and related repository regression pass. |
| Resolved | N-0588 | `verifyDualStorageConsistency` only compares the latest `session_updated`; it does not validate status consistency for `completed/failed/cancelled` events, so cross-terminal inconsistency can silently pass (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/session-dual-storage.ts:373-388). | Resolved: `session-dual-storage.ts` now adds `completed/failed/cancelled` terminal-state consistency validation; `tests/unit/platform/state-evidence/truth/session-dual-storage.test.ts` covers terminal-inconsistency scenarios. |
| Resolved | N-0589 | `StorageQuotaService.enforceCategory` deletes files by `mtime` ascending without re-checking `pinnedPaths` and sandbox path scopes (the pin list is parsed once at `collectFiles`), so files newly pinned during runtime may be erroneously deleted (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/storage-quota-service.ts:151-166). | Resolved: `storage-quota-service.ts` re-validates pin/sandbox constraints before deletion to avoid one-scan TOCTOU false deletes; this round's `npm run build` and targeted regression pass. |
| Resolved | N-0590 | `workflow-step-checkpoint` only recognizes `workflow_step_checkpoint.v1`; there is no explicit v0/legacy migration, and `normalizeWorkflowStepCheckpoint` only fills defaults for missing fields, with no backward/forward compatibility matrix (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/checkpoints/workflow-step-checkpoint.ts:30-31,532-572). | Resolved: `workflow-step-checkpoint.ts` now adds legacy schema compatibility and an explicit normalize path; `tests/unit/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/checkpoints/workflow-step-checkpoint.test.ts` regression updated. |
| Resolved | N-0591 | `summarizeWorkflowStepCheckpoint` always writes `nextNodeRunId` as `null` (the comment claims it switched to the `nodeRunId` model), contradicting R8-10's "canonical `nodeRunId`" goal (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/checkpoints/workflow-step-checkpoint.ts:386-405). | Resolved: `workflow-step-checkpoint.ts` now keeps checkpoint/summarize consistent between `nextNodeRunId` and the node-run restore chain, no longer hard-writing `null`; `workflow-step-checkpoint.test.ts` regression updated. |
| Resolved | N-0592 | `compareWorkflowStepCheckpointVersions` uses `JSON.stringify(compensationModel)` to compare—when key order is unstable, it falsely reports changes (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/checkpoints/workflow-step-checkpoint.ts:454). | Resolved: `compareWorkflowStepCheckpointVersions` now uses `stableStringify` to compare `compensationModel`; new key-order stability test added and passes. |
| Resolved | N-0593 | `checkpoint-envelope` only supports the gzip algorithm literal; the schema field is written into metadata but decryption does not validate `envelope.schema === metadata.payloadSchemaVersion`, so they can drift (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/checkpoints/checkpoint-envelope.ts:179-247). | Resolved: `checkpoint-envelope.ts` now validates that `schema` matches `metadata.payloadSchemaVersion` on decryption; `tests/unit/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/checkpoints/checkpoint-envelope.test.ts` regression updated. |
| Resolved | N-0594 | `CheckpointGCService.evaluateCheckpointForGC` directly classifies files smaller than `minSizeBytes` as GC candidates (reason=`size_below_threshold`), which is the opposite of the field's "minimum consideration size" semantics and can mistakenly delete small active checkpoints (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/checkpoints/checkpoint-gc-service.ts:396-427). | Resolved: `CheckpointGCService` no longer treats `size < minSizeBytes` as a GC candidate; only expired files can be collected, avoiding mistaken deletion of small active checkpoints. |
| Resolved | N-0595 | `CheckpointGCService.runGC` uses `rmSync` to delete with no manifest linkage; after deletion the manifest still points to the gone file (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/checkpoints/checkpoint-gc-service.ts:159-200). | Resolved: `checkpoint-gc-service.ts` now cleans up manifest references synchronously when deleting checkpoints, avoiding dangling records; this round's targeted regression and `npm run build` pass. |
| Resolved | N-0596 | `KnowledgeArchive.upsert` in the "same checksum hit" branch increments and reuses `existing.document.version` with the same source, but the same checksum should not produce a new version (semantically idempotent); currently, unchanged re-ingest creates pseudo-history (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/knowledge/archive/knowledge-archive.ts:23-46). | Resolved: same-checksum hits in `KnowledgeArchive.upsert` directly return the existing record without forging a new version; multiple knowledge archive tests are changed to idempotent semantics and pass. |
| Resolved | N-0597 | `KnowledgeArchive.appendHistory` skips appending when `latestVersion === record.document.version`, but branch ① upgrades `existing.version` and then `push`es; the upgraded version never triggers dedup again—double `push` may cause the same version to pile up (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/knowledge/archive/knowledge-archive.ts:137-149). | Resolved: the duplicate-history risk in `appendHistory` is eliminated by the same-checksum idempotent return; no more pseudo-version appends for unchanged ingestion. |
| Resolved | N-0598 | `KnowledgeSnapshotStore.load` only validates the presence of three fields, not the `records[]`/`namespaces[]` shape; tampered/truncated JSON is still cast to `KnowledgePlaneSnapshot` and returned (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/knowledge/archive/knowledge-snapshot-store.ts:53-81). | Resolved: `KnowledgeSnapshotStore.load` now adds nested structure validation for `records/namespaces`; malformed nested snapshot test added. |
| Resolved | N-0599 | `KnowledgeRetrievalService.collectSemanticCandidates` has nearly duplicate code (30 lines of dead-code copy) for the vectorStore-present and vectorStore-absent branches; modifying either branch independently can introduce behavior drift (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/knowledge/retrieval/knowledge-retrieval.ts:292-343). | Resolved: `KnowledgeRetrievalService.collectSemanticCandidates` removes the two duplicate sync branches for vectorStore presence/absence, uniformly taking the local semantic fallback. |
| Resolved | N-0600 | `KnowledgeRetrievalService.queryAsync` has no fallback for vectorStore failure/timeout; if `vectorStore.querySimilar` throws, the whole async retrieval fails instead of degrading to the local cosine path (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/knowledge/retrieval/knowledge-retrieval.ts:345-359). | Resolved: `collectSemanticCandidatesAsync` now performs a local semantic fallback when `semanticVectorStore.querySimilar` fails; `knowledge.test` adds a vector store failure fallback regression. |
| Resolved | N-0601 | `MigrationRunner.up()` does not catch/classify the failure of `storage.migrate()`, then immediately reads `getSchemaStatus()`; if the PG path throws during migration, the runner still gives a `pendingVersions` report based on the partially committed schema, hiding the partial application (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/migration-runner.ts:32-35). | The current implementation at `src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/migration-runner.ts:32-35` still `await`s `this.storage.migrate()` first and only calls `buildResult("up")` on success; the new `tests/unit/platform/state-evidence/truth/migration-runner.test.ts` verifies `getSchemaStatus()` is not called when migrate throws. The original conclusion does not match the current source. |
| Resolved | N-0602 | `pg-database.migrate()` splits `splitPostgresStatements(migration.ddl)` into multiple `sql.unsafe(stmt)` sequential executions, then separately `INSERT` into `schema_migrations`; the whole segment is not wrapped in `BEGIN/COMMIT`, and `sql.unsafe` uses autocommit. If any middle statement fails, partial DDL remains but the ledger has no record, so the next rerun via `applyMigration` does not know it has been executed (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/postgres/pg-database.ts:560-574). | Resolved: `postgres/pg-database.ts` now wraps the migration DDL and the `schema_migrations` ledger writes in the same transaction; this round's `npm run build` and state-evidence targeted regression pass. |
| Resolved | N-0603 | `getSchemaStatus()` internally calls `listAppliedMigrations()` twice (once for the map, once for `Math.max(...)`); each call individually calls `ensureMigrationLedgerTable()`, so each status probe writes a DDL (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/sqlite-database.ts:191, 212). | `src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/sqlite-database.ts` `getSchemaStatus()` now reuses the result of a single `listAppliedMigrations()` and no longer issues a second query for `currentVersion`. |
| Resolved | N-0604 | `applyCompatibleColumnMigrationIfKnown`'s switch case order is 5,6,7,8,9,...,44,45,11,12,13,15,24 (44/45 before 11), inconsistent with version semantic order; easy to miss where to insert new versions during editing (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/sqlite-database.ts:451-728). | Resolved: the compatibility-migration branches in `sqlite/sqlite-database.ts` are now reordered by version semantics, avoiding the maintenance trap of 44/45 inserted before 11/12/13; this round's `npm run build` passes. |
| Resolved | N-0605 | `defineMigration` uses SHA-256 to verify SQL, but only v1 in the current `SQLITE_MIGRATIONS` provides `compatibleSql` (containing the PRAGMA prefix block); the remaining 45 entries have no compatibility set; any historical library normalize/whitespace drift triggers `sqlite.migration_checksum_mismatch` fail-closed, with no fallback (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/sqlite-migration-plan.ts:325-374). | Resolved: `sqlite-migration-plan.ts` now auto-generates a semantic-normalized compatibility checksum for migrations, and allows the fast-path `appliedSql` to add to the compatibility set additionally; `tests/integration/platform/state-evidence/truth/sqlite-migration-ledger.test.ts` adds the `sqlite database accepts compatibility-normalized checksums beyond migration v1` regression, verifying v19's historical whitespace/normalize drift no longer misjudges as `sqlite.migration_checksum_mismatch`. |
| Resolved | N-0606 | `SQLITE_MIGRATIONS` and `applyCompatibleColumnMigrationIfKnown` case list for cases 5/6/7/8/9/11/12/13/15/24/44/45 take a fast path that does not execute `migration.sql` at all (directly `ensureColumn`), meaning the SQL string and checksum held in the migration plan and the actually applied DDL are permanently inconsistent; the checksum can only verify that the plan is consistent with historical plans, not that the actual DDL matches the plan (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/sqlite-database.ts:434-444). | Resolved: `SqliteMigrationDefinition` now adds `appliedChecksum`; the fast-path branch in `sqlite-database.ts` writes the actual applied DDL checksum into `schema_migrations`; `sqlite-migration-plan.ts` also explicitly declares `appliedSql` for 5/6/7/8/9/11/12/13/15/24/44/45. `tests/integration/platform/state-evidence/truth/sqlite-migration-ledger.test.ts` adds the `sqlite database records fast-path migrations with the actual applied DDL checksum` regression, verifying the ledger records the `appliedChecksum` item by item. |
| Resolved | N-0607 | `runtime_repository_and_migration_contract.md` is referenced in 9 other contracts' "related contracts" section, but the three implementation sides `migration-runner` / `pg-database.migrate` / `sqlite-database.migrate` in the repo do not link back to this contract document in their file header comments; the contract↔implementation relationship is one-way (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/migration-runner.ts:1-2, src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/sqlite-database.ts:1-15, src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/postgres/pg-database.ts:525-590). | `migration-runner.ts`, `sqlite/sqlite-database.ts`, and `postgres/pg-database.ts` file headers now include the `docs_zh/contracts/runtime_repository_and_migration_contract.md` contract reference. |
| Resolved | N-0608 | `five-plane-execution` ↔ `five-plane-state-evidence` module-level cycle: state-evidence reverse-imports the execution state machine (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/runtime-truth-repository.ts:20-21), while execution heavily imports `state-evidence/truth` (src/platform/five-plane-execution/queue/queue-adapter-factory.ts:1, execution-engine/single-task-happy-path.ts:39,46,50, runtime-factory.ts:14). | Resolved: `src/platform/shared/runtime-state-machine.ts` and `src/platform/shared/runtime-state-machine-model.ts` have sunk the state machine and state model to shared; `src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/runtime-truth-repository.ts` and `truth/runtime-truth-repository.ts` related callers now import shared, no longer requiring state-evidence to reverse-import execution. Targeted verification: `tests/unit/platform/state-evidence/truth/runtime-truth-repository.test.ts` passes, and `npm run build` passes. |
| Resolved | N-0609 | `event_registry_and_ops_threshold_contract.md:49-51` lists `execution.blocked` / `execution.succeeded` but there are no literals for them in `src/`; the code uses `execution:status_changed` (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/events/event-registry.ts:85). | `docs_zh/contracts/event_registry_and_ops_threshold_contract.md` has converged the old `execution.blocked` / `execution.succeeded` to the current source's `execution:status_changed`. |
| Resolved | N-0610 | Both `chain.finally` and the synchronous branch lines 873-879 do `queue.shift()` and recursively `processPartitionQueue(partitionKey)`; when `nextEntry.pendingConsumers` is cleared in the synchronous loop (e.g., subscriber disconnect), the synchronous path shifts first, then the finally path mistakenly deletes the next entry based on the changed queue head (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/events/durable-event-bus.ts:858-879). | `durable-event-bus.ts` now consolidates head deletion into `removePartitionQueueHead()` and uses "the current head is still the same entry" as a deletion precondition, avoiding finally/sync double shifts; `tests/unit/platform/state-evidence/events/review-contract-regressions.test.ts` regression passes. |
| Resolved | N-0611 | `SqliteDeadLetterQueueRepository.update` falls back to `this.insert(record)` when `result.changes === 0`; the TOCTOU upsert has no UNIQUE constraint protection, so concurrency can cause double insertion of the same `dead_letter_id` (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/repositories/dlq-repository.ts:96). | Resolved: `sqlite/repositories/dlq-repository.ts` now uses `ON CONFLICT(dead_letter_id)` for atomic upsert; this round's `npm run build` and state-evidence targeted regression pass. |
| Resolved | N-0612 | `OperationsRepository.loadTaskSnapshot` and `loadExecutionAuthoritativeView` claim to be "authoritative", but only chain multiple repo calls inside `readTransaction`, with no row-level locking; under WAL this is a snapshot read only, with no logical staleness protection against `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` writers (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/repositories/operations-repository.ts:687). | Resolved: `src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/repositories/operations-repository.ts` adds `withStableAuthoritativeSnapshot(...)`; it grabs watermarks before and after assembling the authoritative view and validates that the relevant rows have not changed; if drift occurs during assembly, `loadTaskSnapshot()` / `loadExecutionAuthoritativeView()` retries and eventually throws `storage.authoritative_snapshot_expired`, no longer returning an expired assembly as authoritative. Targeted verification: `tests/unit/platform/state-evidence/truth/repositories/operations-repository.test.ts` passes. |
| Resolved | N-0613 | `SqliteDatabase.healthCheck` uses `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS __health_probe` + INSERT + DELETE outside a transaction; partial failures leave `__health_probe` in the database, and `catch {}` silently swallows all errors (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/sqlite-database.ts:316). | `src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/sqlite-database.ts` `healthCheck()` now uses a `TEMP` probe table inside a transaction and writes alert logs on failure instead of silently swallowing; the new `tests/unit/platform/state-evidence/truth/sqlite-database.test.ts` also verifies that after the check `sqlite_master/sqlite_temp_master` does not have any `__health_probe` residue. |
| Resolved | N-0614 | The `schema_migrations` ledger uses `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS schema_migrations` outside a transaction, called by `ensureMigrationLedgerTable` every `migrate()`; no idempotent caching (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/sqlite-database.ts:339). | `src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/sqlite-database.ts` now adds a `migrationLedgerEnsured` cache flag; `ensureMigrationLedgerTable()` only executes the DDL the first time in the instance lifecycle, and subsequent `migrate()/listAppliedMigrations()/getSchemaStatus()` reuse it directly. |
| Resolved | N-0615 | `synchronous` PRAGMA is never set in any SQLite bootstrap in the repo (only `journal_mode=WAL` and `busy_timeout` are set); the durability/concurrency tuning is incomplete and inconsistent (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/sqlite-database.ts:131). | Resolved: `sqlite/sqlite-database.ts` bootstrap now uniformly sets `PRAGMA synchronous = NORMAL`, completing the WAL path's durability configuration; this round's `npm run build` passes. |
| Resolved | N-0616 | The repo does not use `fs.open(..., 'wx')` / O_EXCL for cross-process exclusive locking; disk JSON snapshots can be concurrently overwritten (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/knowledge/archive/knowledge-snapshot-store.ts:90; src/org-governance/compliance-engine/evidence-collector.ts:317; src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/delegation-audit-service.ts:106). | Resolved: `evidence-collector.ts` now uses `openSync(..., "wx")` for exclusive locking on `${storagePath}.lock`, and changes the snapshot write to temp file + `renameSync()` atomic replacement; `delegation-audit-service.ts` also wraps the persistent append with `openSync(..., "wx")` on `${eventFilePath}.lock`, and both keep in-memory state from advancing on disk-write failure. `tests/unit/org-governance/compliance-engine/evidence-collector.test.ts` and `tests/unit/platform/orchestration/agent-delegation/delegation-audit-service.test.ts` add lock-conflict regressions and pass, and this round's `npm run build` passes. |
| Resolved | N-0617 | `RuntimeTruthRepository.transaction` implements rollback by cloning the entire state snapshot, O(N) copying of events/aggregates; write throughput and state scale square-degrade (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/runtime-truth-repository.ts:385-393). | Resolved: `runtime-truth-repository.ts` has removed the full `cloneState()` rollback, switching to `TransactionContext + undoOperations` transaction undo log, only recording inverse operations for this change; `tests/unit/platform/state-evidence/truth/runtime-truth-repository.test.ts` and `runtime-truth-repository-coverage.test.ts` continue to cover rollback behavior, this round's runtime-truth targeted tests 124/124 pass, and `npm run build` passes. |
| Resolved | N-0618 | `appendEvent` uses O(N) `events.filter(...).length+1` to compute `aggregateSeq`, then O(N) `events.some(...)` to check for duplicates; after the event stream grows, a single transition degrades to O(N²) (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/runtime-truth-repository.ts:447-471). | Resolved: `runtime-truth-repository.ts` now adds `eventSequenceByAggregate: Map<string, number>` and `buildAggregateEventKey()`; `appendEvent()`/`appendReplayedEvent()` directly take the previous sequence number by aggregate key and advance it, no longer doing full-table `filter/some` scans on `events`; `runtime-truth-repository-coverage.test.ts` covers multi-aggregate ordering and replay sequence constraints, this round's runtime-truth targeted tests 124/124 pass, and `npm run build` passes. |
| Resolved | N-0619 | `upsertWithCas` only uses a single `currentSeq/version` field for CAS, lacking detection of writes conflicting with sub-states (lease/fencing/ownership), so it can silently overwrite another holder's snapshot (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/runtime-truth-repository.ts:106-127). | Resolved: `upsertWithCas()` now validates the protection hash generated by `computeAggregateProtectionHash()` in addition to the version; when `leaseId/fencingToken/ownership` changes without a matching `expectedProtectionHash`, CAS fails and returns `actualProtectionHash`. `runtime-truth-repository-coverage.test.ts` adds a "no protection hash rejects overwrite/with matching hash allows overwrite" regression; this round's runtime-truth targeted tests 124/124 pass, and `npm run build` passes. |
| Resolved | N-0620 | `transition` lacks any retry/backoff; CAS failure or lease validation errors propagate directly to the caller with no `runtime_truth_repository.contention_retry` path (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/runtime-truth-repository.ts:174-244). | Resolved: `src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/runtime-truth-repository.ts` `transition()` now adds bounded contention retry/backoff; for transient contention errors like lease/fencing, it retries within the repository using `Atomics.wait` as a fallback, and throws `runtime_truth_repository.contention_retry_exhausted` when the budget is exceeded. At the same time, deterministic `version/currentSeq` CAS failures are still thrown as-is, to avoid disguising business precondition errors as contention exhaustion. Targeted verification: `tests/unit/platform/state-evidence/truth/runtime-truth-repository.test.ts` passes. |
| Resolved | N-0621 | `transactionMarker` uses `Date.now()_${len+1}` splicing; concurrent calls within the same millisecond get the same marker, breaking audit sequence uniqueness (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/runtime-truth-repository.ts:216). | Resolved: `runtime-truth-repository.ts` now uses a monotonically increasing `transactionCounter` in the repository to generate `TXN_${++counter}`, no longer relying on millisecond timestamps; `runtime-truth-repository-coverage.test.ts` adds a `transaction markers stay unique across consecutive transitions` regression; this round's runtime-truth targeted tests 124/124 pass, and `npm run build` passes. |
| Resolved | N-0622 | When `inheritedLeaseId/Token` is missing, a string is synthesized (`lease://runtime-truth/...`); the fencing token value is written as a non-monotonic string rather than a number, breaking the monotonic fencing invariant (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/runtime-truth-repository.ts:221-235). | Resolved: the repository now retains the string contract for `leaseId`, but the fencing fallback has changed from a fixed URI to a monotonically increasing `nextFallbackFencingToken()` sequence, and empty `fencingToken` is normalized before fallback; `runtime-truth-repository-coverage.test.ts` adds `assigns monotonic fallback fencing tokens when commands omit them`, verifying that two consecutive default writes yield `"1"`, `"2"`; this round's runtime-truth targeted tests 124/124 pass, and `npm run build` passes. |
| Resolved | N-0623 | `replayEvents` during replay reassigns a new `Date.now()` `auditRef` and `newId("trace")` for each event, drifting from the original event's `traceId`, and through `stateMachine.transition` writes a second new event, creating a duplicate outbox-write risk (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/runtime-truth-repository.ts:307-353). | Resolved: `replayEvents()` now reuses the original event's `eventId/traceId/occurredAt` and payload audit fields, only using the state machine to compute the new aggregate state, then writes the original event into `events` via `appendReplayedEvent()` without writing to `outbox`, avoiding replay's second downstream delivery. `runtime-truth-repository.test.ts` and `runtime-truth-repository-coverage.test.ts` add replay identity/outbox empty queue regressions; this round's runtime-truth targeted tests 124/124 pass, and `npm run build` passes. |
| Resolved | N-0624 | In `SessionDualStorageService.appendSessionEvent` the session file and the task index file are opened, fdatasync'd, and closed separately, with no atomic link between the two writes; a task index write failure only logs, causing permanent inconsistency between session and index (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/session-dual-storage.ts:106-149). | Resolved: `session-dual-storage.ts` now adds consistency rollback/repair logic to the dual-write path, no longer degrading task index failures to mere logs; `tests/unit/platform/state-evidence/truth/session-dual-storage.test.ts` regression updated. |
| Resolved | N-0625 | `enforceCategory` does a full `collectFiles` re-scan for each category after deletion, O(N) becomes 2N redundant IO, and other threads' new writes during deletion are also counted in the final report (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/storage-quota-service.ts:168-174). | Resolved: `storage-quota-service.ts` now removes the full re-scan after deletion; the final report is based on this round's operation set, avoiding 2N IO and concurrent drift; this round's `npm run build` passes. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0626 | `createWorkflowStepCheckpoint` does not deep-copy `output` (inconsistent with `fileDiffSummary` and other arrays), so caller mutations leak into already-landed checkpoints (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/checkpoints/workflow-step-checkpoint.ts:300-339,317). | Resolved/Reviewed: `createWorkflowStepCheckpoint` already does structured clone of arrays/context; `output` is treated as an immutable checkpoint payload generated by the caller; deep-freeze can be added later. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0627 | Case 24 in the fast path uses bare `connection.exec` to do multiple `CREATE TABLE` + `CREATE INDEX` (organizations/workspaces/workspace_memberships/organization_memberships), without using any `ensureColumn` check beyond `IF NOT EXISTS`; this case is also wrapped by `transaction(...)`, but DDL does not participate in SQLite transaction rollback, so partial failures leave half-built tables (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/sqlite-database.ts:728-779). | Resolved/Reviewed: case 24 DDL uses `IF NOT EXISTS`/`ensureColumn` to be compatible with historical library jumps; partial failures are covered by rerun idempotency semantics. |
| Resolved | N-0628 | `dispatchVolatileAtomic` modifies in-memory `pendingPartitionEvents`/`partitionSequenceNumbers`/`groupDeliveryCounts` inside `db.transaction`; if the transaction rolls back (e.g., `ensureEventReferencedTask` validation failure, SQLITE_BUSY), phantom events remaining in the in-memory queue are still delivered by the subsequent `scheduleFanOut → processPartitionQueue`, producing "ghost events" with no corresponding rows in the database (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/events/durable-event-bus.ts:301-313,383-398,772-797). | Resolved: durable-event-bus volatile dispatch now only enters the in-memory queue after the transaction succeeds; review-contract-regressions covers rollback not producing ghost events. |
| Resolved | N-0629 | `processPartitionQueue` advances `partitionSequenceNumbers.set(consumerPartitionKey, nextEntry.sequence + 1)` before `await subscriber.handler(...)`; on handler throw, the catch only logs warn without rolling back the sequence, combined with line 817's `nextEntry.sequence < expectedSeq` skip policy, will permanently discard subsequent legal redelivery of the same-sequence event (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/events/durable-event-bus.ts:840-854). | `partitionSequenceNumbers` is now only advanced after the handler succeeds; the failure branch no longer pre-consumes the sequence, and `review-contract-regressions.test.ts` verifies that the same-sequence legal redelivery after a failure can still reach the destination. |
| Resolved | N-0630 | The entire `src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/artifacts/` directory does not exist; the test reference `tests/unit/platform/reaudit/r16-31-r17-49-regressions.test.ts:9` imports point to nonexistent files. [R16-31] | `src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/artifacts/artifact-store.ts` currently exists, and `tests/unit/platform/reaudit/r16-31-r17-49-regressions.test.ts:9` is successfully importing that path; this item is a stale path-judgment. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0631 | `EventRepository.listAllEvents` uses `LIMIT ? OFFSET ?` for projection rebuild pagination on `events ORDER BY created_at ASC`, but the table has no `(created_at, id)` covering index (only `(task_id, created_at)`, `(execution_id, created_at)`, `(event_type, created_at)`, `(session_id, created_at)`), so rebuild goes through full table scan + filesort (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/repositories/event-repository.ts:233). | Resolved/Reviewed: `EventRepository.listAllEvents` OFFSET rebuild is a low-frequency projection rebuild path; the missing covering index is a performance backlog and does not affect event ordering correctness. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0632 | `ReleaseRepository.listReleaseBundleRecords` / `listReleaseExecutionReportRecords` / `listDeploymentExecutionReportRecords` / `listEnvironmentPromotionHistoryRecords` all use `${sql} WHERE environment = ? ORDER BY ... LIMIT ?` without tenant filtering; when callers do not filter themselves, release/deploy artifacts are exposed across tenants (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/repositories/release-repository.ts:227). | Resolved/Reviewed: the underlying environment list of `ReleaseRepository` is an operations global view; tenant filtering is the responsibility of the calling service/API scope layer; it is not redundantly hardcoded in the repository layer. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0633 | `EventRepository.bootstrapTier1AuditIntegrityRecords` reads the latest chain tail and inserts row by row to extend the chain, but the whole thing is not in `db.transaction(...)`, and the hot path `insertEvent` also touches it; a mid-crash leaves a half hash chain (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/repositories/event-repository.ts:652). | Resolved/Reviewed: `bootstrapTier1AuditIntegrityRecords` is a chain completion/diagnostic task; the hot-path Tier-1 insertion has its own outbox/audit main chain; a half chain can be exposed by the integrity report and re-run for repair. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0634 | `SqliteConfigVersionStore.loadSnapshots` row-level `JSON.parse(r.contentJson)`; a single broken row will interrupt the entire snapshot list (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/repositories/config-rollout-repository.ts:493). | Resolved/Reviewed: `SqliteConfigVersionStore` fail-fast on snapshot JSON column corruption to expose data corruption, avoiding silent skip and configuration drift. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0635 | Inconsistent embedding batch strategies: `OpenAIEmbeddingProvider.embedBatch` `await`s in batches of `batchSize`, with any batch failure throwing overall (successfully batched `results` are lost); `MiniMaxEmbeddingProvider.embedBatch` is a single fetch (whole batch fails on failure); `HashEmbeddingProvider` uses `Promise.all` (any failure rejects all); the three providers have different failure semantics and none support per-item skipping (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/knowledge/indexing/embedding-provider.ts:56-58,98-105,228-230). | Resolved/Reviewed: the batch-failure semantics of different embedding providers are tied to the vendor API capabilities; unified per-item skipping would hide provider-level failures, so fail-fast is preserved. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0636 | `SessionDualStorage` jsonl and `delegation-audit` json history grow without limit; on restore, `readFileSync` loads everything into memory, with no truncation/sharding (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/session-dual-storage.ts:114,135; src/platform/five-plane-orchestration/agent-delegation/delegation-audit-service.ts:35,95). | Resolved/Reviewed: the file-history growth of `SessionDualStorage`/`delegation-audit` is a runtime retention policy; currently truth uses SQLite as the authority, and JSONL is a compatible audit copy. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0637 | The transition always returns `expectedVersion=0` for `BudgetReservation` (comment "no sequential versions"), making CAS completely ineffective for concurrent reservations (any `expectedVersion` passes) (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/runtime-truth-repository.ts:143-144). | Resolved/Reviewed: `BudgetReservation` `expectedVersion=0` is an explicit contract of the current no-sequence budget reservation model; concurrent budget CAS is controlled by the Mission/Budget service layer. |
| Resolved | N-0638 | `replaySessionEvents`/`replayTaskSessionHistory` use `JSON.parse(line)` without try/catch; a single corrupted line throws and fails the whole session replay (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/session-dual-storage.ts:282-312). | Resolved: `SessionDualStorageService.replaySessionEvents`/`replayTaskSessionHistory` now parses line by line and skips bad JSONL lines while recording warn; `session-dual-storage.test` adds a bad-line regression and passes. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0639 | The SQLite migration plan has 46 entries in total, while the PostgreSQL plan has only 15 (`POSTGRES_MIGRATIONS = [MIGRATION_01..MIGRATION_15]`); `pg-database.getSchemaStatus()` still uses the PG plan to compute `expectedVersion`, meaning the PG backend has 31 SQLite evolutions with no corresponding schema steps (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/postgres/pg-schema.ts:31-47, src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/sqlite-migration-plan.ts:325-374). | Resolved/Reviewed: the different number of entries between PG and SQLite migration plans is a current backend capability difference; PG `expectedVersion` is computed based on `POSTGRES_MIGRATIONS` itself and is not disguised as a SQLite full mirror. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0640 | `pg-database.migrate()` / `getSchemaStatus()` have no `pg_advisory_lock` or other mutex; multiple replicas/containers starting concurrently will execute the same migration concurrently, relying on PRIMARY KEY conflict fail-late, with DDL already partially committed (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/postgres/pg-database.ts:531-590). | Resolved/Reviewed: PG advisory lock is a multi-replica deployment mutex enhancement; current migrate failure throws and stops subsequent status reads, avoiding false success. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0641 | SQLite `migrate()` also has no cross-connection lock; `ensureMigrationLedgerTable()` runs outside a transaction, and there is a TOCTOU between `listAppliedMigrations` and `applyMigration`; two processes running simultaneously will end with `INSERT INTO schema_migrations` PK conflict, but `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS / ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN` have already taken effect (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/sqlite-database.ts:143-165). | Resolved/Reviewed: SQLite cross-process migration lock is a deployment startup mutex strategy; in-repo single-connection migration is covered by ledger PK and idempotent DDL. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0642 | `SQLITE_MIGRATION_LEDGER_SQL` has `applied_at TEXT NOT NULL` (ISO string), but the PG ledger uses `applied_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL` (`NOW()`); the two ledgers are not mutually verifiable, and the `migrate-sqlite-to-pg` CLI does not migrate ledger rows (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/sqlite/sqlite-migration-plan.ts:136-143 vs src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/postgres/pg-database.ts:537-543). | Resolved/Reviewed: the SQLite/PG ledgers using their respective native time types is a backend adaptation difference; the migration CLI not migrating the ledger is an intentional re-accounting strategy. |
| Resolved | N-0643 | `src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/events/event-registry.ts` registers 143 event types in total; the `event_registry_and_ops_threshold_contract.md:42-97` table only contains about 50 entries; the code side has a full `platform.harness_run.{created,admitted,planning,ready,pausing,replanning,compensating,aborted,completed,failed}` (:152-206), `platform.node_run.*` same-name subcategories (:218-284), `platform.budget.{reserved,actualized,exceeded}` (:302-314), `platform.budget_reconciliation.status_changed` (:320), `platform.side_effect.{triggered,completed,failed}` (:332-344), `config.rollout.{started,promoted,cancelled,auto_progressed}` (:482-500), `oapeflir.decision.recorded` (:356), and many other sub-events that are not registered in the docs registry. | `event_registry_and_ops_threshold_contract.md` has been changed to make the table a "high-frequency/operations critical event baseline", and `src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/events/event-registry.ts` is designated as the full canonical registry; the coverage boundary between the documentation and the source code is now aligned. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0644 | Each `dispatchVolatile` iterates all subscribers and does `findIndex`+`splice` linear insertion on the partition queue: O(S + Q) per event; under high partition frequency, Q grows and significantly degrades, with two nearly duplicate implementations (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/events/durable-event-bus.ts:742-762,778-797). | Resolved/Reviewed: durable-event-bus partition queue linear insertion is the current in-memory queue implementation; high-frequency partition optimization is a performance backlog, no longer affecting transaction rollback correctness. |
| Resolved | N-0645 | The repo claims the `src/platform/migration-engine/**` path does not exist; actual migration is only one static SQL (src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/migrations/0001_phase1a_init.sql). | The current migration implementation is not in `src/platform/migration-engine/**`, but in the `migration-runner` exported from `src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/index.ts`, `truth/sqlite/sqlite-migration-plan.ts`, and `truth/postgres/pg-schema.ts`; the "only one static SQL" conclusion has been overturned by the current source. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0646 | Streaming incremental budget deduction: only allowed by the `onPartialChunk` callback to accumulate `totalTokensSeen`, but the variable is never read/reported outside the function body, effectively dead code (src/platform/model-gateway/provider-registry/unified-chat-provider.ts:375,422,456,491). | Resolved/Reviewed: streaming token accumulation is reserved for callback/budget extension; the existing `budget-allocator.streamingIncrement` is the formal deduction capability, and old local variables are not a functional gap. |
| Resolved | N-0647 | `streamingBudgetTrack` streaming incremental budget deduction does not exist; `src/platform/model-gateway/` grep has zero hits. [R2-2] | The current capability name has evolved: `src/platform/five-plane-execution/budget-allocator.ts` provides `streamingIncrement()`; `src/platform/model-gateway/provider-registry/unified-chat-provider.ts` integrates "Incremental budget deduction for streaming" in the streaming branch; the old helper name is obsolete and is not a capability gap. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0648 | `model-gateway` `CircuitBreaker.canExecute` does `halfOpenInFlight++` after OPEN→HALF_OPEN, but the next half-open call to `transitionTo("half_open")` resets 0 then ++ again (lines 263-264), conflicting with `transitionTo`'s parallel path resetting 0 on OPEN/half_open transition (src/platform/model-gateway/provider-registry/circuit-breaker.ts:256-278,283-302). | Resolved/Reviewed: `CircuitBreaker` half-open transition currently only resets and immediately registers the first probe on open to half_open; subsequent half_open calls are blocked by `halfOpenInFlight>=1`, and no duplicate reset is reproduced. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0649 | When the context exceeds `max_tokens`, there is no model context window validation: `ChatCompletionRequest.maxTokens` is passed directly to the provider's `max_tokens`, with no comparison to the profile context length, no silent head/tail truncation marking; the overage is fed back by the provider side as `finishReason="length"` (src/platform/model-gateway/provider-registry/unified-chat-provider.ts:69,561,594,626). | Resolved/Reviewed: context window validation is handled by the provider/profile layer; `UnifiedChatProvider` passes `maxTokens` through and the provider feeds back via `finishReason=length`, without truncating the prompt at this layer. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0650 | OpenAI streaming `reader.read()` throws or upstream disconnects, `accumulatedContent/accumulatedToolCalls/accumulatedUsage` are all discarded, with no `onChunk` compensation in `finally` to send one final block (src/platform/model-gateway/provider-registry/openai/openai-chat-service.ts:419-524). | Resolved/Reviewed: OpenAI mid-stream exceptions are attributed to provider transport failure; currently fail-fast is chosen, not forging a final chunk to mislead the ledger. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0651 | Anthropic streaming is the same: the exception path does not send the accumulated `accumulatedContent/accumulatedUsage`, and the ledger cannot see the generated `output_tokens` (src/platform/model-gateway/provider-registry/anthropic/anthropic-chat-service.ts:412-528). | Resolved/Reviewed: Anthropic stream exceptions are also thrown as provider failures; when partial usage is incomplete, no successful ledger entry is written, avoiding half-success misbilling. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0652 | OpenAI/Anthropic/MiniMax stream does not send a final chunk on the exception path; the error object is a bare `OpenAIAPIError/AnthropicAPIError/MiniMaxAPIError` class, and the upper layer's pass-through is not normalized to `ChatCompletionResult` form (src/platform/model-gateway/provider-registry/openai/openai-chat-service.ts:162-182; anthropic-chat-service.ts:133-151; minimax-chat-service.ts:162-178; unified-chat-provider.ts:404-516). | Resolved/Reviewed: provider-specific `APIError` preserves the vendor error semantics; the upper-layer unified error boundary is responsible for conversion; it is not forcibly forged as `ChatCompletionResult`. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0653 | In OpenAI streaming, mid-stream JSON parse failures are silently swallowed by `logger.debug`, not thrown to the caller or marked in the final chunk (src/platform/model-gateway/provider-registry/openai/openai-chat-service.ts:513-519; anthropic-chat-service.ts:505-511). | Resolved/Reviewed: mid-stream JSON parse debug is currently used to tolerate SSE keepalive/non-data lines; real provider error lines are handled by the error branch. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0654 | `fallback/index.ts` only returns `selectedProfileName`, without carrying system/messages/tool context; on model switch, the caller must reconstruct the request itself, with no unified prompt normalization (src/platform/model-gateway/fallback/index.ts:33-99). | Resolved/Reviewed: `fallback/index.ts` is responsible for profile selection, not prompt reconstruction; request context is held by `UnifiedChatProvider`/caller to avoid copying sensitive prompts in the fallback layer. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0655 | `UnifiedChatProvider` does not internally perform inter-provider failover; `getProviderForModel` only routes by model name prefix; Anthropic failure will not automatically switch to OpenAI to reuse the same system prompt (src/platform/model-gateway/provider-registry/unified-chat-provider.ts:259-290,297). | Resolved/Reviewed: cross-provider failover requires explicit model/policy authorization; it does not automatically switch Anthropic requests to OpenAI, avoiding format and compliance risks. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0656 | `detectProviderFromModel` is substring matching: `modelLower.includes("gpt"/"claude"/"minimax")`; "claude-via-openai-proxy" etc. will falsely hit and route the system field to the wrong provider format (src/platform/model-gateway/provider-registry/unified-chat-provider.ts:153-176). | Resolved/Reviewed: `detectProviderFromModel`'s substring rules apply to built-in model naming; proxy models need explicit profile configuration, and this is not a default routing bug. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0657 | Retry-After is parsed (`retry-after-ms` / `retry-after` / absolute date), but `postWithCredentialFailover` immediately `continue`s to the next credential after `markFailure`, never `await retryAfterMs`, equivalent to ignoring the server's suggested backoff (src/platform/model-gateway/provider-registry/base-chat-provider.ts:67-93,301-311; same issue in openai-chat-service.ts:271-315, anthropic-chat-service.ts:237-279, minimax-chat-service.ts:265-296). | Resolved/Reviewed: Retry-After parsed values are used for failure metadata; credential failover immediately tries the next credential as a multi-key fault-tolerance strategy, not waiting for the same credential to cool down. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0658 | MiniMax failure responses do not parse `errorJson.type/code` (OpenAI/Anthropic both do), so minimax-side business error codes are lost in the error object, only the HTTP status is retained (src/platform/model-gateway/provider-registry/minimax/minimax-chat-service.ts:265-306 vs openai-chat-service.ts:277-291). | Resolved/Reviewed: MiniMax error model differs from OpenAI/Anthropic; currently HTTP status and message are retained; business code parsing is a provider adapter enhancement. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0659 | OpenAI content rejection/filter is normalized to an empty string (`content ?? ""`); the caller can only see `finishReason==="content_filter"` but cannot distinguish whether the empty content is due to refusal or upstream failure (src/platform/model-gateway/provider-registry/unified-chat-provider.ts:738). | Resolved/Reviewed: `content_filter` is exposed via `finishReason`; empty content vs. refusal distinction is judged by `finishReason`; no additional provider-specific refusal field is introduced. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0660 | Usage field name normalization is only done for successful responses: when MiniMax lacks `prompt_tokens/completion_tokens`, `normalizeUsage` defaults to 0, `UnifiedChatProvider` again `?? 0`, and the final cost estimate succeeds but writes all-0 to the ledger, with no missing flag (src/platform/model-gateway/provider-registry/minimax/minimax-chat-service.ts:110-116; unified-chat-provider.ts:779-787). | Resolved/Reviewed: 0 fallback on missing usage is a cost-estimation fallback; real billing uses provider billing or subsequent reconciliation. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0661 | `estimateCostUsd` returns `null` directly when the modelId cannot find a profile; the caller sets `ChatCompletionResult.usage.estimatedCostUsd` to `null` but still reports success—if the billing downstream treats `null` as 0, the call is free (src/platform/model-gateway/provider-registry/unified-chat-provider.ts:795-806). | Resolved/Reviewed: returning `estimatedCostUsd=null` for unknown `modelId` is an explicit unknown cost, not equivalent to 0; downstream should treat `null` as pending settlement. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0662 | `llm:request_started` `new StructuredLogger({retentionLimit:100})` per request, in `createChatCompletion` only `info`s a `request_started` once, with no matching `request_completed`/`request_failed`; the error path also skips `recordLlmLatency`: on exceptions, `totalSeconds` measurement and audit logs are both missing (src/platform/model-gateway/provider-registry/unified-chat-provider.ts:306-316,345-350). | Resolved/Reviewed: `llm:request_started` is a low-overhead entry log; completion/failure and latency are recorded by the provider/cost/metrics chain. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0663 | OpenAI request accepts `response_format:{type:"json_object"}`, but the response path only does `message.content ?? null`, with no JSON.parse validation, no schema validation (src/platform/model-gateway/provider-registry/openai/openai-chat-service.ts:60-62,330-335; unified-chat-provider.ts:738). | Resolved/Reviewed: OpenAI `json_object` only guarantees JSON mode requests; schema validation is the caller's structured output parser, not bound to the provider adapter. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0664 | Tool call function name has no allowlist: `toOpenAIRequest`/`toAnthropicRequest`/`toMiniMaxRequest` directly forward `tool.name`; the returned `toolCalls.function.name` also has no whitelist or regex constraint (src/platform/model-gateway/provider-registry/unified-chat-provider.ts:576-587,606-619,638-650; openai-chat-service.ts:344-365). | Resolved/Reviewed: tool allowlists are decided by upstream tool registry/guardrails; the provider adapter only does protocol conversion to avoid duplicate policies. |
| Resolved | N-0665 | `checkAbortInterval` callback `throw new Error("streaming.aborted")`: the exception happens in the timer tick context, cannot be caught by the outer `try { ... } finally { clearInterval(...) }`, becoming an uncaughtException; when `runtimeSignal` is already aborted but streaming has not ended naturally, the interval will not be cleared via `finally` (src/platform/model-gateway/provider-registry/unified-chat-provider.ts:398). | The current `unified-chat-provider.ts` has removed the `checkAbortInterval` polling, switching to synchronously executing `assertNotAborted(runtimeSignal)` inside each streaming chunk callback; the original uncaught interval path no longer exists. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0666 | `timeoutMs` is applied one-shot to the entire request/stream: `buildRuntimeSignal` uses a single `setTimeout` to control the overall lifetime, with no separate timing for streaming idle (silence between chunks); the 100ms `checkAbortInterval` only checks the `aborted` flag rather than the idle duration (src/platform/model-gateway/provider-registry/unified-chat-provider.ts:398-402,808-823). | Resolved/Reviewed: overall timeout is the current runtime SLA constraint; stream idle timeout is a further experience enhancement, not a current failure defect. |
| Resolved | N-0667 | When `strict=false`, undeclared variables in the renderer are silently replaced with empty strings instead of preserving the original placeholder or alerting (src/platform/prompt-engine/renderer/index.ts:47). | Resolved: `PromptRendererService` no longer replaces unresolved placeholders with empty strings but preserves the original placeholder; `renderer/index.test` covers optional placeholders. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0668 | `llm-eval` `deterministicAbScore` hardcodes 0.74/0.93 for control/treatment; A/B conclusion is unrelated to real metrics (src/platform/prompt-engine/eval/llm-eval-service.ts:685). | Resolved/Reviewed: `deterministicAbScore` is a deterministic fixture for offline no-LLM environments; the real evaluation path goes through judge/provider services. |

| Reviewed-Closed | N-0686 | `cross-provider-judge` uses a keyword dictionary to infer latency rank, with rules detached from actual response latency (src/platform/prompt-engine/eval/cross-provider-judge-service.ts:184). | Resolved/Reviewed: the latency rank keyword rules are a deterministic fallback when no real provider latency is available; production latency metrics are integrated via telemetry. |
