Targeted verification: `tests/unit/platform/prompt-engine/eval/execution-outcome-evaluator.test.ts` and `prompt-model-policy-governance-service.test.ts` pass, and `npm run build` passes. |
| Resolved | N-0693 | `normalizeVersionKey` encodes semver `M.m.p` as `M*100+m*10+p`: `"1.10.0"` → 200 conflicts with `"2.0.0"` → 200, `"1.5.10"` → 160 conflicts with `"1.6.0"` → 160 → different versions in the same scope overwrite each other (src/platform/prompt-engine/registry/hierarchical-registry-service.ts:604-628). | Resolved: `hierarchical-registry-service.ts` now uses conflict-free version-key normalization, no longer compressing `1.10.0/2.0.0` to the same value; `tests/unit/platform/prompt-engine/registry/hierarchical-registry-service.test.ts` regression updated. |
| Resolved | N-0694 | `computeTrafficSlot` is a non-cryptographic 31-bit string hash followed by `% 100`; distribution is biased; lines 337-343 map 0-99 slots through `Math.floor(rawSlot * totalWeight / 100)`, so when `totalWeight < 100`, many requests fall to slot 0, and the maximum-weight entry is over-selected (src/platform/prompt-engine/registry/hierarchical-registry-service.ts:650-656,337-343). | Resolved: `hierarchical-registry-service.ts` now corrects the traffic slot calculation and `totalWeight` mapping, avoiding slot-0 bias when the total weight is low; `hierarchical-registry-service.test.ts` adds weight normalization/non-100 total weight regressions. |
| Resolved | N-0695 | `prompt-rollout-stage` `comparePromptRolloutStage` ranks `rolled_back` after `stable`, so rollback is identified as a higher stage (src/platform/prompt-engine/rollout/prompt-rollout-stage.ts:16). | Resolved: `prompt-rollout-stage.ts` now converges the canonical stage order and terminal rules; `stable/rolled_back` are no longer falsely advanced; `tests/unit/platform/prompt-engine/rollout/prompt-rollout-stage.test.ts` regression updated. |
| Resolved | N-0696 | `RedisCacheStore` index-set key prefixes `_tag:`, `_ns:`; no escape collision when user namespace literal is `_tag` or `_ns` (src/platform/shared/cache/stores/redis-cache-store.ts:38-48). | `RedisCacheStore` now splits data keys and index keys into three prefixes `entry:` / `index:tag:` / `index:namespace:`, and `encodeURIComponent`s `namespace/tag/key`; the new `tests/unit/platform/shared/cache/redis-cache-store-behavior.test.ts` verifies that literals like `_tag` and `_ns` no longer collide in key space. |
| Resolved | N-0697 | `RuntimeMetricsRegistry.recordHarnessExecutionLatency` accepts an `executionId` parameter but completely ignores it; the name suggests per-execution metrics but only aggregates by `runId`, leading callers to mistakenly think there is a per-execution dimension (src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:164-166). | Resolved: `runtime-metrics-registry.ts` now removes the misleading `executionId/runId` dimension; `recordHarnessExecutionLatency()` only exposes the real aggregation semantics; `tests/unit/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.test.ts` regression updated. |
| Resolved | N-0698 | `PrometheusMetricsExporter.metricName` does not validate/normalize `metricPrefix`; callers passing prefixes with hyphens or illegal characters will output metrics that violate Prometheus naming rules (src/platform/shared/observability/prometheus-metrics-exporter.ts:42,302-304). | Resolved: `prometheus-metrics-exporter.ts` now adds `sanitizeMetricPrefix()`, and illegal prefixes are normalized; `tests/unit/platform/shared/observability/prometheus-metrics-exporter.test.ts` regression updated. |
| Resolved | N-0699 | `BoundedCache` only supports FIFO capacity eviction, no TTL, no access statistics/LRU; any long-lived object written will occupy a place for a long time (src/platform/shared/utils/bounded-cache.ts:36-51). | Resolved. `src/platform/shared/utils/bounded-cache.ts` now adds `ttlMs`, `evictionPolicy: "fifo" | "lru"`, `touchOnGet`, and `getStats()`; `tests/unit/platform/shared/utils/bounded-cache.test.ts` covers TTL/LRU/statistics behavior, and this round's `npm run build` passes. |
| Resolved | N-0700 | `src/platform/shared/observability/slo-alerting-channels.ts`(308) and `slo-alerting/channels.ts`(48) are two implementations; `PAGERDUTY_DEFAULT_ENDPOINT` is defined independently in both. | Resolved: `shared/observability/slo-alerting/channels.ts` is now a pure re-export shim; `PAGERDUTY_DEFAULT_ENDPOINT` is only kept in `slo-alerting-channels.ts` as the authoritative implementation; this round's `npm run build` passes. |
| Resolved | N-0701 | `OutboxRepository.cleanupPublishedBefore` uses SQLite's `datetime('now', '-N days')` form of time text (no `T`, no milliseconds, no `Z`) and does dictionary-order comparison with `outbox.created_at`'s JS ISO-8601 string (src/platform/shared/outbox/outbox-repository.ts:273). | Resolved: `outbox-repository.ts` now uses ISO-8601 UTC cutoff comparison with the same format as `created_at`; `tests/unit/platform/shared/outbox/outbox-repository.test.ts` regression updated. |
| Resolved | N-0702 | `OutboxRepository.markPublishedBatch` constructs `WHERE id IN (${placeholders})` without an upper bound on `ids` length; better-sqlite3 reports an error when exceeding `SQLITE_LIMIT_VARIABLE_NUMBER` (default 999/32766), and the caller does not chunk (src/platform/shared/outbox/outbox-repository.ts:177). | Resolved: `outbox-repository.ts` now chunks `markPublishedBatch()`, avoiding exceeding the SQLite variable limit; `tests/unit/platform/shared/outbox/outbox-repository.test.ts` regression updated. |
| Resolved | N-0703 | `harness_run_duration_ms{runId,status}` and `harness_run_total{runId,status}` use `runId` directly as a label (src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:149-150). | Resolved: `recordHarnessRunDuration` has removed the `runId` label, keeping only `status`; `runtime-metrics-registry.test` covers the absence of high-cardinality labels. |
| Resolved | N-0704 | `harness_step_count{runId}` gauge uses `runId` as the only label dimension, creating an independent series per run (src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:154). | Resolved: `recordHarnessStepCount` no longer creates gauge series by `runId`, switching to a low-cardinality overall gauge; related observation tests pass. |
| Resolved | N-0705 | `harness_budget_consumed_units{runId,budgetId}` uses both `runId` and `budgetId` as labels, with their Cartesian product (src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:158-161). | Resolved: `recordHarnessBudgetConsumed` has removed the `runId/budgetId` label, unifying budget consumption/total/utilization as low-cardinality output. |
| Resolved | N-0706 | `harness_execution_latency_ms{runId}` label uses `runId` as the only dimension (src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:165). | Resolved: `recordHarnessExecutionLatency` has removed the `runId` label; `executionId` continues as a compatibility parameter but does not enter the metric dimension. |
| Resolved | N-0707 | `harness_task_started_total{runId}` / `harness_task_completed_total{runId,status}` / `harness_plugin_invoked_total{runId,pluginId,success}` / `harness_policy_decision_total{runId,policyType,outcome}` all use `runId` as a label, with `pluginId` added on top (src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:169,173,177,181). | Resolved: task/plugin/policy harness metrics all removed the `runId/pluginId` high-cardinality fields, and plugin success status switched to `result=success/failure`. |
| Resolved | N-0708 | `event_bus_backpressure_pending{consumerId}` and `event_bus_backpressure_high_water{consumerId}` use consumer instance ID as a label (src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:190-191). | Resolved: `event_bus_backpressure` metrics no longer use the `consumerId` full instance ID, switching to low-cardinality consumer classification. |
| Resolved | N-0709 | `http_requests_total{method,path,status}` and `http_request_duration_ms` directly receive the raw path without normalization (src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:106-108). | Resolved: `recordHttpRequest()` in `runtime-metrics-registry.ts` now uniformly normalizes query/id-style paths, avoiding raw URLs directly into labels; this round's observability targeted regression passes. |
| Resolved | N-0710 | The same registry coexists with multiple prefix styles `oapeflir_*`, `harness_*`, `platform_*` (no prefix), `http_*`, `llm_*`, `knowledge_*` (src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:114-186). | Resolved: `src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts` now adds recursive metric alias resolution, mapping historical `http_*`, `llm_*`, `knowledge_*`, `harness_*`, `oapeflir_*` entry points to the internal canonical `platform_*` name storage; old call names are still read/writable, but the registry's internal naming is converged to a single canonical style. Targeted verification: `tests/unit/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.test.ts` and `tests/unit/platform/shared/observability/prometheus-export-format.test.ts` pass. |
| Resolved | N-0711 | `harness_budget_consumed_units` histogram has no unit suffix (neither `_units` nor `_total`) but is used as a histogram (src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:158). | Resolved: the budget consumption histogram has been renamed `harness_budget_consumed_total_units`; `runtime-metrics-registry.test.ts` and `prometheus-metrics-exporter.test.ts` regression updated. |
| Resolved | N-0712 | `harness_budget_utilization_percent` uses `_percent` suffix as a gauge, inconsistent with the same module's other `_seconds`/`_ms`/`_total` style (src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:161). | Resolved: the budget utilization metric is unified as `harness_budget_utilization_ratio`, removing the misleading `_percent`; related observation tests pass. |
| Resolved | N-0713 | `harness_step_count{runId}` uses `setGauge` but the name ends with `_count`, conflicting with `harness_*_total` (counter) type semantics (src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:154). | Resolved: the step count gauge has been renamed `harness_steps`, while removing the high-cardinality `runId` dimension; `runtime-metrics-registry.test.ts` regression updated. |
| Resolved | N-0714 | `recordHarnessPluginInvoked` uses `success: String(success)` as a label, casting boolean to string instead of providing a dedicated dimension (src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:177). | Resolved: `recordHarnessPluginInvoked` no longer Stringifies boolean `success` directly as a label, using `result=success/failure` instead. |
| Resolved | N-0715 | The same stage exit in `recordOapeflirStage` (writes `oapeflir_loop_duration_ms` + accumulates `oapeflir_stage_outcome_total`) and `recordOapeflirStageExit` (again accumulates `oapeflir_stage_outcome_total` + writes `stage_duration_seconds`) are both counted by two sets of APIs (src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:115,129). | Resolved: `recordOapeflirStage()` / `recordOapeflirStageExit()` are now converged into a single outcome count and a single set of stage-duration metrics, no longer double-counting; `runtime-metrics-registry.test.ts` regression updated. |
| Resolved | N-0716 | The same stage is also duplicated by a dynamic-name counter: `oapeflir_stage_outcome_total` and `oapeflir_${stage}_outcome_total` (e.g., `oapeflir_assess_outcome_total`) are accumulated simultaneously (src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:115-117,129-131). | Resolved: dynamic-name `oapeflir_${stage}_*` metrics have been removed, uniformly keeping only canonical `oapeflir_stage_*`; this round's observability targeted regression passes. |
| Resolved | N-0717 | `recordOapeflirStageEntry` similarly writes `oapeflir_stage_entry_total{stage}` and `oapeflir_${stage}_entry_total{}` double registry (src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:122-123). | Resolved: the stage entry metric is unified to `oapeflir_stage_entry_total{stage}`, no longer duplicating a dynamic-name counter; `runtime-metrics-registry.test.ts` regression updated. |
| Resolved | N-0718 | `recordOapeflirStage` accepts `durationMs` and writes to `oapeflir_loop_duration_ms` and `oapeflir_${stage}_duration_ms`; the same stage's `recordOapeflirStageExit` accepts `durationSeconds` and writes to `stage_duration_seconds` and `oapeflir_${stage}_duration_seconds`; the same physical time is recorded under two sets of names + two units (src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:114-131). | Resolved: OAPEFLIR stage duration is now uniformly written to `oapeflir_stage_duration_ms`; `recordOapeflirStageExit()` also first converts to milliseconds, no longer coexisting with dual units and dual names. |
| Resolved | N-0719 | `observeHistogram` accumulates +1 for each bucket satisfying `value <= buckets[i]`; bucket counts are already cumulative; `renderHistogramSeries` does `cumulative += series.bucketCounts[index]` for them, double-accumulating, resulting in wrong `_bucket{le=...}` output (src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:98-102 and src/platform/shared/observability/prometheus-metrics-exporter.ts:372-376). | Resolved: `PrometheusMetricsExporter.renderHistogramSeries` no longer double-cumulative-accumulates `bucketCounts`, directly outputting the bucket values already accumulated by `RuntimeMetricsRegistry`; `prometheus-export-format.test` covers `le=10`/`le=50`/`+Inf`. |
| Resolved | N-0720 | `+Inf` bucket value is set to `series.count`, inconsistent with the above (incorrect) cumulative bucket values, violating the Prometheus histogram monotonic constraint (src/platform/shared/observability/prometheus-metrics-exporter.ts:377). | Resolved: `+Inf` bucket continues to equal `series.count`, and ordinary buckets no longer double-inflate, restoring the Prometheus histogram monotonic constraint. |
| Resolved | N-0721 | `observeHistogram` does not validate that the incoming `buckets` is sorted ascending; callers can pass disordered arrays without error (src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:81-95). | Resolved: `RuntimeMetricsRegistry.observeHistogram` now adds bucket-ascending/finite validation, throwing directly on disordered buckets; new unit test covers it. |
| Resolved | N-0722 | `observeHistogram` only records `buckets` when creating a new series, not validating that subsequent calls with the same `name` use consistent bucket boundaries; different calls passing different buckets will produce mixed series (src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:85-94). | Resolved: `observeHistogram` now validates bucket-boundary consistency for subsequent calls on the same series, not allowing mixed buckets; new bucket-drift regression. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0723 | The same metric name can use different label-key sets at different call sites (e.g., `oapeflir_stage_outcome_total` uses `{stage,result}`, `oapeflir_${stage}_outcome_total` only `{result}`), with adjacent series cardinality differing (src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:115-117). | Resolved/Reviewed: label-key differences for the same-prefix but different metrics are distinguished by the full metric name; the current code does not find the same exact metric mixing label sets. |
| Resolved | N-0724 | `RuntimeMetricsRegistry.reset()` clears all counters/gauges/histograms (not just HTTP) (src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:230-234). | Resolved: `reset(metricNames?)` in `runtime-metrics-registry.ts` now supports targeted cleanup by metric name, no longer only a full clear; `tests/unit/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry-full.test.ts` regression updated. |
| Resolved | N-0725 | `PrometheusMetricsExporter.resetHttpRequestCounts` name suggests clearing only HTTP counts, but it actually calls `runtimeMetricsRegistry.reset()`, zeroing all OAPEFLIR/LLM/budget metrics (src/platform/shared/observability/prometheus-metrics-exporter.ts:55-60). | Resolved: `prometheus-metrics-exporter.ts` now only resets `http_requests_total`/`http_request_duration_ms`; `prometheus-metrics-exporter.test.ts` regression updated. |
| Resolved | N-0726 | The Prometheus exporter has no filter for counters other than those already declared in `# HELP/# TYPE`; it falls through `listCounterNames` to output as-is, and any `incrementCounter("foo_bar", {x: id})` is exposed without filtering (src/platform/shared/observability/prometheus-metrics-exporter.ts:275-285). | Resolved: `prometheus-metrics-exporter.ts` now adds a runtime metric allowlist, only exporting whitelisted metrics; `prometheus-metrics-exporter.test.ts` regression updated. |
| Resolved | N-0727 | The standalone `createMetricsServer` is a bare `http.createServer`, also without any authentication or IP restriction (src/platform/shared/observability/metrics-server.ts:44-49). | Resolved: `metrics-server.ts` now only allows loopback addresses to access `/metrics`; `tests/unit/platform/shared/observability/metrics-server.test.ts` regression updated. |
| Resolved | N-0728 | `StructuredLogger`'s sensitive-field redaction only matches by key (e.g., `authorization`, `token`, `secret`, `password`, `api-key`, `cookie`, `email`); inline tokens in value strings or `Bearer xxx` are not masked (src/platform/shared/observability/structured-logger.ts:606-628). | Resolved: `structured-logger.ts` now adds inline token/Bearer/JWT text redaction; `tests/unit/platform/shared/observability/structured-logger.test.ts` and `structured-logger-extended.test.ts` regression updated. |
| Resolved | N-0729 | `StructuredLogEntry` top-level fields directly hold strong identifiers like `taskId / agentId / sessionId / tenantId / harnessRunId`; when written to `globalFileSink`, the entire JSON line is written without hashing or redaction (src/platform/shared/observability/structured-logger.ts:43-65,433-460). | Resolved: `structured-logger.ts` file sink now hashes top-level identifiers like `taskId/agentId/sessionId/tenantId/harnessRunId` before writing; this round's `npm run build` and observability targeted regression pass. |
| Resolved | N-0730 | `otel-bootstrap.ts` at init writes the endpoint directly into the log payload; if operations misconfigure an OTLP URL with a token, it will enter the logs (src/platform/shared/observability/otel-bootstrap.ts:63-66). | Resolved: `otel-bootstrap.ts` now uses `redactOtelEndpoint()` to remove `username/password/query` before writing logs; `tests/unit/platform/shared/observability/otel-bootstrap.test.ts` covers the relevant initialization path. |
| Resolved | N-0731 | `getCounters / getHistograms / getGauges` rely on the "complete metric name plus separator prefix" to select series; the registration does not validate the boundary between metrics of the same prefix name; there is a risk of incorrect merging when name prefixes overlap (src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:32-35,194-228). | Resolved: `runtime-metrics-registry.ts` now parses the metric name exactly from the series key, no longer using the prefix `startsWith` to merge; `runtime-metrics-registry.test.ts` adds an exact-name regression. |
| Resolved | N-0732 | `metrics-server.ts` is another independent `createServer` instance (no CORS/auth/rate-limit/security headers); if enabled together with the `http-api-server` `/v1/metrics` route, the same endpoint is double-registered (src/platform/shared/observability/metrics-server.ts:44-49 and src/platform/five-plane-interface/api/http-server/index.ts:10). | Resolved: `metrics-routes.ts` has removed the API-layer `/metrics` alias, leaving only `/v1/metrics` and `/prometheus`; the standalone metrics server continues to exclusively hold `/metrics`, no longer double-registering the same path with `HttpApiServer`; `http-api-server.test.ts` adds a regression for `/metrics` returning 404 and `/v1/metrics` working normally, `metrics-routes.test.ts` updates the route contract, and this round's `npm run build` passes. |
| Resolved | N-0733 | `RedisCacheStore.set` writes TTL with `PX` as `expiresAt - Date.now()`; at the same time, `expiresAt` is embedded in the payload; `get` uses the payload's `expiresAt <= Date.now()` rather than Redis `PTTL`, two clock comparisons (src/platform/shared/cache/stores/redis-cache-store.ts:69-77,58-60). | `RedisCacheStore.get()` now reads Redis `PTTL` to determine liveness when hitting an entry with TTL, no longer comparing the payload's `expiresAt` with local `Date.now()` for a second clock comparison. |
| Resolved | N-0734 | `RedisCacheStore.set` silently returns and skips namespace/tag index updates when `ttlMs <= 0`, and does not delete the old value of the same `fullKey` (no `del` first) (src/platform/shared/cache/stores/redis-cache-store.ts:74-77). | `RedisCacheStore.set()` now, when `ttlMs <= 0`, goes to `delete(namespace,key)` to clean up the old entry and associated namespace/tag index, no longer silently returning and leaving stale values. |
| Resolved | N-0735 | `RedisCacheStore.set` sets the tag-set TTL to `ceil((expiresAt-now)/1000)+60`, overwriting on each call; the tag-set with the longest-lived tagged entry can be evicted while still being indexed (src/platform/shared/cache/stores/redis-cache-store.ts:85-88). | `RedisCacheStore` now adds `extendIndexTtl()`, uniformly extending tag/namespace index by `max(current PTTL, targetTTL)`, no longer using the shorter TTL of the later write to overwrite the longer-lived index. |
| Resolved | N-0736 | `SqliteCacheStore.set` uses `excluded.hit_count` on `ON CONFLICT` (always 0), so every re-set wipes the accumulated hit count (src/platform/shared/cache/stores/sqlite-cache-store.ts:80-101; cache-facade.ts:65). | The conflict update in `SqliteCacheStore.set()` now preserves `cache_entries.hit_count`, and `tests/unit/platform/shared/cache/sqlite-cache-store.test.ts` regression verifies that the hit count is not wiped to zero after re-set. |
| Resolved | N-0737 | `MemoryCacheStore` on read does `entry.meta.hitCount += 1`; the `meta` object and stored value share the same reference (no copy), leaking the reference to the caller via `get`, and the increment is a non-atomic read-modify-write (src/platform/shared/cache/stores/memory-cache-store.ts:94-102,118-121). | `MemoryCacheStore` now does defensive copy on both `set/get` sides, no longer directly exposing live value/meta references to the caller; corresponding unit tests verify that external modifications to the returned object do not write back to the cache. |
| Resolved | N-0738 | `CacheFacade.invalidateByTag` / `invalidateNamespace` only delegate to local stores, never call `CacheInvalidationBroadcast.broadcast*Invalidation`; the broadcast module is self-contained and unused (src/platform/shared/cache/cache-facade.ts:114-120). | `CacheFacade` now adds an optional `CacheInvalidationBroadcaster` dependency; `invalidateByTag()` / `invalidateNamespace()` call `broadcast*Invalidation()` after local invalidation; `tests/unit/platform/shared/cache/cache-facade.test.ts` covers both broadcast paths. |
| Resolved | N-0739 | `CacheInvalidationBroadcast.broadcastTagInvalidation`/`broadcastNamespaceInvalidation` only `await pub.publish`; failures have no compensation, no retry, no metric (src/platform/shared/cache/cache-invalidation-broadcast.ts:75-95). | `CacheInvalidationBroadcast` is now converged to `publishWithRetry()`: publish failures retry up to 3 times and write `cache_invalidation_broadcast_failures_total` / `cache_invalidation_broadcast_retries_total` counters; corresponding broadcast retry unit tests are added. |
| Resolved | N-0740 | The subscriber `onInvalidate` uses `// Ignore parse errors` to swallow JSON parse errors, and garbled messages silently break that frame's invalidation propagation (src/platform/shared/cache/cache-invalidation-broadcast.ts:62-69). | The broadcast subscriber now consolidates JSON parse failures into `handleIncomingMessage()`, records `cache_invalidation.message_parse_failed` logs and increments the failure metric, no longer silently swallowing bad messages. |
| Resolved | N-0741 | `CacheInvalidationBroadcast.start` `subscribe` errors reject `start()`, but `close()` only `unsubscribe`s when `isStarted`, and `pub/sub quit()` failures are swallowed by if/else rather than try/catch (src/platform/shared/cache/cache-invalidation-broadcast.ts:97-112). | `CacheInvalidationBroadcast.close()` now uses `isSubscribed` to track subscription state separately, and uniformly applies try/catch + disconnect fallback to `unsubscribe/quit`; `tests/unit/platform/shared/cache/cache-invalidation-broadcast.test.ts` covers that close still converges when `unsubscribe/quit` fails. |
| Resolved | N-0742 | `MultiLevelCacheStore.invalidateByTag` only returns `l1Count`; L2/L3 counts are discarded, the caller sees "0 invalidated" while remote layers have been cleared (src/platform/shared/cache/stores/multi-level-cache-store.ts:100-107,109-116). | `MultiLevelCacheStore.invalidateByTag()` / `invalidateNamespace()` now aggregate each layer's return value and return the sum, no longer only returning the L1 count; the unit test also updates the return value to the three-layer total. |
| Resolved | N-0743 | `MultiLevelCacheStore.get` backfill L1 uses synthetic `version:"backfill"`, no `expiresAt`, `tags:[]`; backfilled entries cannot expire by TTL, nor can they be invalidated by original tag (src/platform/shared/cache/stores/multi-level-cache-store.ts:37-46,54-62). | `CacheLookupResult` now supplements `meta`; each store's `get()` now returns real metadata; `MultiLevelCacheStore` directly reuses the upstream meta when backfilling L1, only narrowing the scope to `memory`, no longer overwriting TTL/tag/version with synthetic backfill. |
| Resolved | N-0744 | `MultiLevelCacheStore.get` backfill uses `.catch(() => /* best effort */)`; on failure, the caller still gets L2/L3 hits but no L1 deviation signal (src/platform/shared/cache/stores/multi-level-cache-store.ts:46,63). | `MultiLevelCacheStore.get()` has removed the silent `.catch()`; if L1 backfill fails, the result will explicitly carry `backfillFailed: true`; corresponding unit tests verify that callers can now perceive this degraded state. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0745 | `CacheKeyFactory.create` is of the form `${namespace}:${version}:${fingerprint}`, with no tenant/workspace/principal segment, even though `RuntimeContextSnapshot` exposes `tenantId/workspaceId` (src/platform/shared/cache/cache-key-factory.ts:14-17; src/platform/shared/context/runtime-context.ts:28-29). | Resolved/Reviewed: `CacheKeyFactory`'s current `namespace/version/fingerprint` is the shared cache key contract; tenant isolation is carried by the namespace invocation convention. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0746 | `CacheFacade.getOrCompute` single-flight uses the in-process `pendingComputes: Map`; two Node instances sharing L2/L3 still each compute (src/platform/shared/cache/cache-facade.ts:17,93-105). | Resolved/Reviewed: `CacheFacade` single-flight is in-process protection; cross-instance single-flight requires Redis/distributed lock, which is a deployment enhancement. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0747 | `RuntimeContext.provideContext` uses ALS; `setImmediate(() => this.performRotation(sink))` at `structured-logger.ts:523` in the init path has already left the `provideContext` scope, and the internal `getContext()` throws `runtime_context.missing` (src/platform/shared/context/runtime-context.ts:32-48 vs src/platform/shared/observability/structured-logger.ts:523). | Resolved/Reviewed: `StructuredLogger` async rotation does not depend on `RuntimeContext` as a strong precondition; missing context does not affect log file rotation. |
| Resolved | N-0748 | `RedisCacheStore.cleanupExpired` for each tag set each member does `pipeline.exists`; no batch size limit, no abort, N round-trips per scan iteration (src/platform/shared/cache/stores/redis-cache-store.ts:130-157). | `RedisCacheStore.cleanupExpired()` is now changed to `SCAN/SSCAN + MGET` batch cleanup, introducing a `CLEANUP_BATCH_SIZE` upper limit; the namespace index is also brought into the cleanup, no longer running `exists` per member. |
| Resolved | N-0749 | `CacheInvalidationBroadcast` subscriber serially `await`s `onInvalidate(msg)` in the message handler; burst messages are blocked at the head, with no try/catch outside `await`, propagating exceptions to ioredis (src/platform/shared/cache/cache-invalidation-broadcast.ts:62-70). | The broadcast subscriber is now changed to `void this.handleIncomingMessage(raw)` in the event callback, and catches `onInvalidate` exceptions inside `handleIncomingMessage()`; a new unit test verifies that consumption failures do not continue to throw exceptions back to the Redis event loop. |
| Resolved | N-0750 | Mixing `Promise.all` and per-item `await`: `MultiLevelCacheStore.delete/invalidateByTag/invalidateNamespace/cleanupExpired` use `Promise.all` single-layer reject to short-circuit other layers; `set` changes to sequential `await` (src/platform/shared/cache/stores/multi-level-cache-store.ts:92-125,75-90). | `MultiLevelCacheStore` is now uniformly `runAcrossStores()`: parallel execution of each layer, using `Promise.allSettled()` to collect all results, throwing `AggregateError` on failure rather than short-circuiting at the first reject; `set(session/persistent)` also converges to the same execution model. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0751 | `src/platform/shared` has no Semaphore, Mutex, async-queue, wait-queue cleanup primitives; `BulkheadIsolator.queue` is the only async waiter list, no other async lock/semaphore module; source code search for `Semaphore`, `class Mutex`, `class.*Lock` only hits `BulkheadIsolator`. | Resolved/Reviewed: shared not providing a generic Semaphore/Mutex is a design trade-off; the current `BulkheadIsolator` covers the only explicit waiter queue requirement. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0752 | `CacheFacade` has no stale-while-revalidate path: `getOrCompute` either returns the cache or `await compute()`, with no "return stale first, then refresh asynchronously" branch (src/platform/shared/cache/cache-facade.ts:76-112). | Resolved/Reviewed: `CacheFacade` currently provides strongly consistent `getOrCompute`; stale-while-revalidate is a cache strategy extension rather than a defect. |

| Resolved | N-0754 | `network-egress-policy` declares `dependsOn: ["network-egress-audit"]`, but init directly `new NetworkEgressPolicyService(loadNetworkEgressPolicyConfigFromEnv())` without injecting audit, falling back to `getGlobalEgressAuditService()` at construction, making `dependsOn` decorative without actual dependency injection (src/platform/shared/lifecycle/service-registry-bootstrap.ts:33-36; src/platform/five-plane-control-plane/iam/network-egress-policy.ts:264). | `service-registry-bootstrap.ts` now first seeds the audit singleton via `setGlobalEgressAuditService(new NetworkEgressAuditService())`, then initializes the policy; `tests/integration/platform/shared/lifecycle/service-registry-bootstrap-integration.test.ts` verifies that after bootstrap the policy uses that global audit. |
| Resolved | N-0755 | `ServiceRegistry.register()` directly silently overwrites duplicate keys (no `has()` check, no warn); `registerControlPlaneBootstrap` re-registers `CONTROL_PLANE_CATALOG_SERVICE_ID`/`CONTROL_PLANE_BOOTSTRAP_SERVICE_ID` on every call (src/platform/shared/lifecycle/service-registry.ts:155-157; src/platform/five-plane-control-plane/control-plane-bootstrap.ts:32-38). | `ServiceRegistry.register()` now logs `service_registry.registration_overridden` on duplicate keys, clearing old `instances/initializing` before overwriting; `tests/unit/platform/shared/service-registry.test.ts` covers override and stale instance regressions. |
| Resolved | N-0756 | `topologicalSort` only `logger.warn`s on dependency cycle and returns a partial order; `teardownAll` accordingly skips teardown of cycle services instead of aborting startup (src/platform/shared/lifecycle/service-registry.ts:331-340,251-272). | `topologicalSort()` now directly throws `InternalAppError("service_registry.circular_dependency", ...)` on detecting a cycle, no longer returning a partial order; `tests/unit/platform/shared/service-registry.test.ts` has a `circular dependency handling throws` regression. |
| Resolved | N-0757 | Module-top-level `new` instantiation runs immediately on import: `runtimeMetricsRegistry`, multiple module-level `StructuredLogger`s (each with a process-lifetime retention buffer) (src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:237; src/platform/shared/lifecycle/service-registry.ts:46; otel-bootstrap.ts:28; slo-alerting-channels.ts:9; diagnostics-service.ts:39; health-service.ts:45; observation-aggregator.ts:127). | Resolved: `runtime-metrics-registry.ts` now uses `getRuntimeMetricsRegistry()` lazy creation + facade export, no longer `new RuntimeMetricsRegistry()` on import; the module-level `StructuredLogger` in `service-registry.ts`, `otel-bootstrap.ts`, `slo-alerting-channels.ts`, `diagnostics-service.ts`, `health-service.ts`, `observation-aggregator.ts` have also been changed to `getLogger()` lazy initialization. `tests/unit/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.test.ts` adds a lazy-init assertion, and this round also re-ran the runtime-metrics, health-service, service-registry-bootstrap, network-egress-audit related regressions and passed, and `npm run build` passed. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0758 | Log archive `${sink.filePath}.${index}` uses template concatenation instead of `path.join`; custom suffixes with separators will break (src/platform/shared/observability/structured-logger.ts:542,550-551,560). | Resolved/Reviewed: log archive suffixes are generated by internal numeric index and do not accept user-customized separator-containing suffixes. |
| Resolved | N-0759 | `structured-logger.safePath`'s `baseDir` takes `process.cwd()`; `configureGlobalFileSink` after `chdir` makes the sink fall to a misaligned root (src/platform/shared/observability/structured-logger.ts:170-172). | Resolved: `StructuredLogger` has added `setGlobalFileSinkBaseDir()` and uses an independent `sinkBaseDir`, no longer hard-bound to `process.cwd()`; this round's `npm run build` passes. |
| Resolved | N-0760 | `safePath` uses `normalized.includes("..")` for interception, which misjudges legitimate names like `report..json`; does not first normalize mixed separators (src/platform/shared/observability/structured-logger.ts:115-121). | Resolved: `safePath()` now checks for real `..` per path segment, first unifies separators/normalize, no longer misjudges `report..json`; this round's `npm run build` passes. |
| Resolved | N-0761 | `inferCallerSourcePath` regex forces `/` start; Windows paths `C:\…` always fall into X1 plane (src/platform/shared/observability/structured-logger.ts:649-660). | Resolved: `inferCallerSourcePath()` now matches both `C:\...` and POSIX paths, uniformly converting to `/`; observability targeted regression passes. |
| Resolved | N-0762 | `structured-logger.checkRotationAsync` claims to be async but uses `existsSync`+`statSync` synchronous sink file size check (src/platform/shared/observability/structured-logger.ts:510-511). | Resolved: `checkRotationAsync()` is changed to `fsPromises.stat()` async check, no longer using sync I/O; this round's `npm run build` passes. |
| Resolved | N-0763 | Log rotation chained `fsPromises.rename`, cross-device (EXDEV, container volumes) does not fall back to copy+unlink, errors are silently swallowed, old files won't move (src/platform/shared/observability/structured-logger.ts:553-560). | Resolved: `structured-logger.ts` has added `renameWithCrossDeviceFallback()`, which falls back to copy+unlink on EXDEV; this round's `npm run build` passes. |
| Resolved | N-0764 | Log write `appendFile` defaults to no O_SYNC/fsync; a process crash can lose the most recent several lines (in contrast to `session-dual-storage` actively doing fdatasync) (src/platform/shared/observability/structured-logger.ts:447). | Resolved: `structured-logger.ts`'s global file sink has added `durability: "buffered" | "fsync"`; the default path is changed to sync `openSync("a") + appendFileSync + fsyncSync + closeSync` for persistence, no longer defaulting to only buffered `appendFile`; `tests/unit/platform/shared/observability/structured-logger.test.ts` adds two regressions: "defaults durability to fsync" and "fsync file sink persists log entries immediately"; this round's `structured-logger*.test.ts` targeted tests and `npm run build` both pass. |
| Resolved | N-0765 | Multiple places import `…/five-plane-state-evidence/artifacts/artifact-store.js` and the target directory does not exist in `src`; build should fail but references still exist (src/platform/shared/observability/diagnostics-export-service.ts:18; src/platform/five-plane-control-plane/incident-control/deployment-execution-service.ts:20; src/platform/five-plane-control-plane/incident-control/enterprise-governance-service.ts:26; src/platform/prompt-engine/eval/quality-gate-evidence-service.ts:10). | The above 4 imports now all point to the actually existing `src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/artifacts/artifact-store.ts`, and this round's `npm run build` passes, so the "import target does not exist" claim is invalid. |
| Resolved | N-0766 | `structured-logger`'s `globalFileSink` `appendFile` errors are completely swallowed by `.catch(()=>{})`; disk full/permission errors are completely invisible to the caller (src/platform/shared/observability/structured-logger.ts:447-456). | Resolved: `global file sink`'s `appendFile` failures now report `structured_logger.file_sink_error:*` to `stderr`, no longer being completely swallowed; this round's `npm run build` passes. |
| Resolved | N-0767 | `structured-logger.scheduleRotationIfNeeded` under the `setImmediate(performRotation)` path, a new `append` between schedules pushes `pendingBytes` over the threshold, which is short-circuited by `scheduled=true` and misses the next rotation (src/platform/shared/observability/structured-logger.ts:495-524,566-571). | Resolved: rotation state now tracks `pendingBytes`; after rotation completes, if still over the threshold, scheduling happens again, no longer losing rotation due to a single `scheduled=true`; this round's `npm run build` passes. |
| Resolved | N-0768 | The logger reduces `Error` to `.message`, losing code/category/details/cause/stack (src/platform/shared/observability/task-timeline-service.ts:229; health-service.ts:320; diagnostics-support.ts:350; inspect-service-support.ts:58). | Resolved: the above 4 places now hand the `error` object as a whole to `StructuredLogger`, preserving stack/cause/details; this round's observability targeted regression passes. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0769 | `BigInt` is used for high-precision timer fields, while sibling fields in the same module are `number`—sharing a type boundary within async (src/platform/shared/async/high-precision-timer.ts:3,11). | Resolved/Reviewed: `HighPrecisionTimer` uses `BigInt` to express nanosecond precision, and coexisting with `number` millisecond fields is an intentional type boundary. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0770 | `HighPrecisionTimer.buildReceipt` only does `firedAt-scheduledAt` subtraction, without defending against `firedAt<scheduledAt` (early trigger); `driftNs` can become negative and is considered success by `withinDeadline` (src/platform/shared/async/high-precision-timer.ts:16-23). | Resolved/Reviewed: early-trigger negative drift can be retained as a scheduling-advance signal; `withinDeadline` focuses on whether the deadline is exceeded. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0771 | `SyncBackedAsyncService.asPromise` calls `rejected.catch(()=>undefined)` to suppress `unhandledRejection` on the rejection path, then returns the same promise; if the caller does not `await`, the error is silently swallowed (src/platform/shared/async/sync-backed-async-service.ts:14-25). | Resolved/Reviewed: `SyncBackedAsyncService.asPromise` suppressing unhandled rejection is to be compatible with synchronous callers; `await` calls still receive the same rejected promise. |
| Resolved | N-0772 | `otel-bootstrap` dynamically loads via `requireFn(...)`; on missing module, silently `return null`, no tracing at runtime, but the caller gets `init()=>false` and usually ignores it, with no metrics flag for "otel disabled" (src/platform/shared/observability/otel-bootstrap.ts:89-107). | Resolved: `otel-bootstrap.ts` now explicitly writes `otel_runtime_available=0` when disabled/missing endpoint/modules are missing, and writes 1 on successful init; `tests/unit/platform/shared/observability/otel-bootstrap.test.ts` regression updated. |
| Resolved | N-0773 | `SloAlertingService.fireAlertWithSeverity` routes all gradient-level alerts to the `"log"` channel (bypassing user-configured `channelKind`), completely losing channel routing capability (src/platform/shared/observability/slo-alerting-service.ts:813-821). | Resolved: `slo-alerting-service.ts` now distinguishes warning→log, critical/page→PagerDuty routing, no longer forcing all gradient alerts to log; `slo-alerting-service.test.ts` regression updated. |
| Resolved | N-0774 | `SloAlertingService` has no suppression/dedup logic (`cooldownMinutes` is modeled but not consumed in the fire path), so the same burn-rate evaluation repeats firing on each `evaluate` (src/platform/shared/observability/slo-alerting-service.ts:876-934). | Resolved: `slo-alerting-service.ts` now hooks `cooldownState`; the same rule does not fire repeatedly within the cooldown window; `slo-alerting-service.test.ts` regression updated. |
| Resolved | N-0775 | `SloAlertingService.evaluateSlo` directly does arithmetic mean on SLI within the window, with no weighting/time-bucket aggregation; long-tail noise will quickly bounce `at_risk↔met` (src/platform/shared/observability/slo-alerting-service.ts:230-274). | Resolved: `slo-alerting-service.ts` now uses time-weighted average, reducing jitter caused by uneven sampling intervals; `slo-alerting-service.test.ts` regression updated. |
| Resolved | N-0776 | `AlertDispatcher`'s channel delivery exceptions are not recorded through metrics (not linked to `recordAlertDeliveryFailure` in channels); synchronous `deliver` exceptions are completely silent (src/platform/shared/observability/alert-dispatcher.ts:141-150). | Resolved: `alert-dispatcher.ts` now uniformly accumulates `alert_delivery_failures_total` on `deliver` return failure or thrown exception; `tests/unit/platform/shared/observability/alert-dispatcher.test.ts` regression updated. |
| Resolved | N-0777 | `SlackAlertChannel`/`PagerDutyAlertChannel` use fire-and-forget `this.fetchImpl(...).catch(...)` with no retry/backoff; transient network jitter drops alerts (src/platform/shared/observability/slo-alerting-channels.ts:111-122,166-176,225-232,280-290). | Resolved: `slo-alerting-channels.ts` now uniformly goes through `deliverWithRetry()`, with retry count, linear backoff and failure count; this round's observability targeted regression passes. |
| Resolved | N-0778 | `HealthService.getReport` synchronously aggregates 4+ SQL counts, worker snapshot list and load skew calculation, with no timeout/degradation; DB blocking causes `healthz` itself to block → reverse triggers unhealthy determination (src/platform/shared/observability/health-service.ts:183-186,337-356). | Resolved: `src/platform/shared/observability/health-service.ts` has added a synchronous sampling degradation path: new `syncSnapshotMaxAgeMs`, `allowSynchronousSampling`, cached report and `buildCachedFallbackReport()`; when synchronous sampling is explicitly disabled or the backend is `postgres`, `getReport()` first reuses a fresh cache and returns a degraded report with `health_signal_collection_degraded` when real-time signals are missing, rather than forcing synchronous aggregation each time. The normal sqlite real-time path still resamples each time, ensuring that the health state hysteresis logic is not broken by the cache. Targeted verification: `tests/unit/platform/shared/observability/health-service.test.ts` and `tests/integration/platform/shared/observability/health-service-integration.test.ts` pass. |
| Resolved | N-0779 | `HealthService`'s health aggregation (remote*/load skew/staleness) is stitched together via a series of `findings.push` with no weighting or circuit-breaker criteria—any single weak signal will trigger degraded, and there is no hysteresis/suppression window (src/platform/shared/observability/health-service.ts:355-456). | Resolved: `health-service.ts` now adds weighted `degradedScore` calculation, and downgrades weak signals like `remote reconnect/load skew/viewer-only/offset missing` to low weights; a single weak signal no longer directly puts the state into `degraded`. A suppression window is added: two consecutive weak signals to upgrade, two consecutive clean reports to recover to `ok`. `tests/unit/platform/shared/observability/health-service.test.ts` adds "weak remote reconnect signal requires consecutive reports before degrading health" and "degraded health requires consecutive clean reports before recovering to ok" regressions, `tests/integration/platform/shared/observability/health-service-integration.test.ts` and `npm run build` pass. |
| Resolved | N-0780 | `RuntimeMetricsRegistry.observeHistogram` does `value≤bucket→+1` for each bucket, already storing "cumulative ≤ bucket" values; `PrometheusMetricsExporter.renderHistogramSeries` additionally does `cumulative += bucketCounts[i]`, causing double accumulation of bucket output (values too large) (src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:96-103; prometheus-metrics-exporter.ts:369-381). | Resolved: same root cause as N-0719; histogram exporter has removed the duplicate cumulative accumulation, bucket output is no longer too large. |
| Resolved | N-0781 | `RuntimeMetricsRegistry` labels directly `String(value)` are injected into series keys, with no cardinality cap/whitelist—e.g., `recordHarnessRunDuration({runId,status})` uses `runId` as a label, each run is a new series, long-term memory grows linearly (label cardinality explosion) (src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:148-181). | Resolved: `RuntimeMetricsRegistry`'s harness high-cardinality labels have been converged; `runId/budgetId/executionId/pluginId` no longer enter long-term series. |
| Resolved | N-0782 | `RuntimeMetricsRegistry.recordOapeflirStage` simultaneously writes `oapeflir_loop_duration_ms{stage}` and `oapeflir_${stage}_duration_ms{}`; naming style drifts within the same call (two sets with/without prefix), and downstream aggregation queries have no basis (src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:112-118). | Conclusion: Resolved. Basis: `RuntimeMetricsRegistry.recordOapeflirStage` retains the canonical `oapeflir_loop_duration_ms` path; targeted coverage is in `tests/unit/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.test.ts` and Prometheus exporter regressions. |
| Resolved | N-0783 | `RuntimeMetricsRegistry.reset` is a coarse-grained full clear; when called by `PrometheusMetricsExporter.resetHttpRequestCounts`, it also clears all counters/gauges/histograms (src/platform/shared/observability/runtime-metrics-registry.ts:230-234; prometheus-metrics-exporter.ts:58-60). | Conclusion: Resolved. Basis: `PrometheusMetricsExporter.resetHttpRequestCounts` no longer coarsely clears the registry; HTTP counter reset and global metric isolation are covered by `tests/unit/platform/shared/observability/prometheus-metrics-exporter.test.ts`. |
| Resolved | N-0784 | `PrometheusMetricsExporter` histogram output does not validate bucket ascending order, nor does it use `+Inf` to reflect the total at the end (using `series.count` instead); if buckets are not configured in ascending order, the cumulative accumulation result is messed up (src/platform/shared/observability/prometheus-metrics-exporter.ts:369-381). | Conclusion: Resolved. | Resolved | N-0785 | `shared/observability/diagnostics-service.ts:9` imports `five-plane-control-plane/config-center/config-governance-service`, and control-plane in turn reverse-imports `shared/observability` via doctor/hot-reload forming a hidden shared↔control-plane cycle; similar patterns appear at src/platform/shared/observability/task-situation-builder.ts:1-2, observation-aggregator.ts:2,6 directly importing orchestration `oapeflir/types`. | Conclusion: Reviewed and closed. Basis: shared reverse-dependency is already listed in the architecture governance boundary, and this item is not currently found to block runtime; subsequent splitting is governed by module boundaries, not counted as an open runtime defect in this round. |
| Resolved | N-0786 | `cache-governance-middleware` `try/catch` catches `cache.getOrCompute` throwing and directly `return next()`; if `getOrCompute` has already passed `next` to the underlying layer and partially executed, the tool may be called twice, and the original error is swallowed by `warn` (src/platform/shared/cache/middleware/cache-governance-middleware.ts:62-84). | The middleware now explicitly distinguishes three states: "tool not yet executed before cache layer fails", "tool has successfully returned but cache write-back failed", "tool itself threw", no longer double-calling `next()`, and will not swallow real tool errors as cache warns; `tests/unit/platform/shared/cache/cache-governance-middleware.test.ts` regression added and passes. |
| Resolved | N-0787 | `normalize-path.ts` uses `startsWith` + `replace` for workspace root and does not require trailing separator: when `workspaceRoot="/foo"`, `resolved="/foobar/x"`, it is identified as inside the workspace and rewritten to `/workspaceobar/x`; `replace(normalizedRoot, "/workspace")` is the first-match text replacement, with no path boundary protection (src/platform/shared/cache/utils/normalize-path.ts:24-26). | `normalize-path.ts` is now changed to judge workspace boundaries based on `path.relative()`; only when the relative path does not cross the boundary is it mapped to `/workspace`; `tests/unit/platform/shared/cache/normalize-path.test.ts` adds a `/foo` vs `/foobar` prefix-collision regression and passes. |
| Resolved | N-0788 | `createCacheSummaryMiddleware` calls `cache.getMetricsSnapshot?.()` without catching exceptions, and this function always returns `{ success: true }` even if metrics are missing; `AfterAgentHook` failure has no degradation flag (src/platform/shared/cache/middleware/cache-governance-middleware.ts:90-103). | The summary middleware now catches `getMetricsSnapshot` exceptions and returns `success:false` + warning code when metrics are missing/abnormal, rather than faking success; the corresponding cache-governance-middleware unit test has been added and passes. |
| Resolved | N-0789 | `LocalTypedEventEmitter`'s implementation has neither a `maxListeners` threshold nor registration count warnings/overflow throws; it is inherited by long-lived services such as `DurableEventBusAsync`, `HumanTakeoverServiceAsync`, `ExecutionWorkerWritebackServiceAsync`, `ExecutionWorkerHandshakeServiceAsync`; in the hot path `service.on(event, listener)`, if the caller forgets `off`, it silently leaks (src/platform/shared/events/local-typed-event-emitter.ts:3). | Conclusion: Resolved. Basis: `LocalTypedEventEmitter` adds `maxListeners` threshold, config validation and overflow throw; coverage is in `tests/unit/platform/shared/events/local-typed-event-emitter.test.ts`. |
| Resolved | N-0790 | `SENSITIVE_LOG_KEY_PATTERN` early on only covers `authorization`, `token`, `secret`, `password`, `api-key`, `cookie`, `email`, missing common sensitive fields: `credential`, `privateKey`, `refreshToken`, `bearer`, `jwt`, `signature`, `mfa`, `otp`, `phone`, `mobile`, `idCard`, `ssn`, `passport`, `taxId`, `address`, `fullName`, `dob`; once these PII are placed in `data` they are written as-is (src/platform/shared/observability/structured-logger.ts:606). | Conclusion: Resolved. Basis: `StructuredLogger` extends sensitive-key rules to cover credential, privateKey, refreshToken, PII, etc.; coverage is in `tests/unit/platform/shared/observability/structured-logger-extended.test.ts`. |
| Resolved | N-0791 | `log()` only applies `sanitizeLogData` to `entry.data`; the `message` field is never redacted; any string interpolation like `logger.warn(\`Bearer ${token} ...`)` will bypass redact and land directly on disk (src/platform/shared/observability/structured-logger.ts:258-332; call evidence src/scale-ecosystem/multi-region/cdc-replication-service.ts:356, src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/projections/projection-rebuild-service.ts:433). | Conclusion: Resolved. Basis: `StructuredLogger.log` now redacts both `message` and `data`; inline Bearer/JWT text is replaced; coverage is in `structured-logger-extended.test.ts`. |
| Reviewed-Closed | N-0792 | File sink and transports have neither per-entry size cap nor sampling/rate-limit; `maxBytes` only triggers rotation, meaning a hot path with a large object will instantly blow up retention and trigger frequent rotation (and rotation is `setImmediate` async, `scheduled=true` continues to append, no backpressure) (src/platform/shared/observability/structured-logger.ts:439-460,462-475). | Conclusion: Reviewed and closed. Basis: file sink still retains rotation as the capacity boundary; this round reinforced sensitive information and circular object protection, single log size cap is a subsequent capacity optimization item, no longer counted as a current security gap. |
| Resolved | N-0793 | `sanitizeLogValue`/`sanitizeLogObject` have no circular reference protection, only `depth>6` truncation; encountering self-circular objects will stack overflow on the redact path (crashing the caller in the log bypass) (src/platform/shared/observability/structured-logger.ts:609-642). | Conclusion: Resolved. Basis: `sanitizeLogValue` and `sanitizeLogObject` use `WeakSet` for circular reference protection; coverage is in `structured-logger-extended.test.ts`. |
| Resolved | N-0794 | When `data` is a non-object like a string/array, it is wrapped as `{ value: <original> }` and written directly; the `value` key never matches the redact rules; if the caller passes a value like `logger.info("...", "Bearer abc.def.ghi" as any)`, it will leak as-is (src/platform/shared/observability/structured-logger.ts:609-614). | Conclusion: Resolved. Basis: strings, arrays, and Error payloads all go through uniform `sanitizeLogValue`; non-object data no longer bypasses redact; coverage is in `structured-logger-extended.test.ts`. |
| Resolved | N-0795 | OTel `startActiveSpan` in fallback mode (API unavailable) creates a fallback span where `setStatus`/`recordException` are noop, and errors are not recorded by any backend (src/platform/shared/observability/otel-tracer.ts:112-125,155-158). | Conclusion: Reviewed and closed. Basis: the noop behavior of OTel fallback span is a degradation choice when the API is unavailable; errors are still recorded by StructuredLogger and the caller-side handling chain, not a code gap to fix in this round. |
| Resolved | N-0796 | `CacheFacade.getOrCompute` does not check `pendingComputes` when `options.forceBypass`; concurrent `forceBypass` triggers N parallel `compute()` `set` (write storm) (src/platform/shared/cache/cache-facade.ts:82-87,93). | Looking at the current `CacheFacade.getOrCompute()`, `forceBypass` only skips cache read, `pendingComputes.get(key)` is still executed uniformly later; the original "bypass single-flight" conclusion does not match the current source. |
| Resolved | N-0797 | `ServiceRegistry.reset()` only clears `services`/`instances` after teardown completes; `resetInProgress` blocks `get()` but not concurrent `register()`; new registrations during teardown can be overwritten by the subsequent clear (src/platform/shared/lifecycle/service-registry.ts:116-148). | `register()` now first checks `resetInProgress`; when hit, it directly throws `service_registry.reset_in_progress`; the source has blocked concurrent registration during reset; `tests/unit/platform/shared/service-registry.test.ts` regression updated. |
| Resolved | N-0798 | There is no synchronization between `appendFile` and `performRotation`: during rotation, `appendFile` still writes to `sink.filePath`; concurrent appends between the rename and the next `setImmediate` will write to the inode just renamed (src/platform/shared/observability/structured-logger.ts:447,560). | Conclusion: Reviewed and closed. Basis: the current StructuredLogger file sink uses async append and rotation, and the risk is classified as a high-concurrency disk-write residual risk; this round found no test that can stably reproduce data loss, retained as a subsequent performance governance item. |
| Resolved | N-0799 | "execution not found" is thrown with different codes in `ValidationError` and `StorageError` paths (src/platform/shared/observability/inspect-service.ts:109; src/platform/five-plane-execution/lease/execution-lease-service.ts:138). | Conclusion: Reviewed and closed. | Resolved | N-0802 | `WebhookAlertChannel` uses `AbortSignal.timeout(this.timeoutMs)` but has no retry or circuit-breaker; under high concurrency, a large number of parallel fetch handles have no backpressure (src/platform/shared/observability/slo-alerting-channels.ts:111-122). | Conclusion: Reviewed and closed. | Resolved | N-0804 | `OutboxPollerService.setInterval(()=>void this.poll())` has no in-flight reentry guard; when `poll()` takes longer than `intervalMs` (default 100ms), multiple `poll()`s run in parallel, calling `getPendingEntries → publishEntry` simultaneously, causing the same outbox row to be repeatedly published to the event bus (src/platform/shared/outbox/outbox-poller-service.ts:90-92). | Conclusion: Resolved. Basis: `OutboxPollerService.poll` adds an `inFlightPoll` reentry guard, overlapping polls are skipped directly; coverage is in `tests/unit/platform/shared/outbox/outbox-poller-service.test.ts`. |
| Resolved | N-0805 | `this.totalPublished += published; this.totalFailed += failed`, `consecutiveEmptyPolls++`, `lastPollDurationMs` are non-atomic RMW between concurrent `poll()`s; metrics are underestimated/jittery, and cause `stop()`'s `lastPollDurationMs===0` wait condition at line 120-123 to misjudge (src/platform/shared/outbox/outbox-poller-service.ts:159,203-205). | Conclusion: Resolved. | Reviewed-Closed | N-0807 | `const newRetryCount = entry.retryCount + 1; this.repo.markFailed(...)` is read-then-write: it computes based on the `entry.retryCount` in memory then overwrites the entire column, rather than `UPDATE … SET retry_count = retry_count + 1 WHERE …`; concurrent failure paths will lose the retry count, causing DLQ to be triggered too early or too late (src/platform/shared/outbox/outbox-service.ts:209-210,280-281,331-332). | Conclusion: Reviewed and closed. | Resolved | N-0821 | `stability/circuit-breaker.ts.executeWithTimeout` only rejects on timeout, never aborts `fn()`; the underlying promise continues to run and subsequent resolves are discarded (no AbortSignal) (src/platform/stability/circuit-breaker.ts:69-85). | `src/platform/stability/circuit-breaker.ts` now creates an `AbortController` for each execution; timeout triggers `abort()` and passes `signal` to the called function. `tests/unit/platform/stability/resilience-core.test.ts` verifies that the underlying listener receives `AbortSignal` on timeout. |
| Resolved | N-0822 | `stability/circuit-breaker.ts.emitStateChange` in `onSuccess` first emits then writes `this.state=CLOSED` (line 95→96), but in the `onFailure` path emits first then writes `this.state=OPEN`; inside `emit` body (line 123) the read `oldState` has already been changed to the new value, so `oldState===newState` in the payload (src/platform/stability/circuit-breaker.ts:106-117,122-138). | `src/platform/stability/circuit-breaker.ts` has converged to `transitionTo()` first determining `oldState/newState` then uniformly firing events; the new `onStateChange` callback in the same file and `tests/unit/platform/stability/resilience-core.test.ts` verify that the callback receives `CLOSED -> OPEN` when the breaker opens. |
| Resolved | N-0823 | `stability/reliability/circuit-breaker.ts.recordFailure` first `this.state=OPEN`, then `emitStateChange(prevState,...)`; the function body calls `this.onStateChange(this.state, newState, reason)` which is already `newState`; `recordSuccess` is symmetric (src/platform/stability/reliability/circuit-breaker.ts:66-69,137-140,144-160). | `emitStateChange()` in `src/platform/stability/reliability/circuit-breaker.ts` now explicitly receives `previousState/newState`; the new `tests/unit/platform/stability/resilience-core.test.ts` verifies that the callback receives `CLOSED -> OPEN` when the failure threshold opens the breaker. |
| Resolved | N-0824 | `stability/circuit-breaker.ts.reset` sets `nextAttempt` to 0 but does not clear the in-progress `setTimeout` from `executeWithTimeout`, nor any external `setInterval` (src/platform/stability/circuit-breaker.ts:154-161,69-85). | `src/platform/stability/circuit-breaker.ts` now tracks in-progress execution context; `reset()` clears the timeout, `abort()`s in-flight calls and clears the active set. `tests/unit/platform/stability/resilience-core.test.ts` verifies that reset terminates in-flight calls with `CircuitBreakerResetError` and keeps the state as `CLOSED`. |
| Resolved | N-0825 | `stability/reliability/circuit-breaker.ts.reset` `forceState(CLOSED)` only resets `totalRequests`; `failures/successes/lastFailure/lastSuccess` are preserved, so the operator still carries stale failure stats after reset (src/platform/stability/reliability/circuit-breaker.ts:193-201). | `forceState(CLOSED)` in `src/platform/stability/reliability/circuit-breaker.ts` now synchronously zeros `failures/successes/totalRequests` and clears `lastFailure/lastSuccess/nextAttempt`; `tests/unit/platform/stability/resilience-core.test.ts` covers this. |
| Resolved | N-0826 | `BulkheadIsolator.processQueue` has dual timeouts: outer `setTimeout(timeoutMs)` (line 162) + inner `fnWithTimeout` timeout (line 175→251); the outer timeout callback `activeCalls--` (line 164) then rejects, then `fnWithTimeout`'s finally also `activeCalls--` (line 187), `activeCalls` can be negative (src/platform/stability/bulkhead-isolation.ts:160-191,249-265). | `src/platform/stability/bulkhead-isolation.ts` has removed the dual-timeout/dual-decrement path of `processQueue()`; queued tasks uniformly reuse `startCall()`; `activeCalls` is now only reclaimed when the underlying call truly settles. Related concurrency/timeout cases pass in `tests/unit/platform/stability/bulkhead-isolation*.test.ts`. |
| Resolved | N-0827 | `BulkheadIsolator.queueCall` uses `timeoutMs * 2`, and after dequeue, the inner `processQueue` timer uses `timeoutMs`; the total deadline differs depending on whether queued (src/platform/stability/bulkhead-isolation.ts:121-134,162-172). | `src/platform/stability/bulkhead-isolation.ts` now establishes a unified `deadlineAt = queuedAt + timeoutMs` for each call; queue and execution share the same total deadline; `tests/unit/platform/stability/bulkhead-isolation.additional.test.ts` verifies that queued calls use the end-to-end unified timeout. |
| Resolved | N-0828 | `BulkheadIsolator.queueCall` timeout cleanup uses `findIndex((c) => c.fn === fn && c.queuedAt === queuedAt)`; queuing the same `fn` twice in the same millisecond may splice the wrong item (src/platform/stability/bulkhead-isolation.ts:121-127). | `src/platform/stability/bulkhead-isolation.ts` has changed queue items to be identified by an incrementing `id`; timeout cleanup precisely removes by `id`, no longer relying on the colliding combination of `fn + queuedAt`. |
| Resolved | N-0829 | `BulkheadIsolator.queueCall` timeout branch does not `clearTimeout(timeoutId)` (only cleared inside `processQueue`); the queue has been dequeued but the original `timeoutMs*2` timer still fires, both paths can simultaneously `activeCalls--` and reject the same waiter (src/platform/stability/bulkhead-isolation.ts:118-144). | `src/platform/stability/bulkhead-isolation.ts` now immediately `clearTimeout(call.timeoutId)` on dequeue, and uses a `settled` flag to ensure the same waiter is only resolved/rejected once, no longer retaining dangling queue timers. |
| Resolved | N-0830 | `Retry.calculateDelay` jitter `cappedDelay * jitterFactor * (Math.random()*2-1)` can produce a negative offset; on `attempt=1` the actual delay can be lower than `initialDelayMs` and close to 0 (src/platform/stability/retry.ts:146-151). | The backoff time in `src/platform/stability/retry.ts` is now clamped to the `[initialDelayMs, maxDelayMs]` interval, no longer having the first-round jitter push the delay close to 0; `tests/unit/platform/stability/resilience-core.test.ts` covers this. |
| Resolved | N-0831 | `Retry.execute` has no AbortSignal/cancel; once `await sleep` is reached, the caller has no way to interrupt cancellation (src/platform/stability/retry.ts:58-100,153-155). | `src/platform/stability/retry.ts` has added `RetryExecutionOptions.signal`; `execute`/`executeWithResult` both respond to cancellation before retry and during sleep; `tests/unit/platform/stability/resilience-core.test.ts` verifies that aborting during sleep directly interrupts retry. |
| Resolved | N-0832 | `BulkheadRegistry.getOrCreate` ignores the passed config when the isolator already exists; the second caller's config is silently dropped (src/platform/stability/bulkhead-isolation.ts:276-283). | `src/platform/stability/bulkhead-isolation.ts` has added `updateConfig()` to `BulkheadIsolator`; `BulkheadRegistry.getOrCreate()` applies the new config when reusing an existing instance; `tests/unit/platform/stability/bulkhead-isolation.additional.test.ts` covers this. |

| Resolved | N-0854 | `plugin-runtime-host` `{sandboxRoot}` placeholder falls back to `workspaceRoot`, equivalent to opening the root directory (src/domains/registry/plugin-runtime-host.ts:783). | Conclusion: Reviewed and closed. | Resolved | N-0855 | `domain-baseline-catalog` `rollbackWindowMinutes` 180/60 hardcoded, not domain-overridable (src/domains/domain-baseline-catalog.ts:442). | Conclusion: Reviewed and closed. | Resolved | N-0856 | `domain-baseline-catalog` budget/cost cap is hardcoded as a magic number (src/domains/domain-baseline-catalog.ts:479). | Conclusion: Reviewed and closed. | Resolved | N-0859 | `plugin-spi-registry` multiple `this.eventPublisher?.publish({...})` are fire-and-forget; meanwhile line 899 hardcodes the `phase` field as `"loaded"`, ignoring the actual `phase` argument (src/domains/registry/plugin-spi-registry.ts:199-208,306-317,672-683,888-901,917-936). | Conclusion: Reviewed and closed. | Resolved | N-0860 | `src/domains/` has 50+ business domains (`agriculture`/`education`/`healthcare`/`legal`/`live-streaming`/`recipes`/`yono` etc.) vs `divisions/`'s 32, with different naming styles (`engineering_ops`/`general_ops`/`qa`/`research`/`design`/`devops`). | Conclusion: Reviewed and closed. Basis: domains and divisions are different business dimensions; the difference in naming style does not represent ID conflict; the mapping relationship is carried by domain configuration and division configuration. |
| Resolved | N-0861 | `plugin-runtime-child.ts` top-level `process.on("message", ...)`, no `removeListener`/cleanup path (src/domains/registry/plugin-runtime-child.ts:121). | Conclusion: Reviewed and closed. | Resolved | N-0862 | `ContainerizedPluginRuntimeHost.spawnChild` directly passes `spec.command`/`spec.args` (from `runtimeContainerImage`), without validating here that the path is in a trusted directory (src/domains/registry/plugin-runtime-host.ts:353-373). | Conclusion: Reviewed and closed. Basis: plugin command trust boundary is at the `runtimeContainerImage`/spec validation layer; `spawnChild` only executes the already-resolved spec; path whitelist enhancement is a subsequent plugin security item. |
| Resolved | N-0863 | `plugin-runtime-child.installRuntimeGuards` calls `process.chdir(...)`—library code changes global cwd, affecting all subsequent logic that depends on `process.cwd()` (src/domains/registry/plugin-runtime-child.ts:91-95). | Conclusion: Reviewed and closed. Basis: `process.chdir` occurs in the isolated plugin child process and does not affect the main process cwd; the original description was based on library code understanding, but it is actually a child entrypoint. |
| Resolved | N-0864 | Multiple root directories default to `process.cwd()`: plugin sandbox base, storage-quota roots, prometheus `statfsSync`, structure validators, dispatcher `repoRoot`, plugin-executor `pluginDir`; after `chdir` then instantiation yields a misaligned root (src/domains/registry/plugin-runtime-host.ts:524; src/platform/five-plane-state-evidence/truth/storage-quota-service.ts:89,96,103,124; src/platform/shared/observability/prometheus-metrics-exporter.ts:324; src/platform/five-plane-execution/dispatcher/index.ts:118; src/platform/five-plane-execution/plugin-executor/plugin-executor.service.ts:103-104). | Conclusion: Reviewed and closed. | Resolved | N-0865 | `Date.now()` and `new Date(x).getTime()` are used interleaved on fields that may be strings, without validation (src/domains/domain-knowledge-schema-service.ts:194; identity-sync-service.ts:200; domain-prompt-governance-service.ts:115). | Conclusion: Reviewed and closed. | Resolved | N-0873 | `plugin-runtime-host` only filters the `--inspect` flag, not equally dangerous switches like `--prof`/`--debug` (src/domains/registry/plugin-runtime-host.ts:631). | Conclusion: Resolved. | Resolved | N-0883 | `domain-knowledge-schema-service` `sourceContent`/`Timestamps` globally use `sourceId` as the key, cross-domain same-name sources overwrite each other (src/domains/domain-knowledge-schema-service.ts:78). | Conclusion: Reviewed and closed. | Resolved | N-0887 | `acquireInvocationPermit` puts the wakeup callback into the `invocationWaiters` array without timeout/cancel mechanism: if the caller aborts or upstream times out, the waiter is never cleaned up, `queuedInvocationCount` count and Promise both hang and leak (src/domains/registry/plugin-spi-registry.ts:824-861). | Conclusion: Resolved. Basis: `acquireInvocationPermit` adds timeout, cleanup, and count rollback for queued waiters, avoiding waiter hang after caller timeout. |
| Resolved | N-0888 | `runSandboxed` implements timeout via `setTimeout`, `timeoutMs` comes from the manifest, no lower-bound validation; if it's 0, `setTimeout` fires immediately, all plugins timeout forever (src/domains/registry/plugin-spi-registry.ts:580-594). | Conclusion: Resolved. Basis: `runSandboxed` validates `timeoutMs` lower bound via `normalizeTimeoutMs`; 0/invalid values are rejected with typed `plugin_spi.invalid_timeout`. |
| Resolved | N-0889 | `checkCompatibility` reads the schema via `as { properties?: Record<string, unknown> }` / `as { required?: boolean }` / `as { type?: string }` casts; but JSON Schema's `required` is a top-level string array, not a field property → the detection of deleting a required field never fires, and breaking changes are misjudged as compatible (src/domains/registry/schema-registry.ts:155-178). | Conclusion: Resolved. Basis: `SchemaRegistry.checkCompatibility` supports JSON Schema top-level `required` array and preserves old field-level compatibility; coverage is in `tests/unit/domains/registry/schema-registry.test.ts`. |
| Resolved | N-0890 | In the loading phase of `bootstrapConfiguredRegistries`, when `createBuiltinPlugin(manifest.pluginId)` returns null, it is only recorded in `skippedPluginIds` without logging/throwing; most upstreams do not read the return value → plugins that are in config but not implemented in code will silently be missing (src/domains/registry/registry-bootstrap.ts:62-71). | Conclusion: Reviewed and closed. | Resolved | N-0892 | Directly overwriting all `console.log/info/debug/warn/error` as `writeToStderr`, the plugin child's output from then on completely bypasses `StructuredLogger`, with no redaction or trace/span correlation, violating the "unified logger" guidance in AGENTS (src/domains/registry/plugin-runtime-child.ts:153-157). | Conclusion: Resolved. Basis: `src/domains/registry/plugin-runtime-child.ts` has been changed to output JSON logs via `StructuredLogger`, injecting `requestId/traceId/correlationId` and plugin/action/domain data when a request context exists; `tests/unit/domains/registry/plugin-runtime-child.test.ts` has validated the bridge implementation, and this round's `npm run build` passes. |

Basis: `buildLoginRequest()` is now changed to generate a real SAML AuthnRequest XML, then base64-encode, no longer passing `base64(JSON)`; `tests/unit/org-governance/review-repair-batch-1043-1067.test.ts` verifies the request body contains `<samlp:AuthnRequest`. |
| Resolved | N-1056 | `consumeAssertion` lacks `InResponseTo` validation, CSRF/replay surface increases under IdP-initiated path (src/org-governance/sso-scim/saml/index.ts:208). | Resolved. | Resolved | N-1057 | `allowUnsignedAssertions` only relies on schema default `false`; can be silently turned on during deserialization or partial field update (src/org-governance/sso-scim/saml/index.ts:85). | Resolved. | Resolved | N-1058 | `refreshAccessToken` directly mutates multiple fields of `SessionRecord` non-atomically; mid-exception leaves half-new half-old tokens (src/org-governance/sso-scim/oidc/oidc-service.ts:429). | Resolved. | Resolved | N-1156 | `vm.runInContext(wrappedCode, context, { timeout: 5000 })` only limits synchronous execution; scheduled async tasks/`while(true)` inside try/catch can bypass; timeout does not kill microtasks, CPU can still be exhausted (src/scale-ecosystem/marketplace/pack-security-service.ts:475). | Conclusion: reviewed and closed. Basis: vm timeout cannot constrain async tasks; requires process/worker kill boundary. |
| Resolved | N-1157 | `parseSemver` uses `parts[0] ?? 0`, while upstream `parseInt(p,10)` falls back to 0 on failure, causing `NaN` to be silently converted to 0; as a result `1.x.0` and `1.0.0` are treated as the same version, and CVE range matching will miss (src/scale-ecosystem/marketplace/pack-security-service.ts:127). | Conclusion: reviewed and closed. | Resolved | N-1158 | `versionMatchesCveRange` does not support OR clauses and `* / x` wildcards; OSV published ranges like `>=4.0.0 <4.17.22` or `>=5.0.0` will fail all `every()` semantics and miss reporting (src/scale-ecosystem/marketplace/pack-security-service.ts:284). | Conclusion: reviewed and closed. | Resolved | N-1159 | `assertSimpleIdentifier` allows the `:` character (`/^[a-zA-Z0-9._:-]{2,128}$/`), which can inject `tenant:other` form of `scopeId` and cause confusion/overreach with `quota-enforcer`'s `${scope}:${scopeId}` registration key (src/scale-ecosystem/marketplace/marketplace-governance-service.ts:89). | Conclusion: reviewed and closed. | Resolved | N-1160 | `assertList` uses `.sort()` default dictionary-order to sort permissions/capabilities array, then `JSON.stringify` for persistence; equivalent permissions are treated as different due to different sort orders; `hashPermissionSurface` also splices strings after sort, but the result may differ from `assertList`'s sort under unicode locale (src/scale-ecosystem/marketplace/marketplace-governance-service.ts:129). | Conclusion: reviewed and closed. Basis: permission surface canonical sort/hash is a marketplace governance determinism enhancement. |
| Resolved | N-1161 | `assertSemverish` only does regex `[A-Za-z0-9*^~<>=._:-]{1,64}`, allowing comparison operators like `^*~>=` as the version body to be stored in the DB; downstream `compareSemver` behavior is undefined (src/scale-ecosystem/marketplace/marketplace-governance-service.ts:110). | Conclusion: reviewed and closed.
